## The "MyPortal K12" development project will unify the management of ESA+ and Opportunity Scholarship functions for schools, service providers, and families for scholastic achievement across North Carolina.
CFI will oversee the planning, design, development, testing, implementation and on-going support of a modern web-based system for administering the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship. The system is to be fully operational for the 2026/2027 school year (applications for the programs start 2/1/26).
Application Count as of 2/28/2024 @ 2:00 PM: 28,000 renewal applications; 67,000 new participant applications

# 2025 CFI/RD Holiday Schedule

*Created: 2025-05-06T16:13:49.503Z by Paula Brownson (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2025-05-06T17:30:41.500Z by Paula Brownson (Unlicensed)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4001988649/2025+CFI+RD+Holiday+Schedule*

---

Below is the holiday schedule and time-off policy for 2025. Please note that the ones with (CFI Only) after them are not official Randstad holidays.  
Time-Off Policy
On the K12 project, we follow the CFI holiday schedule. This means that we don&rsquo;t work on the additional holidays of Good Friday, Juneteeth, or Christmas Eve Day. If you are a contract employee and wish to take off the CFI holidays, know that they will be unpaid. 
However, if you wish to work, you can do so if you get prior approval from your team leader. You must document what you worked on/created since CFI will occasionally audit those results. Please be prepared to turn in your documentation to your team leader. When you do, also copy delivery director Jerry Yancey (jerry.yancy@randstaddigital.com).
2025 Holiday Schedule
1/1/2025, New Year's Day
1/20/2025, Martin Luther King Jr Day
4/18/2025, Good Friday (CFI Only)
5/26/2025, Memorial Day
6/19/2025, Juneteenth (CFI Only)
7/4/2025, Independence Day
9/1/2025, Labor Day
11/27/2025, Thanksgiving Day
11/28/2025, Day After Thanksgiving
12/24/2025, Christmas Eve Day (CFI Only)
12/25/2025, Christmas Day
1/1/2026, New Year's Holiday

# Operational Risk Jira RAID Item Creation Instructions

*Created: 2025-09-09T17:39:43.393Z by Phillip Cartwright*
*Last Updated: 2025-09-18T19:12:10.729Z by Phillip Cartwright*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4253417529/Operational+Risk+Jira+RAID+Item+Creation+Instructions*

---

These instructions will guide you through the process of creating a RAID (Risks, Assumptions, Issues, Dependencies) item in Jira, specifically focusing on Risks.
# Steps to Create a RAID Item
Summary: Provide a brief and concise title that summarizes the risk.
- Example: Data Breach Vulnerability in New System

Description: Articulate the risk and its potential impact using an "If... then" statement.
- Example: If the new system is launched without adequate security testing, then there is a high likelihood of a data breach, potentially leading to financial losses and reputational damage.

Risk Response: If known, detail the actions or strategies planned to address the identified risk.
- Example: Implement a comprehensive security audit by an independent third party, conduct penetration testing, and establish a dedicated incident response team before system launch.

1. Risk Likelihood: Assign a likelihood rating (High, Medium, or Low) based on the following matrix:

| Rating | Description |
| --- | --- |
| High | The event is very likely to occur (e.g., >70% probability). |
| Medium | The event has a significant chance of occurring (e.g., 30-70% probability). |
| Low | The event is unlikely to occur (e.g., <30% probability). |
1. Potential Impact: Assign a potential impact rating (High, Medium, or Low) based on the following matrix:

| Rating | Description |
| --- | --- |
| High | The event would have severe consequences, significantly affecting project goals, budget, or reputation. |
| Medium | The event would have noticeable consequences, causing some disruption or moderate impact on project objectives. |
| Low | The event would have minimal consequences, with little to no disruption or impact on project objectives. |
1. RAID Type: Select "Risk" from the available options.

2. Parent: Link the new RAID item to the relevant parent issue, which in this case is K12-95 RAID items.

3. Assignee: Assign the person who will be accountable for managing this specific risk.

4. Component: Select "Operational Risk" as the component for this RAID item.

## Pain Points

| Opportunity Scholarship Pain Points |  | 11/13/23 |
| RE: Opportunity Scholarship | Rob, FYI to you about a couple things:1. It&rsquo;s confusing when the student status returns to being qualified after having been unresponsive to an award offer. We have an award date field so we 
can distinguish those qualified but not awarded, from the unresponsive, but it&rsquo;s probably worth a conversation to see if that&rsquo;s the best way to label students.1. Sometimes our system calls students &ldquo;eligible&rdquo; and sometimes &ldquo;qualified&rdquo; and the terms mean the same thing.
1. We will always need a way to &ldquo;hold on&rdquo; to a student award without canceling. With these new Tiers, managing that has been more difficult. We can hold payment on an awarded student, but sometimes we don&rsquo;t want the record to appear awarded and confuse the parent. And we have to find those students again if their Tier and lottery number come up. 
Lauren can explain the challenges better than I can. Also, the re-awards have to be done one at a time, which can be clunky. Erin can definitely attest to that. | 04/22/2024 |
## Requirement Questions from the Technical Team
| Topic / Question | Answer or Link to Answer | Date |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Confirm - SEAA wants us to facilitate/execute the lottery, including meeting any legal requirements for operating the lottery? | yes, it is our expectation that the new system be able to execute the lottery process. It may help to discuss more in person, but the gist is we need to randomly assign numbers to students who apply on or before 3/1 and then group them in order of award tier. | 2024/2/14 |
| Military rules/procedures - do these rules apply to persons who have:- a Permanent Change of Station (PCS)?
- a TDY (Temporary Duty) assignment?
- NC as their Home of Record? | the only thing we need to take into consideration for military moving forward is residency. My understanding is as long as they can prove they are relocating to NC or have papers confirming their change orders, we do not need to worry about the length of the assignment. We would reconfirm residency eligibility each year. |  |
| Do the military rules apply, or change, for NC National Guard members? | No |  |
| Is SEAA OK with a &ldquo;qualify, then apply&rdquo; approach, i.e., residency validation PRIOR to completing the application?- Under this scenario, we&rsquo;ll validate with Melissa Data first (they provide an immediate response via an API call)
- Anyone who fails the Melissa Data validation would be pushed through DMV and DoR PRIOR to finishing the application; we&rsquo;d notify them once their results are back (typically 24 hours) and then they can log in and complete the application. (We would need to &ldquo;hold&rdquo; their spot in line, presumably, for lottery purposes?)
- If they fail all three validations, what next? Appeal (manual)?
NOTE: For all eligibility/residency scenarios, a CFI Residency Specialist who knows the unique ESA+/OS rules and processes would handle interactions with the applicants - SEAA staff don&rsquo;t have to facilitate this. | No. We should have the family fully submit the application before running the validation process. Given the population we work with, we have concerns about the user experience and families coming back to finish the application. |  |
| Can non-US citizens receive scholarship funds? Do we need to capture ITIN or an A-number? | They can receive scholarship funds if they are residing in NC. We currently allow the creation of an account with the ITIN for the parent. We are assigning dummy SSNs for children who do not have one, however we would not do this for a parent. The parent needs either an SSN or ITIN to create an account. |  |
| How are you receiving IEPs for ESA+ right now? Preference is to receive electronically, correct? Can I please get a sample set of PEP documents with names/identifiers redacted? We need to see the range of how these come in and whether there is any consistency/standardization. | These are uploaded to MyPortal by the parents, this is a chronic pain point for Tiffany&rsquo;s team. I&rsquo;ll work on getting you some samples. This would cut back on a lot of manual work if we could get this information through DPI. |  |
| Do parents need to be able to initiate payments from their dashboard or is that handled via ClassWallet? (ESA+) If ClassWallet, can we just link them over to the site? Should we discuss single sign-on with ClassWallet? | I definitely think we should discuss single sign-on. It would be great if the systems were more integrated. I know we&rsquo;ll keep talking about the invoice submission and approval process. |  |
| Do you currently have application &ldquo;states,&rdquo; i.e., Awarded, funded; Awarded not funded, Pending award, etc.?- Awards occur at the HOUSEHOLD level, correct? If one kid gets an award, siblings get an award, too, assuming the parents asked for both? Can the scenario exist where a household has multiple students, say an ESA+ and OS student and another child just OS, and the ESA+ student receives an award but the other student does not? | Yes, we have Qualified (eligible), Ineligible, Incomplete, Awarded, Accepted, Declined, Paid, Refund Due.  The current issue with the statuses is you have some that should pertain just to the status of the application and others that should just pertain to the funds. There is also an element of timing associated with the statuses. Students only remain in an awarded status for as long as their award offer is on the table to be accepted by the parent. If the parent does not accept by the deadline, the student reverts back to a status of qualified. This has led to some serious query gymnastics just to get numbers on how many students we have awarded. You can&rsquo;t just pull by the status since you now have 2 types of student in the &rdquo;qualified&rdquo; status &ndash; those who are eligible but were never offered an award, and those who were in fact awarded but didn&rsquo;t accept. |  |
| What happens if a student fails ESA+ eligibility for no IEP? Do we need to allow an appeal? | No need for an appeal process. The statute is very specific about what documentation can be used to qualify for ESA+. |  |
| What&rsquo;s the &ldquo;statute of limitations&rdquo; on account history?- Do we need to display account history for renewal applicants? For how far back? Or is it a clean slate each year?
- Does ClassWallet handle rollover money and expiration of funds? If so, is our job just to get the statuses from them and display it? | I&rsquo;m checking with Kathryn on exact numbers here- We will need to give parents access to past years&rsquo; data, not sure how far back we want to go with this.
- ClassWallet just has a &ldquo;purse&rdquo; which holds the student&rsquo;s available money. Tiffany&rsquo;s team tracks all students and their expenses to determine who is eligible for rollover funds and who needs to have money pulled back at the end of each year. This is a nightmare for her team. |  |
| What interface do the schools use to certify enrollment/attendance now? Can we get screenshots of the interface? | Schools use the same MyPortal system parents use to apply to manage certification and enrollment. I will get you screenshots by the end of this week. |  |
| Confirm: ESA+ money all goes to ClassWallet, including for use to pay an out-of-district public school charge? | ESA+ disbursement depends on what school the student goes to. If the student is at a Direct Payment School, we will disburse first to the school to cover reported tuition. If the student still has funds left over after tuition is paid, the remaining balance goes to ClassWallet. If the student is at an ESA+ Reimbursement school, Out of District Public School, or Homeschool, all funds go to Wallet. |  |
| One of the validation sources we have access to is NC voter registration rolls. It&rsquo;s a nice one because like Melissa Data, it provides an immediate response. How would you (&ldquo;SEAA&rdquo;) feel about inserting that into the validation sequence behind Melissa Data? | Not something in use now; strong possibility, but how current is it? What do we actually obtain - registered only or also last time voted? Maybe put it in combination with something else? Does Melissa Data already capture this? | 2024/2/22 |
| With respect to validation, what is enough to equal &ldquo;ineligible?&rdquo; If we run applicants through Melissa Data, voter registration, DMV, and DoR, is that sufficient? Do we need to run them through one more source? Two more sources? (DPI, DHHS, Commerce/Employment Security?) | We need to operate like RDS - give applicant benefit of the doubt and run through as many validations as we can. Some may need to be in combination, i.e. voter registration + something else. Lauren to speak more with Kathryn about this. |  |
| How does ClassWallet know that a school/vendor/service provider is registered with the state/approved? They are setting up their CW account after that approval is complete, correct? | CW provides a special link to SEAA; SEAA provides this link to approved providers and sends them there for CW account creation. |  |
| What are participants using to verify their income when selected for verification? Tax transcript? Pay stubs? | In-system household worksheet, whether there&rsquo;s income, whether they filed taxes - that&rsquo;s used to determine what documents are used to verify. This needs improvement.90%-ish families filed taxes; families are asked to provide IRS tax transcript (upload a PDF) for every household member who filed a tax return. Some younger filers won&rsquo;t/don&rsquo;t have one, so they&rsquo;ve accepted 1040s. All are a manual review currently.Non-tax filers w/income: they have a unique form where they document where income came from.Rule this year - anyone indicating Tier 4 income will not be verified; verification will start with Tier 1 awardees;No income, no taxes: &ldquo;Basic necessities&rdquo; form that asks them to indicate how they sustained family on no income &ldquo;unconventional&rdquo;; SNAP, WIC benefits not considered income. This form is only provided when at least one relationship between members is Parent.  Evidence could include written statements from others re: support providedCurrently not asking how filers are filing and are manually verifying whether tax transcript counts for one person or two. We may want to obtain info about both parents living in the household at the time of account creation? |  |
| Hypothetical: if a Tier 4 household has a foster child (Tier 1) does the foster child only receive funding if the household is selected in the lottery? Or does the foster child always receive funding while other students in the house may not? | Awards will occur in order of tier. If the family is selected in the lottery, the Tier 1 child could receive award but the Tier 4 kids would have to wait. This is the only scenario like this. Foster kids may be a separate carve out and might get their own lottery number. |  |
| What do we need to report back to schools/vendors/service providers on their dashboard? | Schools: Pending payments, refunds owed back (netting out accts NOT permitted), access to payment history, outstanding tasks, including docs owed (test scores, grad lists, tuition and fees schedule, updated background check for HDMA every 3 years, etc.), certifications to be completed, withdrawals to be completed, suspension status (if suspended)NOTE: documentation at end of year is basically the &ldquo;renewal&rdquo; - if they provide, registration rolls over; if they do not, send to suspension automatically as of a date certain (9/1?) Also, need to determine how to validate completion of DNPE enrollment report. Vendors/Svc Providers: Pending payments, outstanding tasks (more to come on this - need to talk to SEAA staff more) |  |
| For ESA+ direct pays and reimbursements for tuition, is endorsement and certification required just like OS? | Good area for improvement; direct pays work the same way, and that works well. For ESA+ reimbursement schools, money should go straight to class wallet without SEAA staff having to fake the endorsement/certification process. This requires schools to be identified during registration as an ESA+ reimbursement school. (OS Schools are all direct pay.) |  |
| We need to define audit and reporting requirements for the Awards and Disbursement processes. Who is the best person to discuss that with? | Audit: Betsy, Lauren, Kathryn, LeslieReporting: Lauren, Betsy, KathrynWe&rsquo;ll set up days/times to go over to SEAA for these discussions and to see the &ldquo;toolset&rdquo; they&rsquo;re currently using. |  |
| Confirm &ndash; schools/vendors/service providers are required to renew participation annually? | Yes, although for schools &ldquo;renewal&rdquo; is really a task completion process. For vendors/service providers this will be a proactive assertion that they want to renew and may also require some task completion. (TBD) |  |
| How would you feel about having schools use CFNC ProTools for their functions &ndash; certification, dashboard, etc.? | Probably OK, needs more discussion; TBD |  |
| With respect to data, can scholarship program data be &ldquo;co-mingled&rdquo; with state grants data to take advantage of existing functionality? How is data governed today &ndash; who manages all of this? | This is a Betsy/Leslie question. Rob will send question to them, copy Lauren, Jennifer and Padma. |  |
| Envision vs. MyPortal &ndash; who uses what today? | They are the same, but have two names. Envision is an internal name, MyPortal is the user-friendly name for external people. They look different - parent view is totally different, schools/vendors look similar but different features. Admin side (&ldquo;Envision&rdquo;) also looks different.Vendors/svc providers have a completely separate portal currently; it&rsquo;s not connected to CW or to Envision. &ldquo;The Provider Portal&rdquo; - homegrown/built by SEAA. It&rsquo;s what they use for registration, which is a webform that generates a PDF. They have to manually look for new forms in this location.  For credential and provider agreement uploads. |  |
|  | Current system only allows for upload of Word or .pdf docs; would like to be able accept other image files.A mobile app might make sense, especially for certain functions. |  |
| Banking docs for schools/UNCSO disbursementIf we do move away from SO, we could cut out the Vendor Supplier Form and only collect the W9. If this were to be the case, are we ok just collecting the data that would be contained on the W9 or do we have an obligation to collect the actual form?How important is it that we contact the schools over the phone to collect the verbal confirmation? Instead of a phone call, could we do something like 2 factor authentication or run a check through Equifax? | The CFI and SEAA Finance Team agree we need to receive a completed W-9.  This document provides important information that will be used for verification purposes (see below) and also has a penalties of perjury statement that is important to protect our entities.Kevin B. told us about a new system that 529 is looking at to prevent fraud and we hope that it could do the same thing for K-12 school bank account verification. The system is called Early Warning System (EWS) which is available from any bank for a fee (of course 😉).  You input the information that we could capture off of the W-9 as well as the bank account information provided by the entity.  The system will look at the information and provide a score as to the legitimacy of the bank account/entity.  If you receive a score of 90 or better, you generally can rely on the bank account information.    We think the verification could work as follows:New School Bank Account- Input information into EWS
-  EWS provides a score of 90 or greater, system allows the bank account to be set up for ACH.
-  If score is below 90, a task is set up for a staff member to go through the manual verification process of bank account information, similar to what we do today.
Change in Bank Account Information-  School is required to enter the old bank account information.
-  If the old information is correct, then information is submitted to EWS for the new bank account information.
-  If the score comes back at 90 or higher, a confirmation email is sent to two school contacts asking them to verify in the system that they want to change the bank account information. 
-  Once they verify, the bank account information is changed.
-  If score is below 90, a task is set up for a staff member to go through the manual verification process of bank account information, similar to what we do today. |  |
| For product approvals, SEAA staff are logging into ClassWallet to conduct approvals, correct? | Correct. | 3/1/2024 |
| RE: auditing: &ldquo;Normal&rdquo; procedure is to track the last change - who, what, when. Is that sufficient? | No, a complete history is needed, including logging of previous values when changes are made. The Borrower History and RDS/NCAssist Admin model may be the best approximation of the functionality and level of detail that&rsquo;s needed. In some cases, pre-scripted codes may be needed.Concern is with preventing fraud to the extent possible (Mitchener Academy example - registering students who live multiple hours away form the physical location of the school; required IP address research to determine the school was registering these students from their location. |  |
| What do you need to fulfill requests from the State Auditor? | It could be anything as the programs are receiving significant scrutiny. The most recent request form the auditor was &ldquo;a comprehensive list of all transactions details for the last 5 years.&rdquo; There is a heavy concern that the schools receiving payment actually exist and are teaching students. |  |
| Can ClassWallet/payment services provider approve vendors and providers? | No. They occasionally assist with approving invoices for previously-approved products and services from approved vendors, but they do not have the expertise to complete the initial approval of a vendor or service provider,  or a product list from a vendor. | 18 April 2024 |
| Does the system need to verify business' state identification or just EIN? | This is TBD; the EIN is related to the W-9, which has to be physically collected. A business registered in NC has an ID assigned by the Secretary of State, but we need to determine whether there is any reason to verify it. |  |
| For transportation providers, is a non-NC driver license acceptable? | TBD; Lauren is following up on this with the team to decide how they want to do this. They may choose to tighten up their requirements given the improved verification capabilities. |  |
| When a person registers 2 service providers (2 drivers, 2 tutors, etc), is there 1 payment services (ClassWallet) account or are there 2 separate accounts? | There is 1 payment services account. |  |
| Can all drivers be handled with a single Provider Agreement (instead of an individual vs. facility agreement as done today)? | Not on the basis of the current agreements in use. We will explore whether a single agreement is possible. |  |
| Is there a legal reason to treat 2 differently than 3 or more? | See above; TBD |  |
| When there are multiple drivers, tutors, etc., does every individual have to verified/validated as appropriate for the service being offered? | Yes, every single individual must be validated, irrespective of the total number of individuals/type of agreement. |  |
| If total number of tutors, drivers, etc. is changing - especially growing - do the changes/additions have to be validated as they come on and later re-validated? | Yes. Owners/managers need to be able to add new staff and have the appropriate validations completed at the time of addition. Staff also need to be re-validated annually upon renewal, except where the validation cannot change, i.e., a tutor who has had a college degree validated isn&rsquo;t going to &ldquo;lose&rdquo; the college degree. However, a driver can lose a driver license for a variety of reasons and needs to be re-validated annually. This logic may apply to some other situations (licensing situations most likely), so we&rsquo;ll need to assess that and implement as needed to ensure providers are properly licensed. |  |
| Do all therapies require a license? | Yes. See listing from Michele and Lauren for details. |  |
| How many providers currently provide multiple services? | Not sure, and the precise number might be unobtainable due to data inconsistencies. However, Lauren estimates that there are very few, if any, and in most cases these are probably schools that overlap as a service provider through &ldquo;a la carte&rdquo; service offerings available to ESA+ families. |  |
| Renewal preferences? | Let&rsquo;s provide an annual window of June 30 to August 30 for vendor/service provider renewal. That will ensure renewals occur after the final invoice deadline for the prior school year. |  |
| Random discussion points | Like the idea of a vendor being able to submit an invoice via payment service provider and then notifying parent of need to approve; takes some load off of families. Will need to determine whether a payment service provider can accommodate that.Want to consider possibility of a family referring a service provider because they are interested in a particular product or service, but that isn&rsquo;t fully thought out. |  |
| From: Lauren Bader <lbader@ncseaa.edu>
Sent: Thursday, May 2, 2024 5:03 PM
To: David Alexander <DAlexander@ncseaa.edu>
Subject: Retention Policy Question Hi David, I feel like this normally would have been a Katie question, so please let me know if there is a better person to touch base with. I met with Rob today at CFI to talk about the new system and the issue of data retention came up. We are trying to determine at what point a user account should be created in the system for someone attempting to register as a K12 provider (we could also apply this same logic to someone attempting to apply as a school or a family). The way we are discussing it right now, a user account would not be created for the individual until after all of their registration documents are submitted and validated. Upon completion of their application and approval by SEAA, they would be able to create a login. This process should work for those who actually complete the process, but we see plenty of providers and schools start the registration process, but never finish submitting the documentation to complete registration. For people in this situation who do not complete registration, are we obligated to retain any data or documents they had already submitted to us?  If we&rsquo;re not concerned about this data, we&rsquo;re talking about purging the record after a set amount of time during which the school/provider is inactive. If we do need to retain this data, we&rsquo;ll want to rethink this approach and come up with a process to archive incomplete registrations. | From: David Alexander <DAlexander@ncseaa.edu> 
Sent: Friday, May 3, 2024 8:25 AM
To: Lauren Bader <lbader@ncseaa.edu>
Subject: Re: Retention Policy Question Hi Lauren &ndash; Here are a few of my initial thoughts. Happy to explore this further if you like. 1. If they start the registration process but don't finish, we don't have a complete record yet. I feel like our obligation to keep this would be minimal, because they never officially submitted it to us.
2. At most we would be required to keep them as long as the completed ones that made it all the way through the process. How long do you keep those?
3. Do we have any kind of pop-up or warning about how we handle these draft submissions? I feel like we could implement some simple language to warn users during the application that we purge unsubmitted submissions after 30 days (regardless of active or inactive status, even), set them to automatically delete at the 30 day mark, and never worry about it again. 
David | 6 May 2024 |

| Opportunity Scholarship Financial Review SOP |  | The purpose of this document is to set forth the procedure for determining which schools are required to submit a Financial Review, when and how the Authority will notify the schools of this requirement, the collection of relevant documents, and the coordination of efforts between SEAA&rsquo;s Financial Affairs Division and K12 Program staff. This document does not address the review process itself, which is conducted directly by Financial Affairs. |

The policies laid out in this document apply to both Direct Disbursement schools and also to the fees that parents of students enrolled in an ESA+ Reimbursement School can present for reimbursement. |

| Opportunity Scholarship Tuition and Fee Schedule SOP |  | The purpose of this document is to set forth when the Tuition and Fee Schedule is to be submitted, the procedure for schools to submit their Tuition and Fee Schedule to SEAA, the procedure for SEAA&rsquo;s review of the Tuition and Fee Schedule, and how the Authority will respond if a school fails to comply with the submission of a Tuition and Fee Schedule or if they fail to adhere to the Tuition and Fee Schedule when they certify the amount of tuition per scholarship recipient. |
| Awarding SOP |  | This document describes timelines and task lists specific to each program / each award offer group. Full-year awards are typically offered several times between April and December. This document does not address the unique steps for single term (spring-only) awarding. |
| OS Income Verification |  | The Opportunity Scholarship statute requires that program staff verify the income reported on 4% (2024-2025) of applications. This document explains how the applications are chosen for verification, how the 4% is calculated, as well as staff processes and timelines. |

## Ad hoc/on-the-fly data research example from Kathryn Marker
Here&rsquo;s a real world example of data research that&rsquo;s hard for us now. We need for this kind of thing to be easy and quick. This example is about 4 Year Olds &ndash; who can be considered for a scholarship award if they will turn 5 after 8/31 but before 4/16 of the next year. However, there&rsquo;s an &ldquo;attestation&rdquo; they have to submit showing their testing results.
 We need to know &hellip;
 
The number of 4 year old prospective Opportunity students, by Award Tier. I may need to include other info like school choice, and DOB.  
 
The attestation requirement not satisfied keeps the Opportunity application from being considered &ldquo;complete&rdquo;.
 
I want to exclude those that have any other task that keeps the application from being considered &ldquo;complete&rdquo;.
I want to exclude those that will be ineligible for any reason, including for having selected PreK as a grade level.
I want to exclude those that have withdrawn their application.
 
Of those 4 year olds, I also want to know how many have submitted the attestation correctly, how many have submitted something that hasn&rsquo;t been reviewed, and how many have been reviewed and marked resubmit, and how many haven&rsquo;t submitted anything.
 
This is just an example of the search. For us in MyPortal now, what makes this hard is the document status data being separate from the student level data.
K12 System Notifications - information from Kathryn Marker
Hi &ndash; Like with the query example I sent a while back, I hope these examples of notification needs will also help inform system design. Since notifications are attached to queries for batch sending, the two needs are closely aligned. The K12 team needs access to all data fields for queries (and for potential merge fields) so that they can independently manage all notifications quickly.
 
Lauren and others on the team will have insights that I haven&rsquo;t thought of; however, here is a start:
 
Email Notifications
- Staff need to be able to organize (including renaming) and archive (currently all notifications appear in list from all time, inconsistent naming convention can&rsquo;t be corrected)

- Include all data points as potential merge fields (currently significant limitations)

- Must have formatting options: bold, bullet points, color, spacing (currently very little formatting options, notification spacing / font size is difficult to control, notification appearance often unprofessional)

- Able to copy / paste query and notification (keep the first version, edit for the second)

 
- Able to be sent automatically, one time, immediately after action in the system (example: parent submits application, email is generated and released to confirm submission & eligibility status)

Currently: notifications are generated automatically, but overnight; and must be released manually the next morning
 
- Able to be sent automatically, on a regular cadence as determined by the K12 team, until a requirement is satisfied (example: incomplete tasks)

Currently: these notifications have to be set up by the vendor
 
- One notification should function for batch sending and on-demand sending (currently need two identical notifications, one for each purpose)

- Need a solution for appearance of notifications in student record (currently a canceled batch notification appears in the student record &ndash; as &ldquo;canceled&rdquo;)

From Katie G:
&ldquo;&hellip;a couple of requirements for consideration from Walter Marshall (SEAA IT Ops team manager): &ldquo;Thinking about the K12 system requirements, I know there was interest in building in the functionality for e-signatures (to replace DocuSign) in to this new system.  Also, 2FA for all users would probably be a great security protection to have be included in upfront!&rdquo;
 
## Information from Kathryn re: recent changes related to ESA Products
Hi &ndash; Quick follow up to confirm the discussion from this past Monday:
 
1. We collect a business license only for transportation providers. No one else has to submit a business license (now that we are discontinuing the curricula providers).

2. We are adding a question (yes/no; do not collect names) that asks: Do you have children who are recipients of the ESA+ scholarship? No logic needed, just info that we can query on.

3. Therapists or Tutors can be in a &ldquo;facility&rdquo;. Example: a speech therapy clinic or a Sylvan tutoring center; either could sign a facility agreement.

4. FACILITY AGREEMENT: We do not verify individual credentials for the providers. The head of the clinic or tutoring center will warrant the credentials of the staff by signing the facility agreement.

  
A copy of that agreement is attached. Also attached is the policy [and it is provided above in this Confluence page in the Provider Agreements section]. It may help to see pages 6-7 where program staff and legal counsel together documented the process.
Changes to Renewal Household Requirements
ESA+ and OS recipients (dual)
ESA+ only must meet the minimal spend limit to have funding for next year. Must spend $1000in the AY to qualify (no change to this rule)
OS, they have to spend money, but not a required amount. They have to spend something, rule =OS only Must spend $0.01 to be eligible for renewalDual recipients can now spend $1000 out of EITHER program and will be eligible for ESA+
Renewal.

Pre-project Operational Analysis (and associated documents) completed by Shannon Byers

K12 Provider Info from Kathryn Marker
At our meeting yesterday, I said I would circulate this document. The content is final (with a few caveats, below) but the formatting is still being finalized. We have spent the last 12-18 months with our legal counsel reviewing and formalizing important policies. I should give you all of those policies. Happy reading indeed! Seriously, let me know. Attached is our working list of policies.
 
The last page of the SOP is the appendix with the types of credentials and websites for checking them.
 
Caveats re providers: we&rsquo;ve agreed not to resolve the two big questions below at this time, mainly because we do not have the technology to support either.
1. &ldquo;what if&rdquo; scenarios: e.g. how do we monitor / decide to remove a problematic provider?

2. A renewal or updating process; we need a routine way to verify that a provider is still in business, and in some cases re-verify their credentials.

 
And a note re terminology: we&rsquo;ve tentatively decided to call this process an enrollment process, similar to DHHS and medical providers in Medicaid. This label is an example of terminology I&rsquo;d like to review and approve (with Holly&rsquo;s help) since whatever we call it in the system becomes its name. We want to be thoughtful about our labels.
 
THANKS
Kathryn

ESA+ IEP Validation/Verification Information from Kathryn Marker
Hi Rob &ndash; I wanted to let you know that I have a meeting with the head of DPI&rsquo;s Exceptional Children division next week. She is willing to do a pilot project this year where we obtain IEP data from them to document a disability for our new students. We&rsquo;re reviewing documents for several thousand students this year, but maybe &hellip; by next year we&rsquo;ll have a protocol to avoid parents submitting and us reviewing documentation. Next year we won&rsquo;t have the new system in place, but I think we could still make great strides if DPI is willing to work with us.
 
THANKS!
Kathryn

Subject: New Web Content and Mobile App Accessibility Rule for State and Local Governments Hi Rob,Our legal division just sent me some info about a new rule on the accessibility of web content and mobile apps provided by State and local governments. As SEAA is an agency of the State of NC, we will need to make sure that we take this new rule into account when designing the new K12 system as well as the new K12 website. I believe that we have discussed the WCAG version used by SEAA in the past and I have had it on my to-do list to get this info over to you for a while. This new rule spells this out for us: barring approved exceptions, we need to meet WCAG 2.1, Level AA in our web systems. Please let me know if you have any questions. Thanks,Katie  |  |

Some tie in to the SEAA backend to assign tasks, workflow basket. Communication between Service Provider and SEAA Admin = workbasket letting SEAA know there are tasks to work on. If renewal - upgraded information transfer to Class Wallet using APIs. Once the application is done the parents can only see (ESA) payments and such. Brandon believes we can start this now (and deliver early - per Kent).

Renewals = Service Provider - every year, and they have to walk through the entire process. We want to update the renewal process and automate as much as possible. looking at 3-4 diff state agencies, calls into DPI for teaching certificates, Clearinghouse, many different calls for validation.

Start standing up the SEAA Admin site, one module at a time, this will allow us for booking time as we complete and schedule the next module.
Wireframes - Brandon asks if we have any available. Marty says we are hiring or bringing on a couple people to speed this up. NCAssist Loan Admin can be a Springboard (Balsamiq) into SEAA Admin, using code when we can (managing funds, Student inquiry, etc.) A new developer experienced in wireframes should be able to jump right in.
From SEAA, we need to know what the vendor login process will be? Start from new or use pieces of what we already have. Marty says that we put a proposal together to suggest what we want them to choose.
Brandon - is it too early to start talking about Azure? The what and the how?
This is what Sumith is thinking:

Future Phases - each module after team agrees as how to move forward.
Brandon - next steps are the granular details for each of the phases above. How is this stuff going to interact, where to store, how to handle sync with Class Wallet, and a couple other things I missed typing. Is this 10 weeks or 50 weeks. Sumith wants to start wireframes and some data models, to see what we will have to expect.

What Azure technology stack should we go with? Brandon says that is way to early right now. We need to lay out requirements, diagram, application approval, getting the initial architecture on paper. How many people to build this? Kent and Sumith are good with that method.

# External Enrollment and Admin API Design

*Created: 2024-10-03T21:55:56.202Z by John Grant (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2024-10-14T18:52:02.468Z by John Grant (Unlicensed)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3500900373/External+Enrollment+and+Admin+API+Design*

---

## Updated Swagger Documentation with Additional Context
Key Updates and Inferences
- RESTful and HATEOAS Design: The URL paths have been revised to follow RESTful principles, using nouns to represent resources and HTTP verbs for operations. For example:
/v1/enrollment/programs/{token} for retrieving an enrollment program.

- /v1/enrollment/programs/{token}/sessions for creating a new enrollment session.

- /v1/enrollment/sessions/{sessionId}/prompts for submitting prompt data.

HTTP Status Codes: The responses now use more appropriate HTTP status codes, aligning with RESTful conventions and best practices:
- 202 Accepted for asynchronous operations like starting a new enrollment session and updating a program.

- 201 Created for successfully creating a new resource, such as an enrollment session.

- Location header is included in 201 Created and 202 Accepted responses to provide the URL of the newly created resource.

Root Object for Responses: A root object with a "data" property is included for all responses to provide a consistent structure. For example:
Schema Definitions: The schema definitions have been refined based on the example data and function signatures, but some placeholders remain.
Authentication: The stakeholder conversation reveals two authentication methods: OpenID Connect (OIDC) for public-facing users and Okta integration for admins. This should be implemented and documented accordingly.
Versioning: The API paths include the version number ("v1").
Prompt Submission and Next Prompt: The SubmitPromptData endpoint now returns the next prompt in the enrollment process, allowing for a dynamic flow.
Zero-Trust & Secure By Design: The Jira ticket emphasizes security considerations. While token-based access control is mentioned, the specific protocols require further definition and implementation.
Additional Notes
- The API documentation is still a work in progress and needs further refinement based on implementation details and project requirements.

- The schema definitions for request and response objects should be completed.

- The conditional logic for prompts, hinted at in the commented-out code, should be implemented and documented.

- The keypath mentioned in the sources may offer a more compact way to represent data relationships. This requires further investigation and design decisions.

This revised Swagger documentation incorporates the additional context and feedback, moving closer to a well-defined and RESTful API design.



# CRM Requirements (Kathryn)

## Organize stakeholder data (schools & parents & students)
Manage relationships 
- Engage more effectively with parents (particularly ESA+, but also Opportunity)

- Engage more effectively with schools 

Track interactions 
- Phone, automated email, unique email or ticketing system

- Parent information about schools and school information about parents

- Listen with empathy

Solve stakeholder problems quickly, or even proactively
- Help scheduling 1:1 conferences with parents

Gain insights through analytics
- What problems do our stakeholders have

- Where are they getting their information

- Where in our processes are they getting stuck

- What type of customer service are we good or bad at

## The importance of integrating with phone system at both SEAA and at CFI
Inbound calls
- Reduce identity checks and time staff spend making sure they have the right record

- Facilitate connections with stakeholders (better service)

- Staff focus on the caller and the caller&rsquo;s problem; not administrative tasks that can be automated 

Outbound calls
Eliminate using spreadsheets to track outbound calls
Examples of outbound call campaigns: 
- Groups of unique student populations

- Groups of unique school populations

- Parents of enrolled students at schools with compliance or other issues

# DRAFT Hub and Spoke Model for K12

*Created: 2025-06-09T13:08:12.020Z by JIves*
*Last Updated: 2025-06-09T13:09:04.527Z by JIves*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4063428629/DRAFT+Hub+and+Spoke+Model+for+K12*

---

Identity Management Sources
- K12 Entra ID: Will be located in Azure Government cloud along with all the Azure resources for the K12 program.
This is the primary subject of this document.

- The source of truth for all granted permissions.

K12 B2C Entra ID: This will be located in the standard Azure cloud. This is required because Azure Government Cloud doesn't allow B2C connections.
- All
Provider Dashboard Users, 

- School Dashboard Users, 

- Parents and 

- Proxy Parents 

will gain access via the K12 B2C Entra ID tenant.
- CFI Entra ID: Is already located in the standard Azure Cloud alongside K12 development resources.

- SEAA Okta: The identity provider used by SEAA employees.

 
Trust Relationships and Authentication Flow
For a cohesive identity management framework and enable seamless authentication across the K12 program, the K12 Entra ID must establish trust relationships with the following identity providers:
1. K12 B2C Entra ID

2. CFI Entra ID

3. SEAA Okta

These trust relationships are foundational for:
- User Referencing: Enabling K12 Entra ID to recognize and reference users authenticated by these external providers.

- Login Enablement: Ensuring secure and efficient login capabilities for all users within the K12 program ecosystem.

 
 
| %% https://mermaid.livegraph TD    %% Define subgraphs for system boundaries    subgraph Azure_Gov_Cloud[Azure Government Cloud]        K12_Entra[Entra ID<br>K12 Primary<br>Identity Provider]    end    subgraph Azure_Non_Gov_Cloud[Azure Non-Govt. Cloud]        K12_B2C[Entra ID<br>K12 B2C]        CFI_Entra[Entra ID<br>CFI]        K12_B2C_Users[Provider Administrators]        K12_B2C_Users1[School Administrators]        K12_B2C_Users2[Primary & Proxy Parents]        K12_B2C_Users3[CFI Employees]        K12_B2C --> K12_B2C_Users        K12_B2C --> K12_B2C_Users1        K12_B2C --> K12_B2C_Users2        CFI_Entra --> K12_B2C_Users3    end    subgraph Non_Azure[Non-Azure]        SEAA_Okta[Okta<br>SEAA's Okta Provider]        K12_B2C_Users4[CFI Employees]        SEAA_Okta --> K12_B2C_Users4    end    %% Trust relationships    K12_Entra -->|Trust| K12_B2C    K12_Entra -->|Trust| CFI_Entra    K12_Entra -->|Trust| SEAA_Okta    %% Styling for clarity    classDef entra fill:#0078D4,stroke:#005A9E,color:#FFF    classDef okta fill:#00297A,stroke:#001F5C,color:#FFF    class K12_Entra,CFI_Entra,K12_B2C entra    class SEAA_Okta okta    class K12_B2C_Users users |
 
The Hub and Spoke Architecture
 
What:
 
In this "Hub and Spoke" model Azure Entra ID is the central Identity Hub, and APIM and a Unified API Gateway Middleware method, built with C# Azure Functions, is the single, intelligent entry point to all data resources (the spokes).
 
- Azure Entra ID will serve as the central Identity Hub, where all entities and their relationships are defined using a scalable model.

- APIM and a single unified security API middleware method, referenced by all access to Azure Functions, will act as the gateway to all data. This gateway will make authorization decisions based on the identity hub and then securely broker access to the data spokes. 

- Azure SQL and Azure Data Lake Storage (ADLS) Gen2 will contain the data (spokes).

 
Why:
This approach focuses: 
- on minimal support costs by leveraging automation and delegating administration

- scalability and maintainability of an attribute-based system

- a strong defense-in-depth posture at the data layer

- auditable permissions model

 
Guiding Architectural Principles
1. Centralized Identity, Decentralized Administration: Entra ID is the single source of truth for "who is who," but daily user management is delegated to the institutions themselves.

2. Attribute-Driven Authorization: Permissions are determined by the attributes and relationships of an identity, not by managing thousands of individual roles or access lists.

3. Unified API Gateway: All applications access data through a single, secure API middleware method. 
centralizes security logic, making it consistent, maintainable, and easy to monitor. It contains all business and authorization logic, making it easy to update, test, and audit. 

4. No application ever accesses data directly.

Defense-in-Depth: While the API is the primary gatekeeper, the data stores will have their own security layer (RLS, RBAC) to prevent unauthorized access in all circumstances.
 
Step 1: Configure Azure Entra ID (the Identity Hub)
 
1. Object Representation
- People (Students, Parents, Schools Admins, Vendors Admins, etc.): All will be represented as User objects.
Students: Student user objects will be created with accountEnabled: false and no password. They are "non-loginable" "Identity Records" that serve as anchors for permissions. This model is free and does not consume licenses.

Institutions (Schools and Providers): Each will be an Entra ID Security Group (e.g., K12-School-LincolnHigh, K12-Vendor-Mathnasium). The staff who work at these institutions will be members of their respective groups.
 
2. Custom Security Attributes
This is the scalable and manageable way to define relationships. In Entra ID, create an Attribute Set called studentAccessControl and define the following attributes (These will be assigned to each student's user object):
- Attribute Set: studentAccessControl

- Attributes to be defined: 
parentReadWrite (String, Multi-value): Stores the Object ID(s) of the primary parent(s).

- proxyReadOnly (String, Multi-value): Stores the Object ID(s) of the proxy parent(s).

- enrolledSchoolId (String, Single-value): Stores the Object ID of the School's Security Group.

- enrolledVendorId (String, Single-value): Stores the Object ID of the Tutor's Security Group.

 
 
3. Delegated Administration: Administrative Units (AUs) (Key to Minimizing Support)
Use Administrative Units (AUs) to allow schools to manage their own staff.
This is the key to minimizing central IT workload.
1. For each school, create an Administrative Unit (e.g., "AU - Northwood High").

2. Add the school's staff User objects and the school's Security Group to this AU. [Place all of that school's staff User objects and the school's Security Group (K12-School-LincolnHigh) into this AU.]

3. Delegate Roles: 

- Assign the school's designated administrator scoped to the school's AU. This allows them to manage users and group memberships for their own school only.
This will allow school admins to reset passwords and manage group memberships for their school only, without needing to file a ticket with central IT.

 
4. Application Roles
In the App Registration for the API, we will define a small number of broad, capability-based roles
- K12.Admin

- School.Admin

- Vendor.Admin

- PrimaryParent.User

- ProxyParent.User

 
Groups
- For each school, an admin group will be created for it.

- For each vendor, an admin group will be created for it.

 
Summary:
- K12.Admin Role give access to be a K12 administrator.

- Vendor and School roles only give a short cut for guarding against access to all related resources.
A vendor gains access to their 

 
* unanswered question: what if a user is an admin for more than 1 vendor?
* unanswered question: what if a user is an admin for more than 1 school?
 
Parents and ProxyParents gain access to their student's resources by:
1. Being in the role of PrimaryParent or ProxyParent

2. Being granted access via custom security attributes.

 
Step 2: Configure API Management (APIM) to require JWT token access
 
Use Authorization to control what endpoints a request is allowed to call.
For example lock down access to K12.Admin endpoints to K12.Admins.
 
Step 3: Build the Unified Security Gateway (C# Azure Function middleware)
 
This API middleware is the gateway. It centralizes authorization logic.
 
1. Authorization Logic Flow
When a request is received (e.g., GET /api/students/{studentId}/registration_program):
1. Validates the user's JWT token, extracting their Object ID and their broad application role (e.g., Parent.User).

2. Makes a single, efficient Microsoft Graph API call to retrieve the target student's User object, requesting the customSecurityAttributes in the query.

3. A clear, maintainable C# if/else if/else block determines access: 

The middleware business method: 
- IF the caller's role is K12.Global, grant access.

- ELSE IF the caller's role is Parent.User, check if their Object ID is in the student's parentReadWrite or proxyReadOnly attribute.

- ELSE IF the caller's role is School.Admin, get the enrolledSchoolId attribute from the student. Then, make a Graph API call to check if the caller is a member of that school's group.

- ELSE IF... (similar logic for Vendors).

- ELSE, deny access.

 
2. Secure Data Access Methods
Once authorized, the function fetches data securely:
- Azure SQL Data: The function uses the On-Behalf-Of (OBO) flow to exchange the user's token for one scoped to Azure SQL. It then connects to the database as that user, enabling the RLS (Row-level security) defense-in-depth layer.

- PDF Files in ADLS Gen2: The function generates a short-lived (~5 minute), permission-scoped Shared Access Signature (SAS) token for the specific blob requested. It returns a URL containing this SAS token to the client, which downloads the file directly from storage. This is highly secure and performant.

 
Step 4: Implement Defense-in-Depth in Data Stores
This final layer provides failsafe security directly at the data source (protected even from potential application-level bugs).
 
1. Azure SQL: Simplified Row-Level Security (RLS)
- Note: RLS will not contain complex business logic. It will act as a simple backstop.

- A trusted automated process will sync Entra ID attributes to populate an Azure SQL table UserStudentAccessMap. This map table can be updated by a trusted, automated process that syncs from the Entra ID attributes on a timer trigger. UserStudentAccessMap will be locked down to only allowing a Managed Identity to insert/update/delete. Entra ID is the source of truth, the SQL table is only a projection.

- When the API connects to SQL (using the OBO flow), it first sets the caller's identity in the session context: EXEC sp_set_session_context @key=N'UserObjectId', @value=@user_object_id.

- A simple RLS predicate function will then check against the pre-populated UserStudentAccessMap table to ensure a valid relationship exists for that user and student.

 
2. ADLS Gen2 with Hierarchical Namespaces: Simplified RBAC and SAS
- Access is handled by assigning broad RBAC roles (e.g., Storage Blob Data Reader) to the Security Groups (e.g., K12-School-LincolnHigh) at a specific folder level. 

- Of course. Based on the detailed requirements and the relative strengths and weaknesses of different Azure services, here is the best-practice architectural approach. 

- Additionally short-lived SAS tokens generated by the API. This avoids managing millions of individual Access Control List (ACL) entries.

 
| Planned structure for easy permission management ADLS Gen2 with HNS: /{application}/{legal-entity}/{userid}/{documentType} then all documents will be stored inside documentType directories. Roughly:application: maps to the course grained roleslegal-entity: maps to the groups created for schools & vendors, etc.userid: refers to the custom security attributes Semi-fictional Examples: Enrollment:/enrollment/lincoln-high/student123/apartmentleasingagreement When a parent uploads a document:Parents and parentProxy are granted access to their own uploaded document at the {userid} level.The school's admins have access to the document(s) at the legal-entity level.K12.Admin SEAA admins have access to the document(s) at either the application level. Provider Dashboard: /providerdashboard/math-tutor123/employee123/verificationdocument123 When a provider's employee uploads a document:Employee could access their own document(s) at the {userid} level.The provider's admins have access to the document(s) at the {legal-entity} level.K12.Admin SEAA admins have access to the document(s) at either the application level. |

*Last Updated: 2025-08-20T18:19:50.972Z by JIves*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4213407777/Revised+K12+Project+Charter*

---

I. Table of Contents

XI. Sign-Off
II. Project Background 
The North Carolina State Education Assistance Authority (SEAA) currently uses commercially developed software called Envision/MyPortal to administer these programs:
- The North Carolina Personal Education Student Accounts (ESA+) for Children with Disabilities Program, established by G.S. &sect; 115C-590 et. seq., provides scholarships for parents to pay educational expenses, including tuition and fees, for children with disabilities who attend school in an eligible nonpublic setting.

- The Opportunity Scholarship Program, established by G.S. &sect; 115C-562.1 et seq., provides scholarships for parents to pay tuition and required fees for their children to attend school in an eligible nonpublic setting. Student eligibility for this program was significantly expanded by the NC State Legislature beginning for the 2024/2025 school year.

The Envision software is customizable, but SEAA has had to rely on the vendor&rsquo;s availability to make changes. This has resulted in SEAA having to implement manual processes in some cases, to meet new and changing requirements from the State Legislature. In addition, SEAA has identified numerous &ldquo;pain points&rdquo; where the system isn&rsquo;t fully meeting their needs from a functionality and/or efficiency perspective.
SEAA has received an appropriation from the State of NC to develop an in-house system to support these programs moving forward. SEAA is engaging College Foundation, Inc (CFI) to develop, implement and maintain the system on SEAA&rsquo;s behalf. The target date for a fully implemented system is 6/1/2026.
III. Project Overview 
CFI will oversee the planning, design, development, testing, implementation and on-going support of a modern web-based system for administering the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship. The system is to be fully operational for the 2026/2027 school year (applications for the programs start 2/1/2026).
In Scope:
1. System functionality required to apply, renew, disburse and track funds for the Opportunity Scholarship and ESA+ Programs. (See Functional Requirements #1 Section IV)

2. School management functions to support both Programs. (See Functional Requirements #2 Section IV)

3. SEAA administrative functionality/abilities needed to administer both Programs. (See Functional Requirements #3 Section IV)

4. Customer relationship management functionality is needed to manage interactions with customers. (See Functional Requirements #4 Section IV)

5. Technical architecture and infrastructure are needed to support the new system.

6. Conversion of data from Envision/MyPortal to the new system.

7. Integration with SEAA&rsquo;s funds repository vendor.

8. Communications, documentation and planning needed for system implementation and on-going maintenance. (See Non-Functional Requirements #4 Section IV)

9. An exploration of what it would take to integrate the new system with SEAA&rsquo;s phone system. (See Non-Functional Requirements #5 Section IV)

10. Adherence to best practices for web-based system development (See Non-Functional Requirements #3 Section IV)

11. An exploration of whether there is a need to automate or integrate anything related to SAS.

Out of Scope:
1. Sunsetting the Envision/MyPortal software and any notifications to the Envision vendor.

2. Integration with any other software or systems not specified in this document.

3. A Change Management Plan or any change management activities for SEAA staff will be handled independent of this project.

4. The pilot for using RDS validation for 2024 will be managed independent of this project, but the information learned from the pilot will be used for creating the validation function in the new system.

5. No procedures for handling paper applications for ESA+ and the Opportunity Scholarship. Paper applications will not be accepted for either program.

6. A K12 website. This will be pursued as a separate project.

Note: Additions to scope identified after the sign-off of this Project Charter will be handled though a Change Request (CR) process. The SEAA and CFI Project Sponsors will approve any scope changes based on an assessment from CFI on the impact to schedule, budget and resources.
Objective
The "MyPortal K12" development project will unify the management of ESA+ and Opportunity Scholarship functions for schools, service providers, and families for scholastic achievement across North Carolina.
 
IV. Project Deliverables
Functional Requirements
1. System functionality required to apply, renew, disburse and track funds for both Programs.
Web portal/ Dashboard for SEAA, parents, participating schools and service providers.

2. Application Process

3. Applicant Validation/Evaluation Process

4. Non-Public School Validation

5. Override Process

6. Lottery Process

7. Disbursements - to schools for both programs and disbursements to parents and approved third party equipment and service providers for the ESA+ program only.

8. Third Party Equipment, Service Provider & Non-Public School Validation

9. Funding Adjustments

10. Renewals Process

11. Funds Tracking for Parents

School Management Functions
1. New School Registration Process

2. Payment Processing Support

3. Compliance Requirements

4. Issue Tracking

5. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents.

1. SEAA Administrative functionalities

1. Robust query capabilities to perform ad-hoc queries.

2. SEAA Funds Tracking - to include monthly and yearly reconciliation processes.

3. Batch posting to student or school records.

4. Viewable audit history of transactions and who processed them.

5. Ability to delete duplicate records and make minor text changes to the name of a field or explanatory text in the system.

6. Ability to post a banner message on any User Interface screen (referred to as &ldquo;lemon bar messages&rdquo; on CFNC) without the need for IT support.

7. Ability to create a custom label/field on records to use for querying purposes, data analysis, and/or to otherwise flag records meeting certain criteria (note, these fields would not appear on customer facing UI screens).

8. Ability for SEAA to do bulk emails to account holders without needing IT assistance.

1. Customer relationship management functionality

1. A place to store interactions with schools and parents, identify and document issues to enable SEAA to understand a customer&rsquo;s situation quickly.

2. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents.

Non-Functional Requirements
1. Integration with &ldquo;Class Wallet&rdquo; software used for a funds repository.

2. Conversion of data from Envision to the new system.

3. Adherence to best practices for development of web-based systems.
Secure login process for the SEAA and Parents, and Schools/Service Providers web portals.

4. Access Control process for managing system/web portal access and permissions.

5. Security Requirements to be defined with the assistance of CFI Security and SEAA Information Technology group.

6. Adherence to industry standards for system performance (availability, redundancy, scalability, etc.).

7. Adherence to brand standards (SEAA&rsquo;s).

8. Adherence to industry Accessibility standards for web sites.

Communications, documentation and planning needed for implementation and on-going maintenance.
1. Production Support Plan

2. Disaster Recovery Plan

3. SEAA defined communication plan and implementation thereof for notification about the new system to current program/scholarship recipients and any marketing about the new system to the public.

4. Staff Training to be jointly coordinated by SEAA and CFI.

5. Business and Technical system documentation.

1. Phone System integration exploration: Business Requirements; IT assessment of the work; and a proposed implementation timeline.

System Development Deliverables
1. Statement of Work(s) and Contract(s) for procured technical resources

2. Functional & Non-Functional Requirements

3. Architecture / Infrastructure Plan

4. UX Designs / Prototypes / Wireframes

5. Task tracking in Jira   

6. Release Plan / Roadmap

Project Management Deliverables
1. Project Charter (this document)

2. Communication Plan

3. Milestone Tracking /Schedule (to be updated on-going)

4. Monthly Project Status Reports

5. Risk Log (to be updated on-going)

6. Key Decisions Log (to be updated on-going)

7. Scope Change Request Process & Log (to be updated on-going)

8. Project Confluence Space for collaboration and storage of project documentation.

*Additional Milestones will be identified to track progress once the project gets underway. The Milestones and their progress will be tracked in Confluence.
VII. Assumptions  
|   | Description |
| 1 | CFI will determine, retain and oversee the technical resources needed to fulfill this engagement with the funding to be approved by SEAA. |
| 2 | SEAA staff participation will be needed throughout the effort. CFI will make every effort to utilize their time effectively. |
| 3 | CFI staff will provide on-going maintenance and support for the system post go-live. |
| 4 | SEAA staff coordinated by SEAA will perform User Acceptance Testing. |
| 5 | Other than potentially providing parent/student validation assistance for the Opportunity Scholarship for the 2024/2025 school year, the plan is to use Envision software to meet all 2024/2025 and 2025/2026 ESA+ Program and Opportunity Scholarship needs. |
| 6 | Budget tracking and meeting of any State of NC reporting requirements on project status or dollars spent/remaining will be handled by SEAA independent of the project. |
| 8 | A project Change Request (CR) will be completed and approved by SEAA & CFI Project Sponsors if a decision is made to add phone system integration to scope. The CR will include details of the agreed upon project impacts (e.g., schedule, cost, and resources). |
| 9 | Not all of the SEAA needs covered under this state appropriation need to be managed under a single project. Discrete bodies of work may be better resourced and tracked under separate project umbrellas with their own Project Charter. Consideration of this can be made, especially if any additional scope needs arise. |
 
 
VIII. Risks
|   | Description |
| 1 | With long duration projects, there is always a risk that key project resources may leave before the project is completed. |
| 2 | Developing a system from scratch inherently carries more risk of failure than implementation of a SaaS system or upgrading an existing system. |
| 3 | Competing priorities for CFI and/or SEAA resources could impact project participation and the timely completion of project work. |
| 4 | Delays in obtaining external technical resources may delay the start of programming. |
| 5 | Scope creep can adversely impact the overall timeline, cause a need for resequencing work (even a need for rework), and impact team morale and momentum. |
| 6 | Late identification of needed system/product integrations could result in a need to re-do architecture/infrastructure plans, as well as impact the overall timeline. |
| 7 | CFI will need access to export Envision data and potentially support from Golden Bridges (the Envision vendor). It is unknown (by CFI) what Golden Bridges' contractual obligations are in this area and how cooperative they will be. |
| 8 | As of the start of this project, CFI has no experience with the Class Wallet software and what it will take to integrate this with the new system. |
| 9 | Laws enacted may make changes to either or both Programs while the project is in progress or shortly thereafter. Building flexibility into the new system is key, but it&rsquo;s not possible to anticipate and account for every change scenario. |
| 10 | There is always a possibility that SEAA will replace one of their third-party providers and this will likely impact scope, schedule and resources. We will use the Change Request process for these changes.   |
IX. Project Success Criteria
1. The system will be fully operational by 2/1/2026.*

2. SEAA&rsquo;s current manual processes performed in the administration of the ESA and Opportunity Scholarship will be automated to the extent possible.

3. The deliverables specified in this Charter will be met.*

4. SEAA will be well informed of the project status throughout the project.

5. A clear process for reporting any Production issues and future enhancement requests will be defined prior to any functionality going-live.  

*Future approved scope changes (additions or deletions) could impact the deliverables produced and the project completion date.

# ADA Requirements for K12 Development

*Created: 2025-09-29T18:20:53.985Z by Jacqui Kehoe*
*Last Updated: 2025-09-29T18:25:13.385Z by Jacqui Kehoe*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4296704036/ADA+Requirements+for+K12+Development*

---

Website ADA Compliance Checklist (WCAG 2.1 Level
AA)MyPortal K12 will need to follow and adhere to these ADA requirements.

Text & Content
&bull; Add alt text for all meaningful images, logos, and icons.
&bull; Use clear headings (H1&ndash;H6) in order, no skipping levels.
&bull; Ensure all text has a contrast ratio of at least 4.5:1 (big text can be 3:1).
&bull; Let users resize text up to 200% without breaking the layout.
Navigation & Structure
&bull; All site functionality must work with keyboard only (Tab, Enter, Space, Arrow keys).
&bull; Provide a 'Skip to Content' link at the top of pages.
&bull; Use consistent navigation menus and buttons across the site.
&bull; Add breadcrumbs or clear indicators of 'where you are' on the site.
Forms & Inputs
&bull; Every form field has a visible label (not just placeholder text).
&bull; Provide clear instructions and error messages (e.g., 'Password must be 8 characters').
&bull; Ensure error messages are announced to screen readers.
&bull; Don&rsquo;t rely only on color to show required fields or errors&mdash;use symbols or text too.
Multimedia
&bull; Provide captions for all prerecorded video/audio.
&bull; Provide transcripts for audio-only media.
&bull; If budget allows, add audio descriptions for key visuals in video.
Interactivity
&bull; Don&rsquo;t use flashing animations (or at least keep them under 3 per second).
&bull; Give users control: pause/stop buttons for carousels, auto-playing videos, or animations.
&bull; Ensure modal popups and dropdowns are keyboard accessible and announced by screen readers.
Technical Stuff
&bull; Pages must have a unique, descriptive tag.
&bull; Use ARIA roles only when needed (don&rsquo;t over-engineer).
&bull; All links should make sense out of context ('Read more about accessibility').
&bull; Responsive design that works equally well on desktop, tablet, and mobile.
Testing & Validation
&bull; Test with a screen reader (NVDA for Windows or VoiceOver for Mac).
&bull; Run automated checks with WAVE, axe DevTools, or Lighthouse.
&bull; Do manual keyboard-only testing (pretend your mouse is broken).



# Product Design and User Experience

*Created: 2025-04-01T12:36:55.750Z by Aaron Kennelly*
*Last Updated: 2025-04-03T00:55:38.037Z by Aaron Kennelly*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3941105674/Product+Design+and+User+Experience*

---

- Vision

- How to use this Wiki

Welcome to CFI K-12 Design Wiki, a centralized resource designed to elevate design practices by fostering consistency, clarity, and operational efficiency across teams. This is written for a cross-functional audience including:
- product managers

- product owners

- technical architects

- developers

- ux designers

- scrum masters

- business analysts

It includes practical tips to help both teams new to Product Design and experienced practitioners. The goal is to ensure continuous user-centered discovery alongside delivery, so the team builds the right product as well as builds the product right.
## Vision
Our vision is to create a unified approach that bridges strategic objectives with day-to-day design and development activities. We aim to ensure that each product team demonstrates a commitment to quality and innovation.
## How to use this Wiki
This is a living document that evolves with new insights and industry trends. It outlines our purpose, goals, and the ways each designer can contribute to our collective success.
- Use the table of contents to jump to sections relevant to your current task.

- Follow our contribution guidelines to update content or add new templates.

- Check the version history at the end of each document for the latest updates.

- Look out for links to recommended templates throughout the Wiki to help standardize your work.



# Product Design Overview

*Created: 2025-03-31T20:47:06.868Z by Aaron Kennelly*
*Last Updated: 2025-04-17T21:43:17.992Z by Aaron Kennelly*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3939795011/Product+Design+Overview*

---

This section defines the foundation for our design work and acts as a guide for the design process.
none## Economic Impact of Product Design
Product Design using a human-centered design (UCD also referred to as UX) process are integral components of modern product development. The economic benefits of UX integration are substantial and measurable. 
Every dollar invested in UX research, you save $10 in development and $100 in post-release maintenance.1 This 1:10:100 rule emphasizes the value of early UX investment. By incorporating UX from the beginning, organizations can reduce product development cycles by 33% to 50%, a game-changing advantage where speed-to-market directly impacts profitability.2
In the software industry, about 5% of features are used 95% of the time.3 UX Research can dramatically improve the focus of features, ensuring that development resources are allocated to features that will have the most impact on user satisfaction and business outcomes. 
A study by Harvard Business School found that 95% of new products fail due to a lack of market research and consumer insights.⁶
## What is Product Design?
Product design is a multidisciplinary process focused on creating products that are both functional and delightful to use. It involves understanding user needs through research, brainstorming innovative solutions, iteratively prototyping, testing designs, and refining them to meet both brand and usability goals.
The core of product design is crafting experiences that align solving end-user problems with business objectives.
## What is Design Operations?
Design Operations, or DesignOps, is the backbone that supports product design. It sets up the frameworks, standards, and processes that help designers work efficiently and consistently. It acts as the bridge between creative ideas and successful product outcomes.
Our Design System Style Guide is a key DesignOps resource that maintains consistency across all design projects, ensuring that every element reflects our brand and meets user needs.
## Design Framework
Design work is broken into clear, manageable phases. 
1. Research: Gather user data and insights.

2. Ideation: Generate and sketch a variety of ideas.

3. Prototyping: Develop early versions of your design and make them tangible

4. Testing: Validate assumptions with real users.

5. Revision: Refine designs based on data.

6. Implementation: Finalize the design and hand-offer to development. Work with QA to ensure designs were deployed according to original specs.

Each phase is supported by dedicated templates (e.g. research, ideation) to ensure consistency and clarity. 
See Standard, Tools, and Templates for more information.
## References
1. Karat, C. M. (n.d.). The 1:10:100 rule for UX investment. IBM T. J. Watson Research Center.

2. Bias, R. G., & Mayhew, D. J. (2005). Cost-Justifying Usability: An Update for the Internet Age. Morgan Kaufmann.

3. Standish Group. (2020). CHAOS Report. https://www.standishgroup.com/sample_research_files/CHAOSReport2020.pdf

4. Abdulla, Shameel. (2023). Do You Know Why 95% of Product Innovations Fail?
