# Mapping Data from Enrollment Builder

*Created: 2025-09-17T16:05:32.359Z by Robert Beasy*
*Last Updated: 2025-09-29T19:58:34.681Z by Robert Beasy*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4271341591/Mapping+Data+from+Enrollment+Builder*

---

1. Give the overview of how the mapper will work

2. Give overview of how publish will work

3. List work to be done by each conceptual group

 
1. Overview of how the mapper will work

 
Executive Summary
 
After careful review, it is clear that Azure Data Factory with Azure Data Flow is the best choice for mapping data from the enrollment builder to Azure SQL Server. The reasons for choosing Data Factory are primarily that it is Azure's front-line ETL (Extract, Transform, Load) tool and it remains user-friendly while being sufficiently robust to easily meet all K12's needs for data mapping and transformation, also it is relatively cheap for our use case.
 
- Footnote: Modern cloud-based ETL tools are extremely powerful compared to the K12 use case. For this reason, we will want to ensure that operations use single-partition since all of these operations will be transforming a single JSON document. HDFS clustering and Data Bricks operations are not needed. This will reduce cost and speed up all operations.

 
Here are the conceptual groups who need to perform work to enable this epic/initiative:
- Enrollment Builder

- Enrollment Viewer

- Infrastructure

- Data Engineering

- Program-partner Back-end developers

 
Overview
 
At release of K12, we will be providing the first version for each of the 5 enrollment programs that are currently in scope:
- Provider v1,

- Household v1,

- Household Opportunity v1,

- Household ESA+ v1,

- and Schools v1.

 
In this document, I will use the example of the provider enrollment program to avoid overusing pronouns that would likely confuse the reader as much as it did me.
 
When the applicant loads the provider enrollment program, the enrollment-viewer component is loaded with a single parameter specifying the program to load, in this case "provider". The enrollment-viewer component then makes a call to get the current published version of the "provider" enrollment program and loads the program. All enrollment programs will embed in their payload their type and version for the Data Factory pipelines.
As the applicant steps through the enrollment program, the data will be saved to Redis cache [this is a performance optimization that will prevent overloading of the SQL Database during peak times].
When the applicant completes the enrollment program and submits the application, the applicant's request will be stored in a `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/` folder in Azure Data Lake Gen2. 
Azure Data Factory will be triggered by a 15 minute timer running the `provider_v1` pipeline. The output from the Azure Data Factory pipeline will be saved to Azure SQL Server in the relevant provider application schema(s).
 
Azure Data Lake folders for storing JSON documents:
- Data will be initially landed in `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/` as [raw JSON data].

- On error, Azure Data Factory will move a record to  `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/error` folder [still raw JSON data]

- Upon successful processing, data will be moved to  `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/processed-applications/MM/DD/HH/mm/` [Still raw JSON data]

 
These folders will enable easier debugging. Since batches are running every 15 minutes, we will be able to easily find data for a Azure Data Factory batch operation by checking these folders.
Via managed identities, Azure Data Factory and Azure Functions will be allowed to access the ADL `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/` folder as shown in the diagram below.
 
 
 
1. Overview of how publish will work

What is published:
 
The published version of the Provider enrollment program is defined as:
The version of the Provider enrollment program that has been selected for publishing. This might not be the first version of the program to be built, it is possible SEAA will create several iterations before landing on a version they choose to publish. Also, it is possible that the latest version to be worked on will not be the one SEAA chooses to publish. The published version is the version that the SEAA administrators chose to publish.
A Data Lake folder for landing raw data submitted from applicants, in this example it would be:
`/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/`
A published Azure Data Factory Pipeline with a working Data Flow that has been proven to work with the data that outputs from a user submitting a Provider enrollment application.
And of course the Azure SQL Server database with a schema that matches the output format from Azure Data Factory.
 
Why does this need to be defined?: Because the version number needs to be kept in lockstep between K12, Azure Data Lake and Azure Data Factory Pipelines.
 
 
The Publish Process
 
The publish process requires validation of all parts to avoid a scenario where corrupted/unusable data is collected from applicants.
 
Here is an example of publishing v2:
SEAA Publishes v2 of the Provider Program.
V2 of the Provider Program is validated by a program traveling all paths and ending on the final state with valid data collected. The enrollment program needs to be proven to be valid [that all paths lead to a user submission that contains all data required and that no dead ends exist in any branch].
 
Each example of valid JSON data needs to be provided to Data Engineering to facilitate work on v2 of the ADF pipeline. The variations of the data collected during enrollment [based on branches] must all be proven valid.
The infrastructure team creates a new ADL folder `/enrollment-data/provider/v2/enrollment-applications/YYYY/` and places the example JSON data files in the folder to enable Data Engineering work.
 
Data Engineer creates a valid 'provider_v2' pipeline. It will be triggered Azure Data Factory 15 minute timer trigger by new documents landing in `/enrollment-data/provider/v2/enrollment-applications/YYYY/`. When finished, the 'provider_v2' pipeline is published, ready to process v2 requests that begin landing in the `/enrollment-data/provider/v2/enrollment-applications/YYYY/` ADL folder. All of the enrollment variations must all be proven to be ADF mapping convertible to the expected SQL Server in a format.
 
Note: In 99.9%+ of cases, the modification from v1 should be minor. In such cases the Data Engineer will simply clone v1 and make minor changes to produce v2. 
 
v2 is allowed to go live since it has now passed all validation steps.
 
IMPORTANT: Unless all validation steps succeed, it is better to not allow the new version of an enrollment program to be published and be taken live.
What does "taken live" mean?: The public end users submit v2 forms instead of v1 forms when they load the enrollment viewer in the provider application after the published version has changed.
 
Question: What do we do when a user has started an application on v1 and v2 becomes available?
 
Archive old versions
 
Although we are going to use git to support source control for Azure Data Factory work, we are not using git versioning to control release cycles for our ADF pipelines. Instead we are going to allow for  'provider_v1' and  'provider_v2' to exist side by side. This will smooth the release process when SEAA needs breaking changes. However that does mean that we need to have a process in place to retire old versions.
 
 
Note: Azure Data Lake data should not be purged since it is a source of record for data submitted by applicants before transformation.
 
1. List work to be done by each conceptual group

 
Enrollment Builder
 
The enrollment builder will need to:
- Change flow to support selecting the program type first

- Enable versioning of programs separate from published versions

- Enable publish versioning

- Create a final approval for publishing so that Azure Data Factory work is proven valid before allowing a published version to go live.

 
Enrollment Viewer
 
This needs to be broken out into a separate component that can be embedded in partner applications. It will accept only a parameter key pointing to the program type. The API will need to be created for the viewer to call in order to retrieve the latest version of that program type. Also, it will need to embed the program type and version in the JSON payload.
 
Intermediate saves as the user steps through the program will be saved to both local storage and to Redis as JSON format via an Azure Function call. The current save to SQL Server will need to be removed.
 
Finally, it will need to POST data to the submit endpoint for processing. The POST will need to send the JSON formatted data to Azure Data Lake as mentioned above.
 
Infrastructure
 
- Create Azure Data Factory Instance

- Grant access to Data Engineer team

- Create ADO Git repo for Azure Data Factory

- Create the folders for v1 of all applications in Azure Data Lake

- Set up managed identities to lock down access to Azure Data Lake folders, ex: `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/`

- Grant access for Azure Data Factory pipelines to write to Azure SQL Server tables.

 
 Azure Data Factory Security
 
- Data factory Authentication using managed identity.

- Provide RBAC roles or permissions on targeted resources

- Data Classification and Governance: Integrate with Microsoft Purview

- We are going to need ADF to run on an isolated runtime, Isolated Runtimes: Use self-hosted IR. When all the resources are in the cloud there is not a need for self-hosted IR, instead private networks utilizing managed VNets are sufficient for private connectivity.

- Use Private Endpoints: Enable Azure Private Link to connect ADF to data stores and resources exclusively via private endpoints

 
Program Partner Back-end developers
 
All tables where data lands should have standard audit fields added:
- Created_datetimeoffset

- Created_by

- Modified_datetimeoffset

- Modified_by

- Enrollment_FileName [source json document]

 
Why: to address the potential need for data clean up.
 
Data Engineering
 
The Data Engineering team will build and maintain the Azure Data Factory Pipelines.
Enhanced Error Handling and Monitoring for Azure Data Factory Pipelines
To ensure robust monitoring and effective error handling for Azure Data Factory pipeline jobs, the following procedures have been implemented:
Error Monitoring with Azure Log Analytics: Azure Log Analytics is configured to capture and log detailed event data for all pipeline activities, enabling real-time tracking and analysis of errors.
Notification System: An Azure Data Factory monitoring alert system has been established, with email notifications configured to inform the designated group of any issues or pipeline failures, ensuring prompt awareness and response.
Error Data Management: In the event of a pipeline failure, associated data is systematically relocated to the `/enrollment-data/provider/v1/enrollment-applications/YYYY/error` directory for further analysis and resolution.
These measures enhance the reliability and traceability of Azure Data Factory pipeline operations. Please let me know if further clarification or adjustments are required.



# Design and Architecture for PandaDoc Integration

*Created: 2025-10-06T17:44:50.501Z by Robert Beasy*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-06T17:46:34.134Z by Robert Beasy*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4318101545/Design+and+Architecture+for+PandaDoc+Integration*

---

Design and Architecture for PandaDoc Integration
 
Introduction
This document outlines the architecture and integration plan for incorporating PandaDoc into K12. The integration facilitates electronic signatures (e-signatures) for documents, leveraging PandaDoc's API for document creation, signing, and webhook notifications. Signed documents will be stored in Azure Data Lake Gen2 (ADL Gen2) with Hierarchical Namespaces enabled, secured via Entra ID permissions. The backend utilizes Azure Functions for API endpoints and webhook handling, both triggered via HTTP. Azure SQL Database will store metadata and links to documents, while Azure Key Vault will manage PandaDoc API credentials. Managed identities will handle internal Azure resource permissions.
 
The architecture follows the C4 model, providing progressive levels of detail: System Context (Level 1), Containers (Level 2), and Components (Level 3). This ensures a clear, scalable, and secure design. The plan addresses workflow, security implications, non-functional requirements (NFRs), data retention, infrastructure needs, and strategies for development, support, and administration. It incorporates best practices for PandaDoc integration, including webhook security via HMAC verification and IP whitelisting, to mitigate risks associated with ingesting external data.
 
C4 Architecture Model
 
Level 1: System Context
The system context diagram illustrates the high-level interactions between the core application, external users, and PandaDoc.
- Core System: The Angular application and Azure backend, responsible for task management, user interactions, and document storage.

- Users:
Households (end-users signing documents).

- SEAA Administrators (monitor signatures, manage access).

- Schools and Providers (optional access to household documents, configured by SEAA admins).

External Systems:
- PandaDoc: Provides document templates, e-signature workflows, email notifications, and webhooks for completion events.

- Email Service: Integrated via PandaDoc for sending signing links (superseding standard application emails where applicable).

Interactions:
- The core system creates documents in PandaDoc via API calls, triggering emails with signing links.

- Users sign documents in PandaDoc.

- PandaDoc notifies the core system via webhooks upon completion, enabling PDF download and storage in ADL Gen2.

- SEAA admins access monitoring interfaces within the application.

 
 
Level 2: Containers
The container diagram breaks down the core system into deployable units and their interactions.
- Containers:
Angular Frontend: Single-page application handling user interfaces for tasks, in-app messages, and monitoring.

- Azure Functions (API Layer): Serverless endpoints for application logic, including task creation and PandaDoc API interactions.

- Azure Functions (Webhook Listener): Dedicated HTTP-triggered function for receiving PandaDoc webhooks.

- Azure SQL Database: Stores task metadata, document links (to PandaDoc and ADL Gen2), and signature status.

- Azure Data Lake Gen2: Hierarchical storage for signed PDFs, with folders protected by Entra ID.

- Azure Key Vault: Secure storage for PandaDoc API keys and shared webhook secrets.

External Containers:
- PandaDoc API: For document creation from templates, population with metadata, and PDF downloads.

- PandaDoc Webhooks: Push notifications for events like document completion.

Interactions:
- Angular calls Azure Functions API to initiate document creation.

- Azure Functions API uses managed identities to access Key Vault and SQL, then calls PandaDoc API.

- PandaDoc webhooks POST to Azure Functions Webhook Listener, which verifies and processes payloads.

- Webhook Listener downloads PDFs from PandaDoc, validates them, and stores in ADL Gen2 with appropriate Entra ID permissions.

 
 
 
Level 3: Components
The component diagram details key modules within containers.
- Within Angular Frontend:
Task Management Component: Handles task assignment, in-app messages, and links to PandaDoc (via email from PandaDoc).

- Monitoring Dashboard: For SEAA admins to track signature status (queries Azure SQL for metadata).

Within Azure Functions API:
- Document Creation Component: Calls PandaDoc API to create documents from templates (5 fixed templates), populates with tokens/fields (e.g., user metadata), and initiates sending (triggering PandaDoc emails).

- Metadata Storage Component: Inserts records into Azure SQL with PandaDoc document IDs and future ADL links.

Within Azure Functions Webhook Listener:
- HMAC Verification Component: Validates incoming requests using shared key from Key Vault and IP safelisting.

- Payload Processing Component: Parses JSON (including metadata, tokens, fields, products, pricing) for events like document_state_changed (status: "document.completed"), document_completed_pdf_ready, and recipient_completed.

- PDF Download Component: Uses PandaDoc document ID to call Download Protected Document API.

- Validation Component: Applies industry best practices (e.g., virus scanning, format checks; references separate story K12-2667).

- Storage Component: Uploads validated PDF to ADL Gen2 in user-specific folders (e.g., /households/{household_id}/documents/{document_type}/{year}), applies Entra ID permissions (household read, SEAA admin read/write, optional school/provider read configured via SEAA).

- Metadata Update Component: Updates Azure SQL with ADL link and status.

Data Flows:
- Templates referenced by ID in code/configuration.

- Association via tokens (include task/user ID in PandaDoc metadata for mapping).

- Permissions: Managed identities for Azure services; Entra ID groups for ADL folders (e.g., assign household principal to folder ACL).

 
Integration Workflow for E-Signature
1. Template Management: SEAA admins create/version templates in PandaDoc portal (disable forwarding/suggest edits; use variables). Maintain one live version per type (5 templates total). Reference template IDs in Azure Functions configuration.

2. Task Assignment: When a task requires signature, Angular triggers Azure Functions API.

3. Document Creation: API calls PandaDoc to create document from template, populates data (e.g., via tokens including user/task ID), and sends it (PandaDoc emails link to recipient).

4. User Signing: Recipient signs via PandaDoc link (email from PandaDoc; in-app message from application).

5. Webhook Notification: PandaDoc POSTs to Azure Functions endpoint (e.g., https://development-api-enrollment.azure-api.net/enrollment/signedPdfUpload) upon document_completed_pdf_ready.

6. Processing: Verify HMAC/IP, parse payload, download PDF, validate, store in ADL Gen2 folder, update SQL metadata.

7. Monitoring: SEAA admins query SQL via Angular dashboard for status; archive old data post-enrollment.

This supports 80,000+ documents annually by leveraging PandaDoc's scalability.
 
Security Implications and Access Plan
- Webhook Security:
HMAC-SHA256 verification using shared key from Key Vault.

- IP restrictions on Azure Functions to PandaDoc IPs (US: 52.12.31.116, 52.37.240.175, 35.167.41.246; EU if applicable).

- Reject unauthorized calls (HTTP 401/403).

Data Ingestion:
- Validate PDFs for viruses/malware (K12-2667).

- Sanitize payload; only process expected events/fields.

Access Control:
- ADL Gen2: Hierarchical Namespaces with Entra ID ACLs (e.g., household: read; SEAA: full; schools/providers: read if assigned by SEAA via group membership).

- SQL: Row-level security via Entra ID.

- PandaDoc API: OAuth tokens in Key Vault, rotated periodically.

Best Practices: Encrypt data in transit/rest; audit logs in Azure Monitor; comply with GDPR/HIPAA if applicable.
Non-Functional Requirements
| Requirement | Description |
| Scalability | Handle 80,000+ documents/year; Azure Functions auto-scale; PandaDoc API limits monitored. |
| Performance | Webhook response <20s (PandaDoc timeout); document creation <5s. |
| Availability | 99.9% uptime; use Azure regions for redundancy. |
| Reliability | Idempotent webhook processing (check document ID in SQL); PandaDoc retries (up to 3). |
| Maintainability | Versioned templates; configurable endpoints. |
Data Retention Policy
- Retain signed PDFs in ADL Gen2 for 7 years (or per regulatory needs).

- Archive old documents post-enrollment to cold storage tier.

- SQL metadata: Retain indefinitely for auditing; purge inactive records after 10 years.

- Cleanup: Automated Azure Function to move expired data, avoiding clutter in admin workspaces.

Infrastructure Resources Needed
- Azure Functions (consumption plan for API/webhook).

- ADL Gen2 account with Hierarchical Namespaces.

- Azure SQL Database (hyperscale for scale).

- Azure Key Vault.

- Entra ID groups for permissions.

- Azure Monitor for logging/alerts.

Plan for Development, Support, and Administration
- Development:
Phase 1: Implement API for document creation/sending.

- Phase 2: Webhook listener with verification/download/storage.

- Phase 3: Angular updates for monitoring; integration testing with PandaDoc sandbox.

- Use CI/CD (Azure DevOps) for deployments.

Support:
- Monitor via Azure Application Insights; alerts for webhook failures.

- Incident response: Debug using PandaDoc Webhooks History tab.

- Scale testing for 80,000+ documents.

Administration:
- SEAA admins manage templates in PandaDoc portal.

- Rotate keys in Key Vault quarterly.

- Annual review of permissions/Entra groups.

- Documentation: Maintain in Confluence; train teams on workflows.

 
This comprehensive plan ensures secure, efficient integration, addressing all specified requirements while prioritizing scalability and compliance.

# Communication Matrix

*Created: 2025-10-16T15:04:40.866Z by Matt Vaughn*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-16T15:05:56.604Z by Matt Vaughn*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4351721474/Communication+Matrix*

---

# Communication & Notification Matrix
Purpose: map domain events/operations (DDD context) to required external communications (email/SMS/print) and in‑app notifications. Each entry lists DDD element, parent/context, operation triggers, aggregate/root, recipients, recommended channels, reminder patterns (when recipients need reminders/status updates), and message intent (status vs action required).
Assumptions
- Default communication channels: Email (primary), In‑app notifications (secondary), SMS (opt‑in for urgent reminders). Postal/print used only for legal/compliance notices where required.

- Time semantics: "T0" = event occurrence time; reminder timings are suggestions and should be configurable per program.

Guidance for implementers
- Create centralized Notification templates keyed by event type and locale.

- Store notification audit records (who, what, when, channel, delivery status) on the related aggregate for compliance.

- Support idempotency (ensure duplicate events don't create duplicate external messages).

1. Application lifecycle &mdash; submission / status

- DDD element: Event / Aggregate

- Parent / Context: Application & Eligibility Context / Application aggregate

- Operations / triggers: ApplicationSubmitted; ApplicationStatusChanged (underReview, accepted/eligible, ineligible, waitlisted, awarded, declined)

- Aggregate / Root: Application

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian (primary), Caseworker (if assigned), School contact (if relevant)

- Channels: Email (receipt/status), In‑app (status + action tasks), SMS (opt‑in for submission receipt and urgent deadlines)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Receipt confirmation (Email + In‑app) &mdash; immediate

- T0+48h: If supporting docs required and not uploaded, reminder (Email + In‑app)

- Status changes: immediate status notification (Email + In‑app)

- 3 days before any deadline triggered by status (e.g., accept award) &mdash; reminder (Email; SMS opt‑in)

Message intent: Action required (upload docs, sign), Status update (progress)
1. Eligibility / Determination intake

- DDD element: Event / Value object

- Parent / Context: EligibilityDetermination / EligibilityDetermination aggregate

- Operations / triggers: EligibilityDeterminationSubmitted, EligibilityDeterminationResultPublished

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, Caseworker

- Channels: Email (result + instructions), In‑app (detailed determination view)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Submission acknowledgement (Email + In‑app)

- If determination requires more info: reminders at T0+48h and T0+7d

- On final determination: immediate notification (Email + In‑app)

Message intent: Action required (provide docs), Status update (result)
1. Domicile & verification notifications

- DDD element: Events / Aggregate

- Parent / Context: DomicileVerification / DomicileDetermination or EligibilityVerificationCase

- Operations / triggers: DomicileVerificationInitiated, VerificationDocumentationRequested, DomicileVerified, DomicileDenied

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, School (if verification affects school assignment), Caseworker

- Channels: Email (requests/results), In‑app (upload UI + status), SMS (deadline reminders opt‑in)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Verification started (Email + In‑app)

- Deadline reminders: 7d/3d/24h before required document submission deadline (Email; SMS opt‑in)

- On final result: immediate notification with next steps (Email + In‑app)

Message intent: Action required (upload docs), Status update (result)
1. Lottery / award offering / acceptance

- DDD element: Event / Aggregate

- Parent / Context: Awarding & Funding / Award / AwardOffer

- Operations / triggers: LotteryConducted, AwardOfferMade, AwardAccepted, AwardDeclined

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, School Admin

- Channels: Email (result and offer details), In‑app (offer acceptance flow), SMS (opt‑in urgent acceptance reminders)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Lottery results / offer (Email + In‑app)

- 7d/3d/24h before acceptance deadline: reminders (Email; SMS opt‑in)

- If offer accepted: confirmation (Email + In‑app)

Message intent: Action required (accept/decline), Status update
1. Award activation & account creation

- DDD element: Event

- Parent / Context: ScholarshipAccount / ScholarshipAccount aggregate

- Operations / triggers: AwardActivated, ScholarshipAccountCreated, FundsMovedToWallet

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian

- Channels: Email (activation, balance summary), In‑app (wallet UI), Push/SMS (opt‑in for immediate funds notice)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Account created / funds available (Email + In‑app)

- Monthly balance summary (Email; In‑app reports)

Message intent: Status update (balance and funding), Action required (set up payment preferences)
1. To‑Do / Document requests / Deadlines

- DDD element: Entity / Task

- Parent / Context: ToDoItem / Notice aggregate

- Operations / triggers: ToDoCreated, ToDoOverdue, DeadlineApproaching

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, Assigned staff

- Channels: In‑app (task list, progress), Email (initial + overdue), SMS (for urgent/near‑deadline opt‑in)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: To‑Do created (In‑app; Email for primary)

- Deadline reminders: configurable series (7d/3d/1d/2h) based on urgency

- Overdue escalation: 1d/3d escalations to staff (Email + In‑app)

Message intent: Action required (complete task), Status update (completion)
1. ParentAgreement / LEA Release signing

- DDD element: Aggregate / Event

- Parent / Context: ParentAgreement / ParentAgreement aggregate

- Operations / triggers: ParentAgreementRequested, ParentAgreementSigned, LEAReleaseSigned

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, LEA Contact

- Channels: Email (signature request + receipts), In‑app (signing workflow), SMS (opt‑in reminders)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Signature request (Email + In‑app)

- Reminders: 7d/3d/1d before signature deadline; daily until signed up to configurable limit

- On signed: confirmation (Email + In‑app)

Message intent: Action required (sign), Status update (signed)
1. Purchase / purchase review / payment

- DDD element: Aggregate / Events

- Parent / Context: Purchasing & Expenses / PurchaseRequest, ScholarshipAccount

- Operations / triggers: PurchaseSubmitted, PurchaseApproved, PurchaseRejected, PaymentProcessed, RefundProcessed

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, Provider/Seller, Finance team

- Channels: Email (submission + decision + payment receipts), In‑app (purchase history + status), SMS (opt‑in for payment status)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Submission acknowledgement (Email + In‑app)

- On approval/rejection: immediate notification with rationale and next steps (Email + In‑app)

- On payment: payment receipt (Email + In‑app)

- If pending action required (e.g., more docs), reminder series (48h/7d)

Message intent: Action required (supply fix), Status update (approval/payment)
1. Accessory / timing rule violations

- DDD element: Policy validation result

- Parent / Context: Rules Engine evaluation on PurchaseRequest

- Operations / triggers: PurchaseValidationFailed (rule code), PurchaseValidationPassed

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, Review Team (if escalated)

- Channels: In‑app (detailed rule failure), Email (summary + remediation), Staff escalation channels (email + case assignment)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Immediate notification explaining rule violation and remediation steps (In‑app + Email)

- If remediation required: reminders 48h/7d until resolved; escalate to staff if unresolved

Message intent: Action required (correct purchase), Status update
1. Provider / vendor enrollment & payment

- DDD element: Aggregate / Events

- Parent / Context: Provider Enrollment / Provider aggregate

- Operations / triggers: ProviderApplied, ProviderEnrolled, ProviderPaymentProcessed, ProviderPaymentFailed

- Recipients: Provider contact, Finance team

- Channels: Email (application status, payments), In‑app (provider portal), Vendor-specific channels (EFT/remittance files)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Application receipt and enrollment decision notifications

- Payment retries/failures: immediate notification and schedule for retries; monthly payment summaries

- Credential expirations: 30d/7d reminders prior to expiration

Message intent: Action required (complete onboarding), Status update (payments)
1. Compliance / audit / misuse / sanctions

- DDD element: Aggregate / Events

- Parent / Context: Compliance & Agreements / ComplianceRecord

- Operations / triggers: MisuseDetected, ComplianceRecordCreated, AwardTerminated, AwardRevoked

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, Program Admins, Legal (if escalated)

- Channels: Email (formal notices), In‑app (case details), Phone/SMS (ops escalation as needed)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: High‑priority notification to parent and assigned caseworker (Email + In‑app; phone for urgent)

- Escalation timeline: immediate, 24h, 72h depending on severity; legal/termination notices follow required legal cadence

Message intent: Action required (respond/appeal), Status update (investigation progress)
1. Renewal & re‑evaluation

- DDD element: Events / Aggregate

- Parent / Context: Renewal / ContinuingEligibility

- Operations / triggers: RenewalOfferSent, RenewalApplicationStarted, RenewalCompleted

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian

- Channels: Email (announcements + instructions), In‑app (renewal workflow), SMS (opt‑in reminders)

- Reminders / status updates:
Announcement: T0 when renewal window opens

- Periodic reminders: 30d/14d/7d/1d before renewal deadline

- Post-deadline: outcome notification (Email + In‑app)

Message intent: Action required (complete renewal), Status update
1. Verification sample selection / audit sampling

- DDD element: Event

- Parent / Context: Verification Sampling Service / EligibilityVerificationCase

- Operations / triggers: VerificationSampleSelected, VerificationDocumentationRequested

- Recipients: Selected households, Caseworkers

- Channels: Email (notice + instructions), In‑app (upload UI), SMS (opt‑in deadline reminders)

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Selection notification (Email + In‑app)

- Deadlines: 14d/7d/3d/1d reminders and overdue escalation to staff

Message intent: Action required (submit docs), Status update
1. Disbursement & school payment

- DDD element: Event

- Parent / Context: Disbursements / DisbursementOrder / Payment

- Operations / triggers: DisbursementInitiated, SchoolPaid, PaymentConfirmed

- Recipients: School finance contact, Parent/Guardian (if relevant), Program Finance

- Channels: Email (confirmation + remittance), In‑app (payment ledger), Vendor/EFT channels

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Disbursement notice to school (Email + remittance attachment)

- On confirmation: payment confirmed notification

- Exceptions: alert finance team and affected stakeholders immediately

Message intent: Status update (payment executed), Action required (resolve exception)
1. Tax reporting & year‑end notices

- DDD element: Event / Value object

- Parent / Context: Reporting & Tax / TaxReportEntry

- Operations / triggers: Tax1099GPrepared, YearEndStatementAvailable

- Recipients: Parent/Guardian, Finance/Reporting

- Channels: Email (forms + instructions), In‑app (downloadable forms), Postal/print if legally required

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Year‑end forms available notification

- Follow up: 30d reminder to download/acknowledge

Message intent: Status update (forms), Action required (acknowledge/consult tax advisor)
1. Admin / staff operational alerts

- DDD element: System events / Operational notices

- Parent / Context: Platform / Notice / ToDoItem

- Operations / triggers: IntegrationFailure (RDS, ClassWallet), SLA_Breach, ExceptionQueueItem

- Recipients: Admins, On‑call SRE, Program Managers

- Channels: Email (paged + summary), In‑app staff dashboard, PagerDuty/ops channel for critical incidents

- Reminders / status updates:
T0: Immediate alert to on‑call (PagerDuty)

- Status updates: every 15&ndash;60 minutes depending on severity until resolved

Message intent: Action required (investigate/resolve), Status update (incident progress)
Condensed bullet list (event &mdash; preferred channels / category)
- ApplicationSubmitted &mdash; Email / In‑app (receipt)

- ApplicationStatusChanged (underReview/eligible/ineligible/waitlisted/awarded) &mdash; Email / In‑app (status)

- EligibilityDeterminationSubmitted &mdash; Email / In‑app

- EligibilityDeterminationResultPublished &mdash; Email / In‑app

- DomicileVerificationInitiated &mdash; Email / In‑app

- VerificationDocumentationRequested &mdash; Email / In‑app / SMS opt‑in

- DomicileVerified / DomicileDenied &mdash; Email / In‑app

- LotteryConducted &mdash; Email / In‑app

- AwardOfferMade &mdash; Email / In‑app / SMS opt‑in (deadline)

- AwardAccepted / AwardDeclined &mdash; Email / In‑app

- AwardActivated / ScholarshipAccountCreated / FundsMovedToWallet &mdash; Email / In‑app

- ToDoItemCreated / DeadlineApproaching / ToDoOverdue &mdash; In‑app / Email / SMS opt‑in

- ParentAgreementRequested / ParentAgreementSigned / LEAReleaseSigned &mdash; Email / In‑app / SMS opt‑in

- PurchaseSubmitted / PurchaseApproved / PurchaseRejected &mdash; Email / In‑app

- PaymentProcessed / RefundProcessed / SchoolPaid &mdash; Email / In‑app

- PurchaseValidationFailed (rule violation) &mdash; In‑app / Email (explain remediation)

- ProviderApplied / ProviderEnrolled / ProviderPaymentFailed &mdash; Email / In‑app

- MisuseDetected / ComplianceRecordCreated / AwardTerminated &mdash; Email / In‑app / Phone for urgent escalation

- RenewalOfferSent / RenewalDeadlineApproaching / RenewalCompleted &mdash; Email / In‑app / SMS opt‑in

- VerificationSampleSelected &mdash; Email / In‑app / SMS opt‑in

- Tax1099GPrepared / YearEndStatementAvailable &mdash; Email / In‑app / Postal when required

- IntegrationFailure / SLA_Breach / ExceptionQueueItem &mdash; Email (ops) / PagerDuty / In‑app staff alerts



# Class Wallet Matrix

*Created: 2025-10-16T15:20:10.498Z by Matt Vaughn*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-16T15:29:12.696Z by Matt Vaughn*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4350410805/Class+Wallet+Matrix*

---

Purpose: catalog all the K12 domain events, operations, and workflows that require integration with ClassWallet (or equivalent student-account/wallet service). For each item we list DDD context, aggregate/root, operations/triggers, required API interactions (push/pull), reconciliation and idempotency notes, sandbox/test requirements, failure handling, and recommended notification/actions mapping.
Assumptions
- "ClassWallet" is the external wallet/payment provider used for scholarship fund management (activation, distributions, merchant payments, provider payouts).

- Integration should be implemented as a bounded context in the platform with explicit contracts, retry/backoff, and reconciliation jobs.

Guidance for implementers
- Implement async, event-driven integration via an Integration Service that subscribes to platform events and calls ClassWallet endpoints. Prefer webhooks + outbound signed callbacks for reliability.

- Maintain a local mirror of wallet state (balance, holds, transactions) with periodic reconciliation.

- Record all external transaction IDs and ensure idempotent operations using idempotency keys.

- Provide a sandbox harness to simulate ClassWallet responses, error modes, and latency for contract and load testing.

1. Award activation & wallet provisioning

- DDD element: Event

- Parent / Context: ScholarshipAccount / ScholarshipAccount aggregate

- Operations / triggers: AwardActivated, ScholarshipAccountCreated

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Create Wallet / Account (POST /wallets)

- Data sent: applicantId, accountId, legalName, initialBalance, metadata (awardId, program)

- Response: externalWalletId, status, initialBalance

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Use platform awardId as idempotency key when creating wallets

- Reconcile missing wallets nightly: platform account without externalWalletId → requeue create

Sandbox/test requirements:
- API that simulates success, duplicate, and delayed creation responses; support rate-limit simulation

Failure handling:
- On transient error: retry with exponential backoff

- On permanent failure: mark account for manual review and notify finance/ops

Notification mapping:
- On success: notify parent (Email + In‑app) Wallet created

- On failure: notify ops and caseworker for intervention

1. Funds movement (funds moved to wallet / disbursements)

- DDD element: Command / Event

- Parent / Context: ScholarshipAccount, DisbursementOrder / Payment

- Operations / triggers: FundsMovedToWallet, DisbursementInitiated

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Credit Wallet (POST /wallets/{id}/credits) or Transfer (POST /transfers)

- Data sent: amount, currency, externalReference (disbursementId), metadata

- Response: transactionId, status

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Use disbursementId as idempotency key for credits/transfers

- Reconcile ledger differences daily (platform balance vs ClassWallet ledger)

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Support success/failure, delayed settlements, duplicate transaction simulation

- Simulate chargebacks and refunds

Failure handling:
- On pending settlement: mark transaction pending and notify finance

- On permanent failure: retry per policy, escalate after N attempts

Notification mapping:
- On credit success: notify parent (Email + In‑app balance update) and finance

- On failure: notify ops and affected stakeholders

1. Purchase authorization and merchant payments

- DDD element: Command / Aggregate

- Parent / Context: PurchaseRequest / PurchaseRequest

- Operations / triggers: PurchaseApproved → PaymentProcessed

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Authorize Payment (POST /wallets/{id}/authorize) or Pay Merchant (POST /payments)

- Data sent: purchaseId, walletId, amount, merchantId, orderDetails, idempotencyKey

- Response: authorizationId, paymentId, status (authorized/declined)

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Use purchaseId as idempotency key to prevent double-charges

- Reconcile merchant settlements against purchase records nightly

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Test merchant approval, declines, partial captures, and holds

- Simulate network errors and timeouts

Failure handling:
- On decline: notify user with reason code and remediation steps

- On timeout: mark payment pending and run reconciliation

Notification mapping:
- On authorization success/failure: notify parent and provider (Email + In‑app)

1. Refunds & chargebacks

- DDD element: Command / Event

- Parent / Context: PurchaseRequest / ScholarshipAccount

- Operations / triggers: RefundProcessed, ChargebackReceived

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Refund (POST /payments/{paymentId}/refunds), Query Chargeback (GET /chargebacks/{id})

- Data sent: originalPaymentId, amount, reasonCode

- Response: refundId, status

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Track originalPaymentId; idempotency key = refundRequestId

- Daily reconcile refunds/chargebacks against finance ledger

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Simulate partial/full refunds and chargebacks with different timelines

Failure handling:
- On refund failure: escalate to finance; mark for manual processing

- On chargeback: create compliance case and notify parent + ops

Notification mapping:
- On refund: notify parent (Email + In‑app) and finance

- On chargeback: immediate escalation to compliance and parent notification

1. Provider payouts (batch vendor payments)

- DDD element: Command / Event

- Parent / Context: Provider / Provider aggregate

- Operations / triggers: ProviderPayoutInitiated, ProviderPaymentProcessed, ProviderPaymentFailed

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Initiate Payout (POST /payouts), Retrieve Payout Status (GET /payouts/{id})

- Data sent: providerId, externalProviderAccount, amount, payoutBatchId

- Response: payoutId, status, settlementDate

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Use payoutBatchId + providerId as idempotency keys

- Reconcile payouts with bank remittances and provider confirmations

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Batch payout testing, failure in batch, partial success handling

Failure handling:
- On partial failures: retry individual items; escalate if batch failure

Notification mapping:
- On payout success/failure: notify provider and finance (Email + In‑app)

1. Transaction ledger sync & reconciliation

- DDD element: Scheduled job / Event

- Parent / Context: Financials / ScholarshipAccount

- Operations / triggers: NightlyReconciliation, LedgerMismatchDetected

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Fetch ledger / transactions (GET /wallets/{id}/transactions) with pagination

- Data needed: transactions, balances, holds, pending items

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Implement 3‑way reconciliation: platform ledger, ClassWallet ledger, bank settlement

- Auto-create reconciliation cases for mismatches above threshold

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Large dataset reconciliation tests and simulated eventual consistency windows

Failure handling:
- Create exception cases for finance review; alert ops for systemic mismatches

Notification mapping:
- On mismatch: notify finance (Email + In‑app) and create ticket

1. Webhooks / asynchronous callbacks handling

- DDD element: Integration point / Event

- Parent / Context: Integration Service

- Operations / triggers: ClassWalletWebhookReceived (transactionUpdate, payoutUpdate, disputeUpdate)

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Receive signed webhooks; validate signature, process event

- Data: webhook event type, externalIds, status, metadata

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Validate signature and idempotency using webhookId/externalEventId

- Persist webhook receipt and processing status for audit

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Webhook replay, signature invalidation, duplicate delivery, delayed events

Failure handling:
- Respond 200 only after successful processing; otherwise return 5xx to trigger retries

- Queue failed webhook events for replay after manual fix

Notification mapping:
- Map certain webhook events (chargeback, payout failure) to immediate notifications to finance/compliance

1. Authorization & access control (token lifecycle)

- DDD element: Integration security

- Parent / Context: Integration Service

- Operations / triggers: TokenIssued, TokenRotated, TokenRevoked

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: OAuth2 / API key rotation, JWT validation

- Requirements: secure vault storage, rotation policy, least privilege

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Log token events and alert on failed auth spikes

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Support expired/invalid tokens, key rotation simulation

Failure handling:
- On auth failure: alert ops and halt outbound calls until fixed

Notification mapping:
- Notify ops on repeated auth failures or key compromise

1. Limits, holds, and spend controls

- DDD element: Policy / Rules

- Parent / Context: ScholarshipAccount, Rules Engine

- Operations / triggers: HoldPlaced, SpendLimitReached, LimitLifted

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: PlaceHold (POST /wallets/{id}/holds), ReleaseHold, QueryLimits

- Data: holdId, reason, amount, expiry

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Idempotency by holdRequestId; reconcile holds nightly

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Simulate hold placement, expiration, and conflict scenarios

Failure handling:
- If hold fails: notify originating service and place local hold marker for retry

Notification mapping:
- Notify parent of holds/spend limits (Email + In‑app) and staff if escalation needed

1. Reporting & statement generation

- DDD element: Batch job / Event

- Parent / Context: Reporting / TaxReportEntry, Account Statements

- Operations / triggers: MonthlyStatementReady, TaxReportReady

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Fetch historical transactions and balances for period (GET /wallets/{id}/transactions?from..to)

- Data needed: itemized transactions, settlement records

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Ensure exported data is snapshot-consistent and signed/audited

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Large history export performance tests and data integrity checks

Failure handling:
- If export fails: retry and alert reporting team; mark partial exports clearly

Notification mapping:
- Notify parents and finance when statements/tax reports are available (Email + In‑app)

1. Sandbox & contract testing requirements

- DDD element: Nonfunctional / Testing

- Parent / Context: Integration QA

- Requirements:
Sandbox must support: synchronous responses, delayed settlement, webhooks, duplicate events, rate limits, auth variations, failure modes

- Provide a test matrix for happy-path and known error modes

Notes:
- Integration tests should run in CI against the sandbox with contract checks

1. Operational & monitoring requirements

- DDD element: Monitoring / Observability

- Parent / Context: Integration Service / Platform

- Requirements:
Instrument all calls (latency, status codes, error types), track SLOs for outbound integrations, alert on increased error rate or reconciliation failures

- Provide dashboards for balances, pending items, and webhook health

Notification mapping:
- On sustained failure rates or reconciliation anomalies: alert SRE and Finance (PagerDuty + Email)

1. Legal / compliance & audit trails

- DDD element: Compliance

- Parent / Context: Finance / Legal

- Requirements:
Persist all external transaction IDs and signed webhook payloads; store for legally required retention period

- Provide exportable audit trails for each ScholarshipAccount and DisbursementOrder

Notification mapping:
- Notify Legal/Finance on audit requests or data exports

1. General Ledger (GL) posting & export

- DDD element: Accounting / Event

- Parent / Context: Financials / ScholarshipAccount, DisbursementOrder, ProviderPayout

- Operations / triggers: GL_PostRequired (on disbursement, payout, refund), GL_Adjustment

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Fetch settled transactions and settlement details to derive GL entries (GET /wallets/{id}/transactions?settled=true)

- Data needed: settled transaction rows, settlement dates, clearing references, fees

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Use platform transactionId + externalTransactionId as composite key for GL entry idempotency

- Maintain GL export sequence numbers; avoid duplicate posting by tracking export batches

- Implement periodic GL vs ledger reconciliation and auto-create adjustment entries for rounding/fee differences

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Provide settled transaction exports, fee breakdowns, and partial settlement simulations for testing GL posting logic

Failure handling:
- If GL export fails: pause downstream posting, alert Finance, and queue retry with audit trail

- For mismatched records: auto-create exceptions for manual accounting adjustment and track adjustments as separate GL entries

Notification mapping:
- Notify Finance when GL export completes/fails and provide downloadable export (CSV/ISO20022/ERP import format)

1. DNPE (Do‑Not‑Pay / Exclusion) checks

- DDD element: Compliance / Policy

- Parent / Context: Provider Payouts / Provider, ScholarshipAccount / ScholarshipAccount

- Operations / triggers: Before ProviderPayoutInitiated or before Credit Wallet/Transfer to external account

- ClassWallet interactions:
API: Optionally call DNPE check service or include provider status in provider metadata when initiating payouts

- Data sent/required: providerTIN, providerName, providerBankAccount, payoutBatchId

Reconciliation / idempotency:
- Record DNPE check result with providerId and payoutBatchId to prevent repeated payouts to excluded entities

- Track appeal/clearance state so that blocked providers are not retried until cleared

Sandbox/test requirements:
- Simulate DNPE match, partial matches, and false positives in sandbox; support delayed clearance responses

Failure handling:
- On DNPE match: block payout, create compliance case, notify Finance and Legal, and surface remediation steps to ops

- On DNPE service failure: fail-safe to hold payout and queue DNPE check retries; alert ops if persistent

Notification mapping:
- Immediate notification to Finance/Legal on DNPE match with relevant provider details; notify provider only per legal guidance

Condensed bullet list (integration event &mdash; ClassWallet action / category)
- AwardActivated / ScholarshipAccountCreated &mdash; Create Wallet (POST /wallets) / Provisioning

- FundsMovedToWallet / DisbursementInitiated &mdash; Credit Wallet / Funds Movement

- PurchaseApproved → PaymentProcessed &mdash; Authorize/Pay Merchant / Payment

- RefundProcessed / ChargebackReceived &mdash; Refund / Chargeback Handling

- ProviderPayoutInitiated &mdash; Initiate Payout (batch) / Provider Payments

- NightlyReconciliation &mdash; Fetch transactions / Reconciliation

- ClassWalletWebhookReceived &mdash; Handle webhook (transaction/payout/dispute) / Webhooks

- TokenIssued/Rotated &mdash; Manage auth tokens / Security

- HoldPlaced / SpendLimitReached &mdash; PlaceHold/ReleaseHold / Spend Controls

- MonthlyStatementReady / TaxReportReady &mdash; Fetch historical transactions / Reporting



# General Ledger (GL) Integration Matrix

*Created: 2025-10-16T15:45:36.541Z by Matt Vaughn*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-16T15:47:10.653Z by Matt Vaughn*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4352311302/General+Ledger+GL+Integration+Matrix*

---

Purpose: define how platform financial events map to General Ledger entries, export formats, sequencing, idempotency rules, reconciliation processes, exception handling, and retention/audit expectations for the K12 program. This document is organized like communication-list.md and class-wallet-list.md to be directly usable by finance, integration, and ops teams.
Assumptions
- The GL is managed in an external ERP/accounting system that accepts periodic batch imports (CSV, ISO20022, or API). The platform must produce deterministic, auditable exports mapped to GL chart of accounts.

- Core financial events originate from platform aggregates: ScholarshipAccount, DisbursementOrder, PurchaseRequest, ProviderPayout, Refund, Chargeback.

Guidance for implementers
- Each export row must include: platformTransactionId, externalTransactionId (ClassWallet or bank), eventType, amount, currency, debitAccount, creditAccount, description/memo, settlementDate, exportBatchId, sequenceNumber, and checksum.

- Implement export batching with sequence numbering and idempotent imports (do not re-post the same sequenceNumber twice).

- Implement a reconciliation pipeline that performs 3‑way matching between platform ledger, ClassWallet ledger, and bank/ERP postings.

1. GL mapping: Disbursements

- DDD element: Event / Accounting Entry

- Parent / Context: Disbursement / DisbursementOrder

- Trigger: DisbursementInitiated, DisbursementSettled

- GL entries to create:
Debit: Program Expense (expense account e.g. 6xxxx)

- Credit: Cash/Bank Clearing (liability) or Payable (2xxxx)

- Fee entries: separate GL lines for processing fees (expense) and clearing differences

Export behavior:
- Export on settlement (settlementDate); include externalTransactionId and remittance reference

- Batch with exportBatchId and per-row sequenceNumber

Idempotency & sequencing:
- Use composite id: exportBatchId + sequenceNumber; record postedBatchId to prevent duplicates

Reconciliation:
- Match disbursement records to bank statements and ClassWallet settled transactions; flag mismatches > threshold

Notification mapping:
- Notify Finance on batch completion with downloadable export and summary metrics

1. GL mapping: Wallet credits (funds moved to wallet)

- DDD element: Event / Accounting Entry

- Parent / Context: ScholarshipAccount / ScholarshipAccount

- Trigger: FundsMovedToWallet (funding events)

- GL entries to create:
Debit: Cash/Bank (asset) or Funding Source

- Credit: Deferred Revenue / Wallet Liability (liability) until spent

Export behavior:
- Export on settled funding confirmation from ClassWallet

Idempotency & sequencing:
- Use fundingTransactionId as idempotency key; ensure single GL credit per funding

Reconciliation:
- Match funding records to ClassWallet credits and banking settlements

1. GL mapping: Purchases & merchant payments

- DDD element: Event / Accounting Entry

- Parent / Context: PurchaseRequest / PurchaseRequest

- Trigger: PurchaseApproved, PaymentProcessed

- GL entries to create:
Debit: Expense (specific program or merchant category)

- Credit: Wallet Liability / Cash Clearing

- On merchant fee: separate expense entry

Export behavior:
- Export when payment is settled/captured

Idempotency & sequencing:
- Use purchaseId + paymentId composite for idempotency

Reconciliation:
- Match to merchant settlement files and platform records; flag partial captures

1. GL mapping: Refunds & chargebacks

- DDD element: Event / Accounting Entry

- Parent / Context: PurchaseRequest / ScholarshipAccount

- Trigger: RefundProcessed, ChargebackReceived

- GL entries to create:
Debit: Wallet Liability / Cash Clearing (reverse original credit)

- Credit: Revenue Reversal or Expense Reversal (depends on original entry)

- Record chargeback fees as expense

Export behavior:
- Export on refund or chargeback settlement

Idempotency & sequencing:
- Track refundRequestId and originalPaymentId for idempotency

Reconciliation:
- Match refunds/chargebacks to bank/processor notices and create adjustment cases if amounts differ

1. GL mapping: Provider payouts and vendor payments

- DDD element: Batch Event / Accounting Entry

- Parent / Context: ProviderPayout / Provider

- Trigger: ProviderPayoutInitiated, ProviderPaymentProcessed

- GL entries to create:
Debit: Relevant Expense or Payable Clearing

- Credit: Bank/Cash Clearing

- Fees: expense lines for remittance fees

Export behavior:
- Export per payout batch with per-provider detail lines; include payoutBatchId

Idempotency & sequencing:
- Use payoutBatchId + providerId sequence numbers; log external payoutId

Reconciliation:
- Reconcile payouts with bank remittances and provider confirmations. Create exceptions for partial / failed items

1. GL mapping: Fees & processing costs

- DDD element: Event / Accounting Entry

- Parent / Context: Various (payments, payouts)

- Trigger: FeeCharged, FeeSettled

- GL entries to create:
Debit: Processing Fees (expense)

- Credit: Bank/Cash Clearing (or liability if pre-funded)

Export behavior:
- Aggregate fees per period or include per-transaction lines as required by finance

Reconciliation:
- Aggregate and reconcile processor fee statements vs platform fee calculations

1. GL export formats & ERP integration

- Requirements:
Support CSV with clearly defined headers, sequence numbers, and checksums

- Support ISO20022 or ERP-specific API payloads where required

- Include mapping table: platform event types → GL account codes

Security & retention:
- Sign and store export batches; retain per legal retention policy

1. Reconciliation & exception handling

- Requirements:
Nightly 3‑way reconciliation: platform ledger, ClassWallet ledger, bank/ERP statements

- Auto-open exception cases for mismatches beyond thresholds; track lifecycle of exceptions

- Provide audit trail linking GL entries to original platform events and externalTransactionIds

Notifications:
- Alert Finance on reconciliation failures and provide drill-down report

1. Testing, sandbox & performance

- Requirements:
Sandbox exports with sample settled transactions and bank remittance files

- Load test large export batches and reconciliation with large datasets

- Contract tests for ERP import compatibility

1. DNPE & compliance controls (reference)

- Requirements:
For provider payout entries, include DNPE check status in export metadata

- Block GL posting for payouts pending DNPE clearance if required by policy

1. Audit trail & retention

- Requirements:
Persist exportBatchId, sequenceNumbers, checksums, signatures, and link to ScholarshipAccount/DisbursementOrder

- Retain signed webhook payloads, reconciliation reports, and manual adjustment records per legal retention rules

Condensed bullet list (event → GL action / account categories)
- DisbursementSettled &mdash; Debit: Expense (Program) / Credit: Cash Clearing

- FundsMovedToWallet (settled) &mdash; Debit: Cash / Credit: Wallet Liability (Deferred Revenue)

- PurchasePaymentSettled &mdash; Debit: Expense / Credit: Wallet Liability

- RefundSettled / ChargebackSettled &mdash; Debit: Wallet Liability / Credit: Revenue Reversal

- ProviderPayoutSettled &mdash; Debit: Expense/Payable Clearing / Credit: Cash

- FeeCharged &mdash; Debit: Processing Fees / Credit: Cash



# Standards and Practice

*Created: 2025-10-17T14:42:48.098Z by Matt Vaughn*
*Last Updated: 2025-11-03T19:54:49.041Z by Thomas Franey*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4353228809/Standards+and+Practice*

---

For the Front End,
- Alphabetize order for Imports, ViewChild, Inputs, Outputs, Variables

- Variables are grouped as Public/ Private, alphabetized. 

- Methods declared as public/private with return type to the methods, followed by public then private methods

- Subscribe for api calls

- @for, @if, @switch for the loops and conditions

- Add data-qa for buttons

- In Html, arrange classes, inputs, outputs and alphabetize.

- Remove Primeng classes, components

- In css, alphabetize, use variables for fonts and colors.

- Add angular material styles for the component in the shared_assets/css/material_overrides

- Add Inject for service

- Alphabetize Models, Interfaces.

- Format the file, remove unwanted spaces



# RDS Integration Solution Design (K12 Residency Validation)

*Created: 2025-10-24T17:16:44.498Z by Matt Vaughn*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-24T17:27:01.991Z by Matt Vaughn*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4375904312/RDS+Integration+Solution+Design+K12+Residency+Validation*

---

## RDS Integration Solution Design (K12 Residency Validation)
| Document Status | Version | Last Updated | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DRAFT / CONFIRMED ARCHITECTURE | 1.0 | 2025-10-XX | K12 System Architect / Product Owner |
### Executive Summary: K12 Residency Validation Integration
This document summarizes the proposed asynchronous integration between the K12 application (MyPortal K12) and the Residency Determination Service (RDS), managed by CFI. The primary mechanism for communication is Azure Service Bus, utilizing queues hosted within the K12 Azure environment.
The critical architectural decision is that RDS will process requests and retrieve data from state agencies (DOR and DMV), but RDS will return the raw agency evaluation data within the response payload. The K12 system will be the authoritative source of truth for residency determination, applying K12-specific business rules and logic to the raw data provided by RDS.
The process is asynchronous, typically taking 24&ndash;36 hours to send a response back, based on the DOR and DMV file processing schedules (daily).
### 1. Solution Summary and Architecture
#### 1.a. Integration Flow and Key Components
The K12 Residency Validation service is part of the overall RDS validation service intended to validate K12 applicant residency.
Data Flow:
1. K12 Request: The K12 application creates a request payload containing household member data (e.g., described in Request payload documentation). K12 puts a message for residency validation data into an Azure Service Bus Queue (hosted in the K12 Azure environment). RDS will provide an acknowledgement response for the request.

2. RDS Processing: The RDS validation service picks up the message from the K12 Azure queue to process the request. The RDS system then validates residency using DOR (tax filings, daily at 7:30 PM EST) and DMV (Driver's Licence evaluation and Vehicle Registration, daily at 7:00 AM EST).

3. RDS Response: When responses return from DOR and DMV, RDS processes them and pushes the result back into a K12 Azure Service Bus Queue. RDS will send the raw data from each agency evaluation (DOR and DMV) as part of the overall response payload, which includes detailed tracking information. The response structure also includes a completionStatus and any expand/expando data for correlation.

4. K12 Determination: K12 processes the residency response, runs its own residency rules (based on input from SEAA) against the raw agency data, and performs a rollup to determine residency (validationResult = Y/N). K12 is the source of truth for the final residency determination decision.

Key Integration Technology:
- Messaging: Azure Service Bus is used for reliable asynchronous messaging, including message queues and dead letter queues.

- K12 Platform: The target system is a cloud-native, microservices-based architecture hosted on Microsoft Azure, utilizing services like Azure App Service, Azure Functions, Azure SQL Database, and Azure Key Vault.

#### 1.b. Environment(s) for Solution
The integration components leverage the K12 Azure environment. RDS requires connectivity to the K12 queues across all relevant environments.
| Environment Type | Purpose/Usage | Location |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Development/CI/CD | Developer testing, automated testing | K12 Azure Environment / Sandbox |
| Staging/UAT | User acceptance testing | K12 Azure Environment |
| Production | Live operations | K12 Azure Environment |
For each environment, K12 must provide RDS (CFI) with the necessary connection information and credentials to access the queues.
### 2. Define Security Handshake
The security model focuses on providing RDS (as an external client) secure access to the K12-owned Azure Service Bus queues.
1. Secure Connection Requirement: A secure connection to the Azure Service Bus is mandatory.

2. Credential Management: K12 needs to provide RDS with Connection Information (credentials, etc.) for each environment to enable RDS to connect to the K12 message queue. These credentials (API keys, connection strings) will typically be managed securely by the K12 system using Azure Key Vault.

3. Access Control: RDS will be a client coming in to pick up and drop off messages in the K12 Azure environment. K12 may need to open up firewalls to allow this access.

4. Standard Security Protocols: The underlying K12 architecture utilizes robust security features including Azure Active Directory (Entra ID) and Azure Private Link for network isolation. The final technical specification will need to confirm the exact authentication mechanism used for the Service Bus connection, which may be API keys or Mutual TLS (mTLS).

5. Auditability: Security procedures must ensure a full audit trail for all residency determinations and API calls for compliance.

### 3. When/Schedule for Solution
The integration is a multi-phased project tracked across multiple Program Increments (PIs).
| Project Epic/Version | Status | Target Fix Versions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Enabler and RDS Integration ([K12-2964]) | Ready for BA | PI 2, PI 3 |
| RDS Integration ([K12-3844]) | Open (Implementation Epic) | PI 4 |
Integration Timeline Milestones (Draft Roadmap):
The comprehensive integration roadmap suggests the following sequence, assuming the discovery and research were completed around October 2025:
| Phase | Activities/Target |
| --- | --- |
| Phase 0: Discovery & Planning | Schedule meeting, review MOUs, finalize architecture. |
| Phase 1: Proof of Concept (PoC) | Set up sandbox access, implement basic authentication, test case creation/query. |
| Phase 2: Adapter Development | Build RDS Adapter microservice, implement retry/circuit breaker, add caching layer. |
| Phase 3: Integration Testing | End-to-end testing, load testing, security penetration testing, UAT. |
| Phase 4: Pilot Deployment | Deploy to production with feature flag (10% traffic). |
| Phase 5: Full Rollout | Enable integration for 100% of applications, establish ongoing monitoring. |
Operational Timeline:
- Agency Processing: DMV runs daily at 7:00 AM EST, and DOR runs daily at 7:30 PM EST.

- Determination Time: The process is asynchronous and normally takes 24&ndash;36 hours to send a response back.

- Peak Volume: The biggest volume of residency determination requests is expected during the application period (February&ndash;April).

### 4. Data Retention Requirements (Needs Discussion)
The current plan requires more focused discussion and definition regarding data retention, as this is currently a critical unknown and a high-priority topic.
Current Status and Requirements:
- Critical Unknown: Need more information, requirements, and rules about data retention for residency information.

- Data Classification: The data exchanged includes PII (SSN, name, address, DOB) and is considered HIGH sensitivity.

- Audit Requirement: All RDS and K12 residency operations, including the raw data received from DMV and DOR, must be included in the application audit log system for full traceability and external auditing.

- Statutory Framework: The K12 team needs to determine the applicability of retention policies. Residency verification falls under G.S. 115C-562.3, which mandates electronic verification across state agencies.

- Action Items: Jill/PVG were assigned to retrieve the next level of requirements/retention policies from SEAA. The technical specification template currently leaves audit log retention (_____ years) and data retention (_____ years) as [TO BE DETERMINED].

Needed Discussion Points:
The team must convene a focused meeting to finalize retention rules, specifically covering:
1. K12 Source of Truth Data: How long must K12 retain the final residency determination, and must this align with FERPA guidelines for student records?

2. Raw Agency Data Retention: Since RDS returns the raw data from DOR/DMV evaluations, what is the required retention period for this high-sensitivity PII?

3. Audit Log Retention: What is the mandated retention period for the comprehensive audit log detailing every stage of the validation process (request, responses, final decision, appeals)?

4. Data Deletion: Are there "right to be forgotten" or data minimization requirements that dictate when and how archived or purged cases must be handled?

5. Agency MOUs: Confirm data retention obligations stemming from the underlying data sharing agreements RDS has with DOR and DMV.



# K12-Web-Enrollment

*Created: 2025-10-24T20:48:56.342Z by Vincent ch*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-24T21:11:30.593Z by Vincent ch*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4374790184/K12-Web-Enrollment*

---

# k12-web-enrollment &mdash; Developer Documentation
Purpose
- Front-end for secure, role-based access to educational resources and enrollment workflows.

- Built with Angular + TypeScript. Uses RxJS, Angular Material/PrimeNG, Luxon, MSAL (Azure AD), SignalR.

- Integrates with backend enrollment/program APIs, Azure authentication, and CI/CD pipelines (Azure).

This document is formatted for printing and distribution to developers. It consolidates architecture, key concepts, component/service references, best practices, testing, deployment, and troubleshooting.
## Table of contents
1. Introduction

2. Directory structure (high level)

3. Key concepts & patterns

4. Important components & services (summary)

5. Coding practices & tools

6. Testing strategy

7. Development & deployment

8. Common issues & troubleshooting

9. Contributing

## 1. Introduction
k12-web-enrollment is the Angular front-end suite for enrollment and program authoring. It supports multiple apps (admin, household/enrollment, providers, schools) and a shared library for reusable UI, models and services. The front-end manages secure flows, prompt-driven enrollment sessions, and role-based admin authoring.
Primary integrations:
- Backend Enrollment & Program APIs (configured per-environment).

- Azure AD via MSAL for authentication and role-based guards.

- Real-time notifications via SignalR for admin workflows.

- CI/CD using Azure Pipelines / Static Web Apps; secrets injected via Key Vault.

## 2. Directory structure (high level)
- projects/
admin/ &mdash; Admin app (program authoring, task queue, message center)

- household/ &mdash; Public enrollment flows (household-facing)

- providers/, schools/ &mdash; Additional apps

- shared/ &mdash; Buildable library: shared components, models, services, pipes, utils

src/environments/ within each app &mdash; environment.{development,testing,production}.ts
docs/ &mdash; printable documentation (this file)
azure-*.yml &mdash; CI/CD pipeline definitions
README.md &mdash; repository-level README
Important folders inside each app:
- src/app/pages &mdash; feature pages and major route targets

- src/app/components &mdash; UI components local to the app

- src/app/services &mdash; HTTP/api clients, business logic services

- src/assets &mdash; static assets, mock data

Organization patterns:
- Shared UI and models live in projects/shared and are consumed by apps via the public API export.

- Feature-first layout: pages -> components -> services.

- Emphasis on small, reusable components and clear separation of presentation (templates) and logic (component class / services).

## 3. Key concepts & patterns
Component-based design
- Small, focused components (e.g., EnrollmentRenderer, PromptRenderer) that compose into pages.

- Prefer Inputs/Outputs and Observables for communication.

Angular modules & standalone patterns
- Use component-level imports (imports: [CommonModule, ReactiveFormsModule, ...]) to keep components portable. Feature modules load app-level providers.

Dependency injection
- Services injected via Angular DI. Use providedIn or module providers as appropriate.

Routing & guards
- Lazy-loaded routes for feature modules. MSAL-based guards protect authenticated routes.

- Route data used to configure features and breadcrumbs.

Reactive forms
- Enrollment prompts rendered as dynamic Reactive Forms (FormGroup / FormArray). Validators applied programmatically.

Observables & RxJS
- Services return Observables from HttpClient. Components subscribe with pipe operators and cleanup with takeUntil or async pipe.

Error handling
- Services bubble errors; components convert into user-friendly messages using a toast/message service. Use centralized HTTP error mapping in shared utils.

Dynamic prompt rendering
- Prompt model drives the UI: renderer builds FormGroup tree, maps to SubmitPrompt DTO, and emits back/submit events.

## 4. Important components & services (summary)
EnrollmentComponent (household)
- Purpose: orchestrates enrollment session, fetches prompts, navigates through flow, submits final enrollment.

- Integrates: household PromptService, shared EnrollmentRendererComponent.

- Example pattern:
On init: call promptService.getNewEnrollment(token) to start session.

- Subscribe with takeUntil(this.destroy$) and present errors via toast.

PromptService (household)
- Role: client wrapper for enrollment endpoints used by household flows.

- Key methods:
getNewEnrollment(token)

- submitPrompt(sessionId, promptPayload)

- getReviewPrompt(sessionId)

- submitEnrollment(sessionId)

- getSessionPromptByToken(sessionId, promptToken)

Best practices: map HTTP errors, retry on transient failures where appropriate, keep methods small and focused.
PromptService (admin)
- Role: authoring calls (create/update/delete prompts), simulate enrollments, manage program tokens.

- Uses Admin-side auth tokens and program-level endpoints.

EnrollmentRendererComponent (shared)
- Role: build Reactive Forms from Prompt model, validate, emit mapped SubmitPrompt payload.

- Key patterns:
createFormGroup() iterates PromptComponent and PromptInput to build nested controls.

- formatSubmitPromptData() converts form values to API payload.

PromptRendererComponent (shared)
- Role: dynamically render input components (binary, checkbox, form-field, file-upload, review) and wire events.

ProgramService (admin)
- Role: program-level CRUD and visualization helpers for program graph/tree, version history, archive/restore.

Shared utilities & services
- ToastMessageService &mdash; centralized user notifications.

- AuthTokenService &mdash; token handling for admin workflows.

- Shared models in projects/shared/src/lib/shared_models.

## 5. Coding practices & tools
Style & naming
- Components: PascalCase with suffix Component (e.g., EnrollmentComponent).

- Services: PascalCase with suffix Service (e.g., PromptService).

- Files: kebab-case filenames (enrollment-form.component.ts).

Separation of concerns
- Keep templates minimal&mdash;avoid embedding logic in templates. Place business logic in component classes or services.

Async patterns
- Prefer Observables with proper unsubscription. Use async pipe in templates when possible.

- Use async/await only for Promise-based logic; do not await Observables directly.

Error handling
- Handle HTTP errors in components and show friendly messages. Log and provide context for telemetry.

Linting & formatting
- ESLint + Prettier configured at repo root. Run linting before commits:
npm run lint (project may have task aliases)

Editor config (.editorconfig) and Prettier exist to standardize styles.
Documentation & comments
- Comment non-obvious algorithms and data transformations (e.g., submit payload formatting).

- Add unit tests for edge cases and document expected Prompt model shape in shared mocks.

## 6. Testing strategy
Unit tests
- Frameworks: Jasmine + Karma (default) for components and services.

- Tests live beside code in *.spec.ts files.

- Use HttpClientTestingModule to mock HTTP in service tests.

- Mock services with jasmine.createSpyObj for dependencies.

Integration tests
- Component-level integration tests that mount renderer components with realistic Prompt model mock.

- Test dynamic form building, validation, and mapping to submit payloads.

Recommended coverage
- Prioritize services and renderers for high coverage.

- Cover success / failure / validation / network error flows.

Sample test pattern (conceptual):
- Arrange: create component with TestBed, provide mocks for PromptService and ToastMessageService.

- Act: trigger component init and simulate service responses.

- Assert: ensure form built correctly and submit triggers correct service calls.

## 7. Development & deployment
Local setup (Windows)
Install dependencies:
- npm install

Build shared library:
- npm run build:shared

- or ng build shared

Serve an app (examples):
- npm run start:admin

- npm run start:enrollment

- or ng serve --project=admin --port=4200

Configure environment files:
- Edit projects//src/environments/.ts to set API URLs and MSAL client IDs for local testing.

Testing
- Run unit tests:
ng test --project=household

- or npm run test:household (if script exists)

CI/CD
- Azure pipelines files at repo root (azure-enrollment-*.yml) perform:
Build shared library

- Build project (production/testing)

- Replace secrets (MSAL client IDs) using Key Vault and sed steps

- Publish dist/<app>/browser to static hosting

Production notes
- Ensure environment.production.ts contains production API endpoints and MSAL configuration.

- Secrets should be injected at pipeline time &mdash; do not store secrets in repo.

## 8. Common issues & troubleshooting
Shared library changes not visible
- Rebuild shared and restart dev server: ng build shared && ng serve --project=<app>.

Missing MSAL or API keys
- Check environment.*.ts in project and pipeline secret replacement tasks.

Memory leaks (subscriptions)
- Use takeUntil/destroy$ in components or async pipe. Search for lingering subscriptions in components.

Dynamic prompt rendering errors
- Verify prompt model shape: unique keyPath values, input identifiers, and expected Prompt schema. Compare with mock in projects/shared/src/lib/shared_assets/mocks/.

HTTP error debugging
- Inspect network tab and API request payloads. Ensure auth header (MSAL) is present for protected endpoints.

## 9. Contributing
- Follow the repo's branching strategy and commit conventions.

- Run linting and tests before opening PR.

- Document changes to the Prompt model or public APIs in docs and update shared mocks/tests.

- For large shared changes, coordinate with dependent app owners &mdash; shared lib rebuilds may be required.

Appendix &mdash; Quick examples
Reactive form creation (pattern):
typescriptHTTP service example (pattern):
typescript {
  return this.http.get(`${environment.adminApiUrl}${environment.enrollmentUrl}/enrollment/${token}/newenrollment`);
}]]>
