| Endorsement | - School Endorsement &ndash; Schools certify that the amount of tuition and fees to be paid on behalf of the student is correct and corresponds with the submitted tuition and fee schedule. Schools endorse funding each semester; once for fall funding and once for spring funding.
- Parent Endorsement &ndash; Once the school has endorsed the award amount, the parent certifies that the amount of tuition and fees to be paid to the school on behalf of the student is correct. Parents endorse funding each semester; once for fall funding and once for spring funding.
- KEY: Endorsement = student enrolled and attending
Students and Parents endorse each semester, for each student by each program. | KM |  |
| Enrollment | Providers ENROLL to get into the system.  (avoid saying &ldquo;approve&rdquo;) | KM |  |
| Enrollment Verification Form | ESA+ uses this electronic form to understand the co-enrollment for any students not full-time. There is a reduced award for ESA+ students co-enrolled in public school. | KM |  |
| ESA+ Provider | Individuals or facilities that have enrolled with SEAA to provide services to ESA+ families. These include:1. tutoring or teaching services and educational therapies (providers for services), and 
2. companies that sell curriculum (providers for products).
Note that ESA+ Providers do not charge tuition. Only private schools (registered with DNPE) charge tuition. Providers can charge for &ldquo;tutoring and teaching services&rdquo;. Note also that the curriculum providers are being phased out. This category will no longer exist after summer 2026. | KM |  |
| Facility Provider | ESA+ providers enroll individually, or as a facility. Specific policies apply when determining the eligibility of an entity to be considered as a facility. | KM |  |
| Financial Review | The K12 programs require all nonpublic schools that enroll 70 or more students who use scholarship funds for at least one semester during the academic year to contract with a Certified Public Accountant (CPA) to perform a financial review. | KM |  |
| Financial Review Threshold | The number of students enrolled in an eligible school in a semester required to trigger a financial review (i.e., 70 or more scholarship students in a semester). | KM |  |
| Fiscal Year | Dates determined by the eligible school for the purposes of accounting. The financial review, if required, is due 4 months after the school&rsquo;s fiscal year ends. | KM |  |
| Graduation Roster | Schools are required to report graduation data for all high school seniors who received K12 Program funds. | KM |  |
| Home School | Students educated in a home school could be eligible for the ESA+ Scholarship. We avoid using home school as an adjective or a verb. We don&rsquo;t use a hyphen. | KM |  |
| Income Calculator | Interactive online calculator to help Opportunity Scholarship families anticipate their Award Tier | KM |  |
| Income Verification | K12 staff verify the income reported by Opportunity Scholarship parents in Award Tiers 1, 2, or 3. (6% required to be verified) | KM |  |
| Income Verification Worksheet | Intake form where Opportunity Scholarhsip parent provides household and income details to support the reported household size and income. | KM |  |
| Individual Cost | Amount the student owes for the entire school year after applying any discount and/or accounting for other financial aid (not including K12 Program funding) the student will receive | KM |  |
| Initial Eligibility | Parents of prospective new ESA+ students must provide documentation of a disability as part of the new student application. This is similar documentation but not exactly the same requirement as Continuing Eligibility. | KM |  |
| Institutional Aid | Discounts or waivers in a student's tuition rate that are determined by an individual student&rsquo;s specific relation to the school. This includes but is not limited to grandfathered rates, tiered rates, faculty discounts, and sibling discounts. | KM |  |
| IRS Tax Return Transcript | Document required of most income-earning household members (when the household income is being verified for the Opportunity Scholarship) | KM |  |
| K12 Operations Team | Staff that help administer the Opportunity Scholarship and ESA+ Programs and are housed in SEAA&rsquo;s Division of K12 Programs. | KM |  |
| Lottery | Students whose applications are received in the Priority Application Period are assigned a randomly generated number. For Opportunity, students are offered an award by lottery number, starting with Award Tier 1, then 2, etc. to the extent funds remain available. | KM |  |
| Marketplace | ClassWallet term for the closed online shopping space where ESA+ parents shop, and where the transaction is reviewed before it completes. | KM |  |
| MyPortal | The current K12 Programs database, which is one word, written with a capital M and a capital P. This is the database that will be replaced by the new system which will be called MyPortal K12. (Note that the current MyPortal will continue to serve SEAA&rsquo;s Higher Ed and Repayment Services.) | KM |  |
| Nonpublic School | We use the term nonpublic as all one word. The term refers to a private or home school, as determined by the North Carolina Division of Non-Public Education, which is a separate state agency that uses the hyphenated version of Non-Public. At SEAA, this term can be abbreviated internally as NPS, and at SEAA, NPS typically refers to the private school (and not a home school). | KM |  |
| Opportunity Scholarship Program | The official name of the program. Use for all references (no abbreviations available). | KM |  |
| Out of District Public School (OoD) | ESA+ will reimburse parents of students who are enrolled in a public school district other than the district to which they are assigned, in cases where the Out of District school charges tuition. | KM |  |
| Parent Agreement | The ESA+ Program requires parents to sign a Parent Agreement each year. | KM |  |
| Provider Support Specialist | SEAA K12 staff who works on the Provider Support team. |  |  |
| Refund | Refers nearly always to schools that are required to return scholarship funds to SEAA. Typically this is because a student has withdrawn, but it may also be as a result of a school correcting its costs. | KM |  |
| Registration | Schools REGISTER to receive funds from K12 scholarships. The school registration is open from late January through June 15 each year. | KM |  |
| Reimbursement School | Schools that choose not to receive ESA+ funds directly from SEAA. Families who enroll their students in these schools pay tuition and fees out of pocket and are reimbursed by ESA+. These schools must be registered with DNPE (although they are opting not to additionally register with SEAA). | KM |  |
| Reimbursement | ESA+ is not a reimbursement program. Families cannot routinely purchase items or pay for services outside the ClassWallet platform and request reimbursement. However, there are a few narrow exceptions where ESA+ will reimburse ESA+ parents. | KM |  |
| Renew / Renewal | Current families renew their student&rsquo;s scholarship each year. Returning students are &ldquo;renewals&rdquo;. SEAA does not use the words &ldquo;apply&rdquo; or &ldquo;application&rdquo; in reference to renewing students. This distinguishes the &ldquo;new&rdquo; process from the &ldquo;renewal&rdquo; process. | KM |  |
| Required Fees | Costs schools may report along with tuition; costs required of all students such as books, technology, or curriculum. Note that &ldquo;academic&rdquo; as a descriptor of fees is removed. Fees must simply be required of all students. | KM |  |
| Residency | Students must live in North Carolina to benefit from the Opportunity Scholarship and ESA+ Programs. Note that (a) the residency requirement for the K12 Programs mirror the NC public school requirement, and (b) the K12 requirement is far less stringent than NC&rsquo;s residency requirement for in-state tuition for college. | KM |  |
| Scholarship | A scholarship awarded under the K12 Programs. | KM |  |
| Scholarship Specialist | SEAA K12 staff who work on the ESA+ / Opportunity Scholarship Support team. | KM |  |
| School Support Specialist | SEAA K12 staff who works on the School Support team. |  |  |
| School Year | Two semesters, beginning with the fall semester. | KM |  |
| Special Education | Specially designed instruction to meet the needs of a child with a disability. | KM |  |
| Standard Cost | Required tuition and fees a nonpublic school charges students generally, per grade level, per curriculum track, and per school year | KM |  |
| State | The State of North Carolina. | KM |  |
| Student Transfer | Refers to the process with families; a student benefits from scholarship funding at one school but then leaves during the school year to attend a different school. | KM |  |
| Supplemental Services | ESA+ funds can be used for tutoring, therapies, and transportation, and these services may be offered by schools (schools thus functioning as ESA+ Providers). Schools registered with DNPE (including those also registered with SEAA) can offer Supplemental Services in addition to receiving disbursements for required tuition and fees. | KM |  |
| Task | Action required (e.g. document submission) |  |  |
| Testing and Reporting | Direct Payment School requirement: administer a nationally standardized test to all scholarship students 3rd grade and higher (ACT specifically required for 11th graders); submit a copy of student test results to SEAA by July 15, and an aggregate report per the aggregate requirements. | KM |  |
| Transaction Review | The process of approving or rejecting ESA+ parent requests to purchase products or pay for services. Transaction Counselors are employed by College Foundation, Inc. and are the primary staff responsible for this review. The K12 team does not distinguish CFI vs SEAA to external stakeholders. Everyone is part of the K12 team. | KM |  |
| Tuition and Fee Schedule | An itemized statement from a nonpublic school submitted to SEAA that sets forth the tuition, fees, expenses, costs, discounts, and financial aid (other than the K12 program scholarships) that are needed to calculate the total cost of tuition for the school year for an individual student. Due annually by July 15 for the upcoming school year. | KM |  |
| User Type/User | Term to refer the the combined groups interacting with K12 (e.g. Schools, Households, Providers) |  |  |
| Vendor Master Form | Form required for any entity doing business with the UNC System Office (the UNC System Office processes payments on behalf of SEAA) | KM |  |
| W9 | The ESA+ Program requires parents to sign a W9 (acknowledging the possibility of a tax liability) each year. | KM |  |
| Wallet | Refers to the ESA+ account currently provided by the vendor ClassWallet. SEAA prefers not to have the vendor&rsquo;s exact name implemented permanently as part of MyPortal K12. | KM |  |
| Withdraw / Withdrawal | Refers to the process with schools; a student stops attending at any point during the school year. Depending on the timing the school may have to return funds. A student that changes school after one school year but before the next is not a withdrawal. | KM |  |
## Acronyms
| Acronym | Description | SEAA Confirmed By |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DNPE | North Carolina Division of Non-Public Education | KM |
| ESA+ | Education Student Accounts program | KM |
| HDMA | Highest Decision-Making Authority for the nonpublic school. The staff member with the highest decision-making authority at the eligible school as defined by the bylaws, articles of incorporation, or other governing document of the school. | KM |
| HRE | Highly Regulated Entity - providers that have NPI numbers and are allowed to offer services within NC, as confirmed through NC Tracks. These providers do not have to provide credentialing information when enrolling or renewing. | KM |
| K12 | Refers to &ldquo;kindergarten through twelfth grade.&rdquo; Note that there is no space or dash between K and 12. | KM |
| SEAA | The (North Carolina) State Education Assistance Authority | KM |
| DPI | (North Carolina) Department of Public Instruction, which is the source of various data which SEAA uses to verify eligibility. | KM |
| DHHS | (North Carolina) Department of Health & Human Services, which is the source of various data which SEAA uses to verify eligibility. | KM |

# DevSecOps Glossary

*Created: 2024-11-01T15:57:07.478Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2024-11-01T16:18:00.108Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3577741324/DevSecOps+Glossary*

---

IaaS -
Infrastructure as a Service (IaaS) is a type of cloud computing service that offers essential compute, storage, and networking resources on demand, on a pay-as-you-go basis. IaaS is one of the four types of cloud services, along with software as a service (SaaS), platform as a service (PaaS), and serverless.
Migrating your organization's infrastructure to an IaaS solution helps you reduce maintenance of on-premises data centers, save money on hardware costs, and gain real-time business insights. IaaS solutions give you the flexibility to scale your IT resources up and down with demand. They also help you quickly provision new applications and increase the reliability of your underlying infrastructure.
IaaS lets you bypass the cost and complexity of buying and managing physical servers and datacenter infrastructure. Each resource is offered as a separate service component, and you only pay for a particular resource for as long as you need it. A cloud computing service provider like Azure manages the infrastructure, while you purchase, install, configure, and manage your own software&mdash;including operating systems, middleware, and applications.
https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/resources/cloud-computing-dictionary/what-is-iaas/?msockid=2dfa465b52c8698637025341530f685d 
IaC - 
Infrastructure as Code (IaC) is a method of managing and provisioning IT infrastructure using code, rather than manual configuration. It allows teams to automate the setup and management of their infrastructure, making it more efficient and consistent. This is particularly useful in the DevOps environment, where teams are constantly updating and deploying software.
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/devops/deliver/what-is-infrastructure-as-code 
Terraform - 
Terraform is a popular infrastructure-as-code tool that allows you to automate the provisioning and management of infrastructure resources. It uses configuration files written in the HashiCorp Configuration Language (HCL) to define the desired state of your infrastructure, and it uses various commands to apply those configurations and manage your infrastructure resources.
Terraform is an open-source infrastructure as code (IaC) software tool which can be used to provision the infrastructure of a cloud platform. The scripts which have been used to provision infrastructure can be human-readable configuration files that can be versioned, reused, and shared. You can provision a wide range of resources in the cloud by using terraform like compute, storage, networking, and application services, across a variety of cloud providers and on-premises environments.
The language used in the terraform is declarative type where you can just mention the required state, no need of mentioning how to achieve that desired state. With the help of the state file terraform will create, modify, or destroy resources.
https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/what-is-terraform/ 
https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/tutorials/azure-get-started?product_intent=terraform 
https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h970ZBgKINg

# Project Charter

*Created: 2024-02-15T18:13:20.181Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*Last Updated: 2024-07-29T10:38:51.189Z by Phillip G von Gretener*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3023601669/Project+Charter*

---

## I. Table of Contents
16falselistfalsepipe## II. Project Background 
The North Carolina State Education Assistance Authority (SEAA) currently uses commercially developed software called Envision/MyPortal to administer these programs:
- The North Carolina Personal Education Student Accounts (ESA+) for Children with Disabilities Program, established by G.S. &sect; 115C-590 et. seq., provides scholarships for parents to pay educational expenses, including tuition and fees, for children with disabilities who attend school in an eligible nonpublic setting.

- The Opportunity Scholarship Program, established by G.S. &sect; 115C-562.1 et seq., provides scholarships for parents to pay tuition and required fees for their children to attend school in an eligible nonpublic setting. Student eligibility for this program was significantly expanded by the NC State Legislature beginning for the 2024/2025 school year.

The Envision software is customizable, but SEAA has had to rely on the vendor&rsquo;s availability to make changes. This has resulted in SEAA having to implement manual processes in some cases, to meet new and changing requirements from the State Legislature.  In addition, SEAA has identified numerous &ldquo;pain points&rdquo; where the system isn&rsquo;t fully meeting their needs from a functionality and/or efficiency perspective.
SEAA has received an appropriation from the State of NC to develop an in-house system to support these programs moving forward.  SEAA is engaging College Foundation, Inc (CFI) to develop, implement and maintain the system on SEAA&rsquo;s behalf. The target date for a fully implemented system is 2/1/2026.  
## III. Project Overview 
CFI will oversee the planning, design, development, testing, implementation and on-going support of a modern web-based system for administering the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship. The system is to be fully operational for the 2026/2027 school year (applications for the programs start 2/1/26).  
### In Scope:
1. System functionality required to apply, renew, disburse and track funds for the Opportunity Scholarship and ESA+ Programs. (See Functional Requirements #1 Section IV)

2. School management functions to support both Programs. (See Functional Requirements #2 Section IV)

3. SEAA administrative functionality/abilities needed to administer both Programs. (See Functional Requirements #3 Section IV)

4. Customer relationship management functionality needed to manage interactions with customers. (See  Functional Requirements #4 Section IV)

5. Technical architecture and infrastructure needed to support the new system.

6. Conversion of data from Envision/MyPortal to the new system. 

7. Integration with SEAA&rsquo;s funds repository vendor.  

8. Communications, documentation and planning needed for system implementation and on-going maintenance. (See Non-Functional Requirements #4 Section IV)

9. An exploration of what it would take to integrate the new system with SEAA&rsquo;s phone system. (See Non-Functional Requirements #5 Section IV)

10. Adherence to best practices for web-based system development (See Non-Functional Requirements #3 Section IV)

11. An exploration whether there is a need to automate or integrate anything related to SAS.

### Out of Scope:
1. Sunsetting the Envision/MyPortal software and any notifications to the Envision vendor.

2. Integration with any other software or systems not specified in this document.

3.  A Change Management Plan or any change management activities for SEAA staff will be handled independent of this project. 

4. The pilot for using RDS validation for 2024 will be managed independent of this project, but the information learned from the pilot will be used for creating the validation function in the new system.

5. No procedures for handling paper applications for ESA+ and the Opportunity Scholarship.  Paper applications will not be accepted for either program.

6. A K12 website. This will be pursued as a separate project.

Note:  Additions to scope identified after the sign-off of this Project Charter will be handled though a Change Request (CR) process. The SEAA and CFI Project Sponsors will approve any scope changes based on an assessment from CFI on the impact to schedule, budget and resources.  
## IV. Project Deliverables
### Functional Requirements
1. System functionality required to apply, renew, disburse and track funds for both Programs. 
Web portal/ Dashboard for SEAA, parents, participating schools and service providers.

2. Application Process

3. Applicant Validation/Evaluation Process

4. Non-Public School Validation 

5. Override Process

6. Lottery Process

7. Disbursements - to schools for both programs and disbursements to parents and approved third party equipment and service providers for the ESA+ program only. 

8. Third Party Equipment, Service Provider & Non-Public School Validation

9. Funding Adjustments 

10. Renewals Process

11. Funds Tracking for Parents 

School Management Functions
1. New School Registration Process

2. Payment Processing Support

3. Compliance Requirements

4. Issue Tracking

5. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents. 

SEAA Administrative functionalities 
1. Robust query capabilities to perform ad hoc queries.

2. SEAA Funds Tracking - to include monthly and yearly reconciliation processes.

3. Batch posting to student or school records.

4. Viewable audit history of transactions and who processed them.

5. Ability to delete duplicate records and make minor text changes to the name of a field or explanatory text in the system.

6. Ability to post a banner message on any User Interface screen (referred to as &ldquo;lemon bar messages&rdquo; on CFNC) without the need for IT support. 

7. Ability to create a custom label/field on records to use for querying purposes, data analysis, and/or to otherwise flag records meeting certain criteria (note, these fields would not appear on customer facing UI screens).

8. Ability for SEAA to do bulk emails to account holders without needing IT assistance.   

Customer relationship management functionality 
1. A place to store interactions with schools and parents, identify and document issues to enable SEAA to understand a customer&rsquo;s situation quickly.

2. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents. 

### Non-Functional Requirements
1. Integration with &ldquo;Class Wallet&rdquo; software used for a funds repository.

2. Conversion of data from Envision to the new system.

3. Adherence to best practices for development of web-based systems.
Secure login process for the SEAA and Parents, and Schools/Service Providers web portals.

4. Access Control process for managing system/web portal access and permissions. 

5. Security Requirements to be defined with the assistance of CFI Security and SEAA Information Technology group.

6. Adherence to industry standards for system performance (availability, redundancy, scalability, etc.).

7. Adherence to brand standards (SEAA&rsquo;s).

8. Adherence to industry Accessibility standards for web sites.

Communications, documentation and planning needed for implementation and on-going maintenance. 
1. Production Support Plan 

2. Disaster Recovery Plan

3. SEAA defined communication plan and implementation thereof for notification about the new system to current program/scholarship recipients and any marketing about the new system to the public. 

4. Staff Training to be jointly coordinated by SEAA and CFI. 

5. Business and Technical system documentation.

 Phone System integration exploration:  Business Requirements; IT assessment of the work; and a proposed implementation timeline.  
### System Development Deliverables
1. Statement of Work(s) and Contract(s) for procured technical resources

2. Functional & Non-Functional Requirements

3. Architecture / Infrastructure Plan

4. UX Designs / Prototypes / Wireframes

5. Task tracking in Jira   

6. Release Plan / Roadmap 

### Project Management Deliverables
1. Project Charter (this document)

2. Communication Plan

3. Milestone Tracking /Schedule (to be updated on-going)

4. Monthly Project Status Reports

5. Risk Log (to be updated on-going)

6. Key Decisions Log (to be updated on-going)

7. Scope Change Request Process & Log (to be updated on-going)

8. Project Confluence Space for collaboration and storage of project documentation. 

## V. Project Resources 
Note:  The use of the word &ldquo;Product&rdquo; in this section refers to both the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship.

## VI.  Major Milestones
|  | Milestone | Milestone Lead | Target Date |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Create Confluence Space / Project Document Repository | Martha Haley | 2/15/2024 |
| 2 | CFI/SEAA Project Kick-off Meeting | Martha Haley | 2/15/2024 |
| 3 | Project Charter (signed off by SEAA & CFI Sponsors) | Martha Haley | 2/29/2024 |
| 4 | Begin technical team staffing process | Pete Rau | 3/1/2024 |
| 5 | Initial minimum requirements for potential multi-phase approach (for interim product for 2/1/25 release) | Rob Hines | 3/15/2024 |
| 6 | Checkpoint for a go/no-go decision regarding a multi-phase approach | Rob Hines | 4/15/2024 |
| 7 | Discovery, initial phase (incl. mapping current state, pain points, future state) complete | Rob Hines | 4/30/2024 |
| 8 | Development Phase begins* | Pete Rau | 5/1/2024 |
| 9 | Prospective launch of interim product | Martha Haley | 2/1/2025 |
| 10 | Project Completion* | Martha Haley | 2/1/2026 |
*Additional Milestones will be identified to track progress once the project gets underway. The Milestones and their progress will be tracked in Confluence.  
## VII.  Assumptions  
|  | Description |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | CFI will determine, retain and oversee the technical resources needed to fulfill this engagement with the funding to be approved by SEAA. |
| 2 | SEAA staff participation will be needed throughout the effort.  CFI will make every effort to utilize their time effectively. |
| 3 | CFI staff will provide on-going maintenance and support for the system post go-live. |
| 4 |  SEAA staff coordinated by SEAA will perform User Acceptance Testing. |
| 5 | Other than potentially providing parent/student validation assistance for the Opportunity Scholarship for the 2024/2025 school year, the plan is to use the Envision software to meet all 2024/2025 and 2025/2026 ESA+ Program and Opportunity Scholarship needs. |
| 6 | Budget tracking and meeting of any State of NC reporting requirements on project status or dollars spent/remaining will be handled by SEAA independent of the project. |
| 8 | A project Change Request  (CR) will be completed and approved by SEAA & CFI Project Sponsors if a decision is made to add phone system integration to scope.  The CR will include details of the agreed upon project impacts (e.g., schedule, cost, and resources). |
| 9 | Not all of the SEAA needs covered under this state appropriation need to be managed under a single project.  Discrete bodies of work may be better resourced and tracked under separate project umbrellas with their own Project Charter.  Consideration of this can be made, especially if any additional scope needs arise. |
## VIII.  Risks
|  | Description |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | With long duration projects, there is always a risk that key project resources may leave before the project is completed. |
| 2 | Developing a system from scratch inherently carries more risk of failure than implementation of a SaaS system or upgrading an existing system. |
| 3 | Competing priorities for CFI and/or SEAA resources could impact project participation and the timely completion of project work. |
| 4 | Delays in obtaining external technical resources may delay the start of programming. |
| 5 | Scope creep can adversely impact the overall timeline, cause a need for resequencing work (even a need for rework), and impact team morale and momentum. |
| 6 | Late identification of needed system/product integrations could result in a need to re-do architecture/infrastructure plans, as well as impact the overall timeline. |
| 7 | CFI will need access to export Envision data and potentially support from Golden Bridges (the Envision vendor).  It is unknown (by CFI) what Golden Bridges' contractual obligations are in this area and how cooperative they will be. |
| 8 | As of the start of this project, CFI has no experience with the Class Wallet software and what it will take to integrate this with the new system. |
| 9 | Laws enacted may make changes to either or both Programs while the project is in progress or shortly thereafter.  Building in flexibility into the new system is key, but it&rsquo;s not possible to anticipate and account for every change scenario. |
| 10 | There is always a possibility that SEAA will replace one of their third party providers and this will likely impact scope, schedule and resources.  We will use the Change Request process for these changes.   |
## IX.  Project Success Criteria
1. The system will be fully operational by 2/1/26.*

2. SEAA&rsquo;s current manual processes performed in the administration of the ESA and Opportunity Scholarship will be automated to the extent possible.

3. The deliverables specified in this Charter will be met.*

4.  SEAA will be well informed of the project status throughout the project.

5.  A clear process for reporting any Production issues and future enhancement requests will be defined prior to any functionality going-live.  

*Future approved scope changes (additions or deletions) could impact the deliverables produced and the project completion date.

# K12 System Replacement Project Charter - Version Distributed to full team on 2/14/2024

*Created: 2024-01-21T13:56:24.585Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*Last Updated: 2024-02-15T18:13:08.018Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/2973630849/K12+System+Replacement+Project+Charter+-+Version+Distributed+to+full+team+on+2+14+2024*

---

## I. Table of Contents
16falselistfalsepipeNote:  The text in teal within this document was added yesterday based on a meeting with Rob, Martha, Kathryn, Katie and Lauren.  The CFI Technical team needs an opportunity to review these additions and assess whether they will impact their stated technical Milestones in this document and/or their ability to commit to the completion of this entire scope by 2/1/26.  
The target date for obtaining comments on this Project Charter from the Technical team, as well as comments from any other team members or stakeholders will be 2/23/24, with the goal of obtaining Sponsor sign-off shortly thereafter.     
## II. Project Background 
The North Carolina State Education Assistance Authority (SEAA) currently uses commercially developed software called Envision/MyPortal to administer these programs:
- The North Carolina Personal Education Student Accounts (ESA+) for Children with Disabilities Program, established by G.S. &sect; 115C-590 et. seq., provides scholarships for parents to pay educational expenses, including tuition and fees, for children with disabilities who attend school in an eligible nonpublic setting.

- The Opportunity Scholarship Program, established by G.S. &sect; 115C-562.1 et seq., provides scholarships for parents to pay tuition and required fees for their children to attend school in an eligible nonpublic setting. Student eligibility for this program was significantly expanded by the NC State Legislature beginning for the 2024/2025 school year.

The Envision software is customizable, but SEAA has had to rely on the vendor&rsquo;s availability to make changes. This has resulted in SEAA having to implement manual processes in some cases, to meet new and changing requirements from the State Legislature.  In addition, SEAA has identified numerous &ldquo;pain points&rdquo; where the system isn&rsquo;t fully meeting their needs from a functionality and/or efficiency perspective.
SEAA has received an appropriation from the State of NC to develop an in-house system to support these programs moving forward.  SEAA is engaging College Foundation, Inc (CFI) to develop, implement and maintain the system on SEAA&rsquo;s behalf. The target date for a fully implemented system is 2/1/2026.  
## III. Project Overview 
CFI will oversee the planning, design, development, testing, implementation and on-going support for a web-based system for administering the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship.  The system is to be fully operational for the 2026/2027 school year (applications for the programs start 2/1/26).  
### In Scope:
1.  All functionality needed to administer both the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship (as defined more granularly in the Project Deliverables section below), including functionality needed for school and service provider participation in the programs.

2. The technical architecture and infrastructure needed to support the new system.

3. Conversion of data from Envision/MyPortal to the new system. 

4. Integration with the &ldquo;Class Wallet&rdquo; software, used as a funds repository.  

5. Communication plan/approach lead by SEAA for notification and information about the new system to current program/scholarship recipients, as well as any advertising to the public.  

6. Defining and implementing a Production Support Plan.   

7. A collaboration by SEAA and CFI on staff training on the new system. 

8. Technical and Business documentation for the new system.

9. Adherence to identified accessibility standards. 

10. Exploration for potential inclusion of an integration with the phone system as part of this project (see related Assumption #8 in Section VII).  

11. Document storage for ESA+ and Opportunity Scholarship program related documents.

### Out of Scope:
1. Sunsetting the Envision/MyPortal software and any notifications to the Envision vendor.

2. Integration with any other software or systems not specified in the Project Deliverables section.

3.  A Change Management Plan or any change management activities for SEAA staff will be handled independent of this project. 

4. The pilot for using RDS validation for 2024 will be managed independent of this project, but the information learned from the pilot will be used for creating the validation function in the new system.

5. No procedures for handling paper applications for ESA+ and the Opportunity Scholarship.  Paper applications will not be accepted for either program.

6. A K12 website. This will be pursued as a separate project.

Note:  Additions to scope identified after the sign-off of this Project Charter will be handled though a Change Request (CR) process. The SEAA and CFI Project Sponsors will approve any scope changes based on an assessment from CFI on the impact to schedule, budget and resources.  
## IV. Project Deliverables
### Functional Requirements
Web Portals 
1. A web portal accessed via a secure login process for SEAA to be able to view and administer both programs.

2. A web portal/dashboard accessed via a secure login process for Parents to be able to apply, indicate a school change, and track use of funds for both programs. 

3. A web portal/dashboard accessed via a secure login process for participating schools and other service providers to register and fulfill program responsibilities for both programs. 

Functions to Support the ESA+ Program
1. Application Process

2. Applicant Validation/Evaluation Process

3. Override Process

4. Lottery Process

5. Disbursements - to schools, parents and approved third party equipment and service providers

6. Third Party Equipment, Service Provider & Non-Public School Validation

7. Funding Adjustments 

8. Renewals Process

9. SEAA Funds Tracking - to include monthly and yearly reconciliation processes

10. Funds Tracking for Parents - via integration with a CFI developed web portal and the Class Wallet software.

11. Tracking communications with customers.

12. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents. 

13. Administration Capabilities for SEAA

Functions to Support the Opportunity Scholarship 
1. Application Process

2. Applicant Validation/Evaluation Process

3. Non-Public School Validation

4. Override Process

5. Lottery Process

6. Disbursements to schools 

7. Funding Adjustments

8. Renewals Process

9. SEAA Funds Tracking - to include monthly and yearly reconciliation processes

10. Funds Tracking for Parents 

11. Tracking communications with customers.

12. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents. 

13. Administration Capabilities for SEAA

School Management Functions
1. Registration Process

2. Payment Processing Support

3. Compliance Requirements

4. Issue Tracking

5. Document tracking, document review & eSignature of legally binding documents. 

### Non-Functional Requirements
1. Integration with &ldquo;Class Wallet&rdquo; software.

2. Secure login process for the SEAA and Parents, and Schools/Service Providers web portals.

3. Access Control process for managing system/web portal access and permissions. 

4. Security Requirements to be defined with the assistance of CFI Security and SEAA Information Technology group.

5. Conversion of data from Envision to the new system.

6. Adherence to industry standards for system performance (availability, redundancy, scalability, etc.).

7. Production Support Plan 

8. Disaster Recovery Plan

9. Accessibility standards

10. A communication plan/approach and implementation thereof for notification/information about the new system to current program/scholarship recipients and any marketing about the new system to the public. 

11. Staff Training

### System Development Deliverables
1. Statement of Work(s) and Contract(s) for procured technical resources

2. Functional & Non-Functional Requirements

3. Architecture / Infrastructure Plan

4. UX Designs / Prototypes / Wireframes

5. Task tracking in Jira (or other agreed upon project tracking software used by the procured vendor).   

6. Release Plan / Roadmap 

### Project Management Deliverables
1. Project Charter (this document)

2. Communication Plan

3. Milestone Tracking /Schedule (to be updated on-going)

4. Monthly Project Status Reports

5. Risk Log (to be updated on-going)

6. Key Decisions Log (to be updated on-going)

7. Scope Change Request Process & Log (to be updated on-going)

8. Project Confluence Space for collaboration and storage of project documentation. 

## V. Project Resources 
Note:  The use of the word &ldquo;Product&rdquo; in this section refers to both the ESA+ Program and the Opportunity Scholarship. 
| Role | Description | Name |
| SEAA Project Sponsor | - Project champion.
- Legitimizes the project&rsquo;s goals and objectives, keeps abreast of major project activities, and is the decision-maker for the project. 
- Signs off on the Project Charter and scope change approver.  
- Monitors the budget and coordinates approval of budget related decisions with SEAA Executives.
- Makes policy decisions for the Product.
- Keeps SEAA Executives apprised of the project. | Kathryn Marker |
| CFI Project Sponsor & CFI Product Owner | - Responsible for ensuring CFI is meeting SEAA expectations in the planning and execution of this project.  
- Working with the Business and the CFI Sr. BA to define the &ldquo;future state&rdquo; for the Product.
- Makes day to day decisions and answer questions from the technical team regarding features/functionality in consultation with SEAA Product Lead.
- Responsible party for ensuring the Product satisfies SEAA&rsquo;s vision.
- Keeps CFI Executives apprised of the project. | Rob Hines |
| CFI Project Manager | - Ensures the project follows a clearly defined approach.
- Ensures information is disseminated according to a Communications Plan. 
- Coordinates with CFI Project Sponsor and other CFI team members to identify key milestones; develop and maintain a schedule; identifies and manages project risks.
- Establishes and implements a Change Request process for managing project scope.
- Anticipates and responds to resource needs and/or conflicts.
- Anticipates potential challenges and issues; coordinates the resolution of problems. | Martha Haley |
| SEAA Project Manager | - Collaborates with the CFI Project Manager on the planning, management and execution of the project.
- Responsible for marshalling SEAA resources as needed and ensuring timely responses from SEAA on decisions and information requests. | Katie Gerhardt |
| SEAA Product Lead | - The Business Lead for the project.
- Primary Subject Matter Expert for business functions/requirements.
- Review, approve and prioritize user stories for development.
- Participate in maintaining the user story backlog. | Lauren Bader |
| SEAA Business Analyst | - Subject Matter Expert.  
- Participates in defining and documenting business requirements.
- Aid as needed to prioritize and refine requirements to be developer ready.
- Assist the Product Lead as needed/requested. | Padma Tumu |
| SEAA Quality Assurance Analyst | - Subject Matter Expert
- Responsible for planning and coordinating User Acceptance Testing for SEAA.
- Assist the Product Lead as needed/requested. | Jennifer Sills |
| CFI Executive Technology Lead | Responsible for:- The project&rsquo;s technology and architecture decisions.
- The software development approach used to deliver the new system. 
- Identification of technical resource needs/skills and retaining those resources.
- Overseeing the performance of all technical resources on the project.  
- Ensuring that technical non-functional requirements are defined and met. 
- Ensuring a Production Support Plan is defined and documented prior to go-live to ensure a smooth transition to the post-project state. | Pete Rau |
| CFI Sr. Business Analyst | Responsible for:- The elicitation and documentation of project requirements.  
- Translation of requirements into user stories for developer consumption.
- Coordinating with the UX Designer on wireframes and presenting prototypes to the Business for review and approval.
- Performing assistance and research as requested, to determine the best approach for meeting a requirement or deliverable. 
- Help/provide support to ensure implementations go smoothly from a Business and Technical perspective. 
- Responsible for maintaining the Requirements documentation in Confluence. | Michael Darnell |
| CFI UX Design | Responsible for the UX Design to ensure UX design best practices are followed.  This includes providing oversight for any external UX Design resources. | Kwame Amponsah |
| CFI Architect & Software Development Coordinators | Responsible for:- Ensuring that the architecture and technologies used for this project align with our current technology environment and best practices.
- Providing regular check-ins and guidance to the technical resources on the project.
- Providing support and guidance on the identification and resolution of technical issues. 
- Notifying the CFI Project Manager, CFI Project Sponsor, and CFI Executive Technical Lead of any technical risks or significant technical issues.  | Marty Flournory &           Sumith Mathur |
| Executive Stakeholders | Executive stakeholders to be kept abreast of the overall status of the project. | Andrea Poole, Wendy McAlister &  Betsy Rozakis |
| External Technical Resources | CFI will identify, retain and manage the external technical resources needed to fulfill this engagement.  These resources might include a Lead Architect, Developers, UX Designer, QA/Testers and a Scrum Master. | TBD |
## VI.  Major Milestones
|  | Milestone | Milestone Lead | Target Date |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Create Confluence Space / Project Document Repository | Martha Haley | 2/15/2024 |
| 2 | CFI/SEAA Project Kick-off Meeting | Martha Haley | 2/15/2024 |
| 3 | Project Charter (signed off by SEAA & CFI Sponsors) | Martha Haley | 2/29/2024 |
| 4 | Begin technical team staffing process | Pete Rau | 3/1/2024 |
| 5 | Initial minimum requirements for potential multi-phase approach (for interim product for 2/1/25 release) | Rob Hines | 3/15/2024 |
| 6 | Checkpoint for a go/no-go decision regarding a multi-phase approach | Rob Hines | 4/15/2024 |
| 7 | Discovery, initial phase (incl. mapping current state, pain points, future state) complete | Rob Hines | 4/30/2024 |
| 8 | Development Phase begins* | Pete Rau | 5/1/2024 |
| 9 | Prospective launch of interim product | Martha Haley | 2/1/2025 |
| 10 | Project Completion* | Martha Haley | 2/1/2026 |
*Additional Milestones will be identified to track progress once the project gets underway. The Milestones and their progress will be tracked in Confluence.  
## VII.  Assumptions  
|  | Description |
| 1 | CFI will determine, retain and oversee the technical resources needed to fulfill this engagement with the funding to be approved by SEAA. |
| 2 | SEAA staff assistance will be needed throughout the effort, but especially in the early-to-middle stages when requirements are being developed and design is underway. CFI will make every effort to utilize their time effectively. |
| 3 | CFI staff will provide on-going maintenance and support for the system post go-live. |
| 4 |  SEAA staff coordinated by SEAA will perform User Acceptance Testing. |
| 5 | Other than potentially providing parent/student validation assistance for the Opportunity Scholarship for the 2024/2025 school year, the plan is to use the Envision software to meet all 2024/2025 ESA+ Program and Opportunity Scholarship needs. |
| 6 | Budget tracking and meeting of any State of NC reporting requirements on project status or dollars spent/remaining will be handled by SEAA independent of the project. |
| 7 | The new system will provide a conduit to access funds information directly on the Class Wallet software (versus pulling Class Wallet data to display on the new system). |
| 8 | A project Change Request  (CR) will be completed and approved by SEAA & CFI Project Sponsors if a decision is made to add phone system integration to scope (i.e., upon the completion of the exploration - In Scope section #9).  The CR will include details of the agreed upon project impacts (e.g., timeline, cost, additional resources). |
| 9 | Not all of the SEAA needs covered under this state appropriation need to be managed under a single project.  Discrete bodies of work may be better resourced and tracked under separate project umbrellas with their own Project Charter.  Consideration of this can be made, especially if any additional scope needs arise. |
## VIII.  Risks
|  | Description |
| 1 | With long duration projects, there is always a risk that key project resources may leave before the project is completed. |
| 2 | Developing a system from scratch inherently carries more risk of failure than implementation of a SaaS system or upgrading an existing system. |
| 3 | Competing priorities for CFI and/or SEAA resources could impact project participation and the timely completion of project work. |
| 4 | Delays in obtaining external resources may delay the start of programming. |
| 5 | Scope creep can adversely impact the overall timeline, cause a need for resequencing work (even a need for rework), and impact team morale and momentum. |
| 6 | CFI will need access to export Envision data and potentially support from Golden Bridges (the Envision vendor).  It is unknown (by CFI) what Golden Bridges' contractual obligations are in this area and how cooperative they will be. |
| 7 | As of the start of this project, CFI has no experience with the Class Wallet software and what it will take to integrate this with the new system. |
| 8 | Late identification of needed system/product integrations could result in a need to re-do architecture/infrastructure plans, as well as impact the overall timeline. |
## IX.  Project Success Criteria
1. The system will be fully operational by 2/1/26.*

2. SEAA&rsquo;s current manual processes performed in the administration of the ESA and Opportunity Scholarship will be automated to the extent possible.

3. The deliverables specified in this Charter will be met.*

4.  SEAA will be well informed of the project status throughout the project.

5.  A clear process for reporting any Production issues and future enhancement requests will be defined prior to any functionality going-live.  

*Future approved scope changes (additions or deletions) could impact the deliverables produced and the project completion date.

npm add --global nx

ng build shared

npm run build:shared

# System Development Life Cycle (SDLC)

*Created: 2024-07-16T14:09:58.586Z by Dan Sullivan*
*Last Updated: 2024-09-30T01:39:14.405Z by Dan Sullivan*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3316285441/System+Development+Life+Cycle+SDLC*

---

#### effective 8/1/2024
# Purpose and Objective
The System Development Lifecycle (SDLC) is designed to foster flexibility, continuous feedback, and collaboration in the design, development and ongoing support of new systems, as well as major upgrades to critical legacy systems.  The SDLC is used to clarify the requirement that the security function throughout.  It ensures that the team is responsive to changing requirements and delivers high-value features incrementally.
The SDLC supports the CFI Security Policy and provides guidance for the implementation of several NIST Controls, which are described in Relevant NIST Controls.
### Scope 
The SDLC applies to projects that begin on or after the Effective Date, and that are either (a) new, critical systems being designed and developed or acquired; or (b) new, major upgrades of critical legacy systems.  The Executives determine whether a particular endeavor meets this criteria.
The SDLC acronym is used globally to refer variously to:  
- System Development Lifecycle as used by NIST;  

- Software Development Lifecycle as generally used by the software industry;  

- Security Development Lifecycle as used by Microsoft; and  

- Secure Software Development Lifecycle as used by OWASP

CFI uses the term SDLC to refer to all of the transitions that a System goes through as it progresses from  initial inception through to sunset; and all of the related firmware, hardware, connectivity, and other  environmental configurations necessary to support that progression.  In the context of the SDLC, CFI  uses the term System or Information System to refer to a &ldquo;&hellip;discrete set of information resources  organized for the collection, processing, maintenance, use, sharing, dissemination, or disposition of  information.&rdquo; (Source: NIST 800-53)
### Effective Date  : 8/1/2024
### SDLC Phases and Artifacts
The SDLC phases are further described in SDLC Phases. The Artifacts associated with  each phase are further described in the Glossary
# SDLC Phases
The SDLC includes project-level activities, as well as iterative cycles called Sprints, each sprint typically lasting 2 weeks. Each cycle includes the following phases:
1. Envisioning (project based)

2. Design Sprint (project based)

3. Planning (sprint based)

4. Execution (sprint based)

5. Review (sprint based)

6. Release (sprint based)

7. Operations & Maintenance (ongoing)

8. System Sunset (project based)

| In order to adhere to the SDLC, the topics in the Artifact list need to be addressed prior to exiting the Phase.  Each team has latitude to determine how best to do so.  For example, a separate document for each topic may not be necessary.  Projects that follow an Agile approach use the Phases identified below to guide execution.  Artifacts are the outputs of sprints. They should be identified during the planning of each Phase, and collected by the Project Manager or Product Owner.See Artifact Checklist for a list of the Artifacts that should be considered.  Decisions about how to address each Artifact are documented, regardless of whether an Agile approach is used. |
| SDLC Phase  | Typical Activities |
| (1) Envisioning (Project based)Establish a shared vision, value case, and high-level goals for the system or initiative | - Define the product vision and objectives.
- Problem statement.
- Identify stakeholders and their needs.
- Create/update a high-level product roadmap.
- Develop an initial product backlog with prioritized user stories.
- Complete selected Artifacts. |
| (2) Design (Project based)Design, prototype and test possible solutions to validate desirability, feasibility, and viability | - Identify target audience&rsquo;s needs.
- Ideate & prioritize solutions.
- Prototype and test .
- Design.
- Data analysis.
- Complete selected Artifacts. |
| (3) Planning (Sprint based) Plan the work for the upcoming sprint | - Conduct Sprint Planning meetings.
- Select user stories from the product backlog for the sprint; create sprint backlog.
- Estimate effort.
- Complete selected Artifacts. |
| (4) Execution (Sprint based)Develop and test features iteratively within a sprint | - Daily Stand-up meetings to synchronize the team.
- Development/coding of user stories.
- Continuous integration and testing.
- Collaboration with stakeholders for feedback.
- Complete selected Artifacts. |
| (5) Review (Sprint based)Evaluate the work completed during the sprintFocus on continuous improvement of processes and team performance | - Conduct Sprint Review meetings.
- Demonstrate completed features to stakeholders.
- Collect feedback and update the product backlog.
- Conduct Sprint Retrospectives.
- Identify and implement process improvements.
- Track and measure performance metrics.
- Complete selected Artifacts |
| (6) Release (Sprint based)Deploy the outcomes of the sprint (or sprints) to production | - Conduct release planning.
- Deploy the working increment to production.
- Create documentation and training.
- Monitor the release and address any issues.
- Gather feedback from users.
- Complete selected Artifacts. |
| (7) Operations & MaintenanceOperate and maintain the System in
Production | - Bug fixes.
- Minor feature modifications.
- Security patching.
- Tuning and upgrading hardware configurations. |
| (8) System Sunset (Project based) Retire the System and dispose of System information, hardware and software | - Completed Disposal Plan.
- Exit Approval from System Owner.
- Complete selected Artifacts. |
### SDLC and Project Management  
As a System evolves through the various SDLC phases, projects may be used to acquire, build,  implement or change the System.  For example, there might be a discovery project to establish the need for a System. There might be an acquisition project to perform marketplace comparisons, negotiate a contract and acquire an external System.  Alternatively, there might be a development project to build a System in-house.  Once implemented, a new feature might be added through subsequent development or maintenance projects. Consequently, an individual System may be the end-result of many projects, over the years.  
CFI uses Agile, Waterfall and hybrid project management methodologies and concepts to manage its  projects.  The Project Management Team is responsible for development and implementation of repeatable project management processes, further described here: Project Management Processes.
### SDLC and Configuration Management  
SDLC requirements intersect with and supplement the workflows outlined in the Configuration  Management Plan (&ldquo;CM Plan&rdquo;).  The purpose of the configuration management discipline is to (a) ensure the overall stability and security of an existing, controlled environment; (b) facilitate broad  communication related to planned changes; and (c) ensure proper authorizations are in place prior to moving forward.  Therefore, any changes to the controlled environment are implemented according to the workflows described in the CM Plan.  QA, Beta and Production are CFI&rsquo;s controlled environments.  Checking code into these environments would follow the procedures described in the CM Plan, for example.
### Security Requirements  
The SDLC is designed to incorporate information security considerations throughout.
Security Requirements are a subset of and documented within Business Requirements.  In order to benefit from a repeatable process, a baseline of Security Requirements is stored in an inventory which collects (a) security-related security test cases from previous projects; and (b) Security Engineering Principles.  The Security Officer coordinates an initial review of the inventory during the Design Phase, and selects requirements which are applicable to the System.  The Security Impact Analysis is referenced when selecting Security Requirements.  The selected requirements are an input to the Security Test Plan.
### Monitoring  
Each Product Owner is responsible for ensuring adherence to the SDLC.  Adherence is measured by (a) the review and selection of applicable Artifacts; (b) the documentation of the selection decisions; and (c) the creation or completion of the selected Artifacts.
### SDLC Revisions  
The Vice President of Technology Services is responsible for (a) coordinating a review of CFI&rsquo;s SDLC at least annually; (b) updating this document as needed; and (c) communicating changes to impacted personnel.
# Appendices  
- Artifact Checklist

- Glossary - Roles, Artifacts & Other Terms

- SDLC Phases

- Project Management Processes

- Summary of Configuration Management Plan

- Relevant NIST Controls

- Agile Roles and Responsibilities

- Security Engineering Principles

- Security Test Plan

# SDLC Glossary

*Created: 2024-07-16T14:09:59.684Z by Dan Sullivan*
*Last Updated: 2024-09-30T01:51:56.822Z by Dan Sullivan*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3316285483/SDLC+Glossary*

---

Artifact. Document required by the SDLC. Artifacts are denoted by an asterisk (*) in the Glossary.
*Artifacts from the Acquisition Process.  Documents and physical items that are generated and kept throughout the acquisition process such as RFP&rsquo;s; proposals or options considered; selection criteria or scoring rubrics;  presentation notes, and POC results.
*Artifacts from Change Management Process (on going). {Definition is TBD.}
*Architectural Diagram. Visual representation of the System architectural design.  
*Business Requirements*. Describes the characteristics of the proposed System from the viewpoint of  the business. Should include input, output and reporting requirements; internal processing functions or  transactions; necessary interfaces; and security and privacy requirements, for example.  
*CM Plan Updates. Identifies and documents the new configuration items which will be introduced  into the controlled environment, as a result of this new or changed System.  Describes the plan for managing the new items. The CM Plan provides guidance on how to incorporate the CM Plan Updates into the existing processes.  
Critical System(s). Refers to an Information System which is critical to the delivery of services in  support of CFI&rsquo;s mission. Designations are made by the Executive Committee. Systems are considered  as a whole, including related hardware, software, firmware, connectivity and other environmental  configurations necessary to support the System.  
Critical Legacy System. A Critical System that is already either in production, in development or in the  process of acquisition as of the Effective Date of this SDLC.  
Deployment Plan. Detailed checklist used by a project team to ensure deployment readiness, including  plans for creating necessary administrator and end-user documentation.  
*Disposal Plan. Captures the plan for decommissioning a System and its supporting infrastructure;  includes planning for conversion, disposal or destruction of System data.  
Executive Committee. Comprised of CFI&rsquo;s President, all Vice Presidents, and others as appointed by the  President.
*Exit Approval. Provided by a Product Owner to indicate that (a) all SDLC Artifacts are complete; and (b)  the Owner approves phase-exit. Additional tasks and/or approvals may be needed by Configuration  Management and/or Project Management processes.  
*Infrastructure Plan. Captures the provisioning blueprint for the infrastructure intended to stand up a  System. This includes complete details of all relevant client devices, browsers, etc., the full complement  of web, application, database servers, as well as System interconnection documentation.  
Information System. A discrete set of information resources organized for the collection, processing,  maintenance, use, sharing, dissemination, or disposition of information.  (Source: NIST 800-47)
*Interconnection Security Agreement. Provides technical and security requirements for direct  connections between two Systems.  Explains the security controls in place to protect the confidentiality,  integrity, and availability of the Systems and associated data. (Source: NIST 800-47)
Product Backlog.  A prioritized list of work that is derived from the Product Roadmap and its requirements.  The most important items are shown at the top of the Product Backlog so the team knows what to deliver first.
Product Roadmap.  A shared source of truth that outlines the vision, direction, priorities, and progress of a product over time.  It's a plan of action that aligns the organization around short and long-term goals for the product or project, and how they will be achieved.
*Production Support Plan. Documents any expected routine maintenance required for the System, and  defines the requirements, roles and responsibilities for all activities while it is in production.  This includes (a) ongoing technical support; (b) administration of the System; (c) security features; and (d) monitoring.  Identifies how software and hardware documentation will be initially obtained and maintained.
*Problem Statement. Captures the high-level summary of the business or technology problem is that a  proposed System is intended to address, and what is expected and what is not expected from the  System.  It should include the background and context of the proposed System, including why it is being implemented and what business values it is meant to produce.
*Process Flow Diagram. Documents the intended or actual process flows for the System under  development or the System being replaced, in the context of the business processes the System implements, impacts or interacts with.
Product Owner. Person or entity having responsibility for the development, procurement, integration,  modification, operation and maintenance, and/or final disposition of an Information System.  Responsible for ensuring adherence to the SDLC.
Project Management Team. Responsible for coordinating with IT, QA and Business managers to  develop and implement templates and standard processes for SDLC, project onboarding and project  management.
*Roles & Privileges Matrix. Captures the various user/actor roles that are required for the System, as well as their access privileges.  A matrix may need to be created for each user type, in order to clarify differing roles amongst End-Users, as well as differing roles amongst administrative users.  Also known as an access control matrix.
*Roles & Responsibilities for Security.  Describes who is responsible for what, with respect to all security considerations, throughout all SDLC activities.
SDLC. Refers to all of the transitions that a System goes through as it progresses from inception through  to disposal; and all of the related firmware, hardware, connectivity and other environmental  configurations necessary to support that progression.
*SDLC Implementation Plan.  Describes the approach to SDLC that will be utilized by a specific project.  This is used when a Project Team decides to provide clarity around roles, responsibilities, tools and processes beyond that which is provided in the enterprise SDLC.
SDLC Repository. Refers to the location used for storing SDLC Artifacts. If Artifacts are stored within a  project repository, then the SDLC Repository will act as an index.
*Security Impact Analysis. Conducted in order to determine the extent to which changes to the System  may impact the security of the System or the associated environment; and to determine the security  controls needed to ensure confidentiality, integrity and availability of the System, to the extent  practicable.
System: See Information System.
*Test Plan. Describes the scope, approach, resources and schedule of intended test activities to be  performed on the System prior to implementation, including security assessment activities. When tests are complete, the results are retained as evidence of Plan completion. Likewise, flaw remediation and/or acceptance activities should be captured as Artifacts.
*Updates to CFI&rsquo;s Security Risk Management Program.  Review CFI&rsquo;s Security Risk Management Program and determine whether any additional risk management activities need to be incorporated into the current project, and whether any revisions to the Risk Management Program are needed.



# SDLC Phases

*Created: 2024-07-16T14:09:59.818Z by Dan Sullivan*
*Last Updated: 2024-09-29T18:22:11.308Z by Dan Sullivan*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3316285497/SDLC+Phases*

---

1. Envisioning (project based)    

The Envisioning phase is the initial stage where the project's vision, goals, and objectives are defined. This phase involves identifying the business need, scope, and high-level requirements of the project.  Key stakeholders, including product owners, customers, and senior management, collaborate to establish a clear understanding of what the project aims to achieve.  This phase results in a project charter or vision document that outlines the project's purpose, strategic alignment, and expected outcomes.
1. Design Sprint (project based)   
The Design Sprint phase is a time-boxed effort aimed at validating ideas and designing solutions quickly. It typically lasts one to two weeks and involves cross-functional teams working together to prototype and test solutions for critical problems.  The goal is to reduce risk by getting early feedback from users and stakeholders before significant resources are invested.  This phase includes activities such as brainstorming, sketching, prototyping, and user testing, resulting in a validated design concept.

1. Planning (sprint based) 

The Planning phase occurs at the beginning of each sprint and focuses on defining the sprint goals, selecting user stories from the product backlog, and estimating the effort required for each task.  The team collaborates to create a sprint backlog, which is a subset of the product backlog items that the team commits to completing during the sprint.  This phase ensures that the team has a clear plan and a prioritized list of tasks to work on, setting the stage for successful execution.
1. Execution (sprint based)  

The Execution phase is where the development work takes place.  The team works on completing the tasks defined in the sprint backlog, following agile practices such as daily stand-up meetings, pair programming, and test-driven development.  This phase involves coding, testing, and integrating new features or improvements into the product increment.  Continuous collaboration and communication among team members and stakeholders are essential to address any issues or changes that arise during this phase.
1. Review (sprint based) 

The Review phase, also known as the Sprint Review or Demo, occurs at the end of each sprint.  During this phase, the team showcases the work completed during the sprint to stakeholders and gathers feedback.  The goal is to demonstrate the progress made and ensure that the product increment meets the acceptance criteria and stakeholder expectations.  This phase provides an opportunity for stakeholders to provide input, which can be used to refine and prioritize future work.
The Review phase also focuses on enhancing the development process and the product itself.  This involves regular retrospectives, where the team reflects on the sprint's successes and challenges and identifies areas for improvement.  The team discusses what went well, what didn't, and how they can improve their practices and processes.  Continuous improvement is a core principle of agile, promoting a culture of learning and adaptation to increase efficiency and effectiveness over time.
1. Release (sprint based)  

The Release phase involves deploying the completed product increment to the production environment.  This phase includes final testing, quality assurance, and any necessary documentation or training.  The goal is to deliver a functional and valuable product to end-users.  Depending on the project's requirements, releases can be scheduled at the end of each sprint or after multiple sprints.  This phase ensures that the product is ready for use and that any deployment issues are addressed promptly.
1. Operations & Maintenance (ongoing)

Systems and products are in place and operating; enhancements and/or modifications to the System are  developed and tested; and hardware and software components are added or replaced. Continuously  monitor performance of the System to ensure that it meets user and security requirements.  Utilize Configuration Management (CM) activities to document any proposed or actual changes in the security plan of the System.  Significantly large changes may warrant the creation of new project, which would begin with &ldquo;Envisioning&rdquo;.
1. System Sunset (project based)  

The System Sunset phase occurs at the end of the product's lifecycle when it is no longer needed or is being replaced by a new system.  This phase involves planning and executing the decommissioning of the system, ensuring that data is migrated or archived appropriately, and that users are transitioned to the new system or alternative solutions.  Key activities include communicating with stakeholders, updating documentation, and managing any remaining support or maintenance tasks.  The goal is to ensure a smooth transition and minimize disruption to business operations.

# Relevant NIST Controls

*Created: 2024-07-16T14:10:00.257Z by Dan Sullivan*
*Last Updated: 2024-09-29T18:23:24.167Z by Dan Sullivan*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3316285539/Relevant+NIST+Controls*

---

### SA-3 System Development Lifecycle  
1. Manage the CFI System using the System Development Life Cycle (&ldquo;SDLC&rdquo;) which incorporates information security considerations.

2. Define and document information system security roles and responsibilities throughout the SDLC.

3. Identify individuals having information system security roles and responsibilities.

4. Integrate the organizational security risk management process into SDLC activities. 

### SA-4: Acquisition Process (CE 1, 2, 9, 10)
1. Include requirements, descriptions, and criteria in acquisition contracts in accordance with applicable federal and state laws and regulations, as described in the Acquisition Procedures.

2. Require a description of the functional properties of the security controls to be employed.

3. Require documentation of design and implementation information for the security controls to be employed that includes: security-relevant external system interfaces; high-level design; and configuration instructions adequately detailed to support implementation and operations.

4. Require identification early in the SDLC of the functions, ports, protocols, and services intended for organizational use. 

### SA-5 System Documentation  
1. Obtain administrator documentation that describes:
Secure configuration, installation, and operation;

2. Effective use and maintenance of security functions; and

3. Known vulnerabilities regarding configuration and use of privileged functions.

Obtain user documentation that describes:
1. User-accessible security functions and how to effectively use those functions;

2. Methods for User interaction, which enables individuals to use the system in a more secure manner; and

3. User responsibilities for system maintenance.

Protect documentation as required, in accordance with the ​Risk Management Program.
Document attempts to obtain documentation when it is either unavailable or nonexistent, and assess risk of insufficient documentation, and take corrective action if necessary.
Distributes documentation to CFI staff as needed to perform their job. 
### SA-8 Security Engineering Principles  
Apply security engineering principles in the specification, design, development, implementation, and modification of information systems.
### SA-9: External Information System Services (CE 2)
1. Require that providers of external information system services comply with information security requirements and employ security controls in accordance with federal and state laws and regulations.

2. Define and document required oversight and User roles and responsibilities with regard to external information system services.

3. Employ periodic sampling techniques to monitor security control compliance by external service providers on an ongoing basis.

4. Require providers of external information system services to identify the functions, ports, protocols, and other services required for the use of such services. 

### SA-10 Developer and Configuration Management  
The organization requires the developer of the information system, system component, or information  system service to:  
1. Perform configuration management during the system life cycle.

2. Document, manage, and control the integrity of changes to the CFI System.

3. Implement only approved changes to the CFI system.

4. Document approved changes to the CFI System and the potential security impacts of such changes.

5. Track security flaws and flaw resolution within the CFI System and report findings to CFI Management. 

### SA-11 Developer Security Testing and Evaluation  
1. Create and implement a security assessment plan (&ldquo;Test Plan&rdquo;) as described in the SDLC.

2. Perform unit, integration, system and regression testing in accordance with the Test Plan.

3. Produce evidence of the execution of the Test Plan and the results of the security testing/evaluation.

4. Implement a verifiable flaw remediation process.

5. Correct flaws identified during security testing/evaluation.

### SA-17 Developer Security Architecture and Design
Require the developer of the information system, system component, or information system service to produce a design specification and security architecture that:
a. Is consistent with and supportive of the organization&rsquo;s security architecture which is
established within and is an integrated part of the organization&rsquo;s enterprise architecture;
b. Accurately and completely describes the required security functionality, and the
allocation of security controls among physical and logical components; and
c. Expresses how individual security functions, mechanisms, and services work together to
provide required security capabilities and a unified approach to protection.### AC-3: Access Enforcement
Use security groups to control logical access between users and processes acting on behalf of
user and information system objects such as devices, files and domains.Access Control Matrices are developed as part of the security risk analysis in the System
Development Life Cycle (SDLC).1. Data is encrypted at rest and in transit to ensure access is protected from unauthorized access.

Documented approval from CFI Management is required. Active Directory and individually issued
RFID proximity cards are used to control access to CFI Systems, in accordance with the
approved access requested.Access to security logs, System Administrator functionality and any other privileged access is
tightly controlled and restricted to explicitly authorized users who need such elevated privileges
in order to perform job duties.2. Use certificate access for multi-factor authentication for Linux systems.

### AR-7: Privacy-Enhanced System Design and Development
Design information systems to support privacy by automating privacy controls in accordance with CFI&rsquo;s System Development Life Cycle (&ldquo;SDLC&rdquo;).
