### *** DRAFT/NEEDS TO BE BUILT-OUT ***
- Product Owner / Product Manager: Defines product vision, prioritizes the backlog, and ensures maximum value delivery.

- Scrum Master: Facilitates the agile process, removes impediments, and ensures team adherence to agile practices.

- Development Team: Self-organizing, cross-functional team responsible for delivering increments of the product.

- Stakeholders: Provide input and feedback, and validate the product's value.



# Security Engineering Principles

*Created: 2024-07-16T20:57:02.097Z by Dan Sullivan*
*Last Updated: 2024-09-29T18:23:48.386Z by Dan Sullivan*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3316940801/Security+Engineering+Principles*

---

## CFI embraces the following principles to guide the specification, design, development, implementation, and modification of its information systems. 
### Risk-based Controls.  
Utilize a risk-based approach when tailoring security controls to meet organization and operational needs.
### Defense in Depth
Develop layered protections; design multiple security measures to protect the software and the data.  Assume that no single security measure is infallible.
### Least Privilege
Each component of the software should operate with the least amount of privilege necessary to complete its tasks.  This minimizes the potential damage from an exploit or breach in that component.
### Fail Securely
Design the software to handle errors or security breaches in a way that minimizes exposure or data loss. This often means defaulting to a "safe" state where sensitive operations are shut down or access is restricted in the event of a failure.
### Secure by Design
Incorporate security requirements into the system development lifecycle.  Treat security as an integral part of the software design, not an afterthought.  This involves considering security implications in every design decision, and incorporating secure coding practices from the outset.  Utilize security-oriented design review to help identify vulnerabilities, ensure controls are specified and meet security requirements.  Document and formally acknowledge security exceptions, where requirements are not fully met.
### Separation of Duties
Different roles and responsibilities should be clearly defined and separated to prevent fraud and unauthorized access.  This means, for example, that the person who writes the code should not be the one to deploy or audit it.
### Tiered Deployment
Utilize separate Development, Testing and Production environments.  Do not use production data in non-production environments.
### Code Review
Conduct code reviews to identify potential vulnerabilities prior to deployment.
### Independent Testing
Utilize a separate QA team to test builds prior to deployment to production.
### Authentication
Authenticate users and processes to ensure appropriate access control decision both within and across domains.  Utilize two-factor authentication.
### Input Validation
Validate input from all external sources to prevent improper or malicious data formats from entering the system.  Assume all input is malicious until proven otherwise.
### Encryption
Use strong encryption to protect data in transit and at rest, ensuring that even if data is intercepted or accessed without authorization, it remains unreadable without the encryption keys.
### Audit
Design and implement audit mechanisms to detect unauthorized use and to support incident investigations.



# Security Test Plan

*Created: 2024-09-28T22:32:26.421Z by Dan Sullivan*
*Last Updated: 2024-10-25T02:00:03.172Z by Dan Sullivan*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3486351621/Security+Test+Plan*

---

### Introduction
The purpose of the Security Test Plan is to ensure that software testing is conducted in a systematic and organized manner; and to develop team alignment regarding the activities that are necessary to do so.  Following the steps outlined, below, will help clarify (a) who is responsible for what; (b) the security requirements; (c) and how the Team will approach the security testing function.
### Planning Activities
1. Identify the Roles and Responsibilities that are needed on this project.

2. Identify the Security requirements, standards, and guidelines that are applicable to this project.

3. Discuss, decide and document the general approach to security testing that will be used on this project.  Include a brief description of how the team will approach these activities:
Unit, integration, system and regression testing.

4. Expectations, procedures and tools for documentation of (a)Test Cases; (b)testing activities; (c)testing results; (d) remediation of anomalies; (e)periodic reporting; and (f)Sponsor acceptance.

### Release- or Sprint-based Activities
1. Create Test Cases/stories; enter Test Cases into tracking tool for planning and monitoring purposes.

2. Prioritize and schedule testing activities necessary to address Test Cases.

3. Document results of testing activities; provide testing artifacts where possible.

4. Identify and document any identified anomalies (i.e., flaws, bugs&hellip;).

5. Prioritize identified anomalies; schedule remediation activities according to priority.

6. Document results of remediation activities.

7. Provide periodic updates to Sponsor regarding (a) coverage of Test Cases; (b) rate of anomalies detected per Test Case; (c) assessment of progress against plan; and (d) any concerns with remainder of testing activities.

8. Document Sponsor&rsquo;s review and acceptance of any unresolved anomalies, and acceptance of deliverables.

The organization requires the developer of the information system, system component, or information system service to:

2. Perform [Selection (one or more): unit; integration; system; regression] testing/evaluation at [Assignment: organization-defined depth and coverage];

3. Produce evidence of the execution of the security assessment plan and the results of the security testing/evaluation;

4. Implement a verifiable flaw remediation process; and

5. Correct flaws identified during security testing/evaluation.

### CFI Policy
SA-11: Developer Security Testing and Evaluation
1. Create and implement a security assessment plan (&ldquo;Test Plan&rdquo;) as described in the SDLC.

2. Perform unit, integration, system and regression testing in accordance with the Test Plan.

3. Produce evidence of the execution of the Test Plan and the results of the security testing/evaluation.

4. Implement a verifiable flaw remediation process.

5. Correct flaws identified during security testing/evaluation.

1. SR-112 SA-11
Control: Create and implement a security assessment plan ("Test Plan") as described in the SDLC.

2. Discussion: Modify existing processes to track and report. (BHIS p23)

SR-113; SA-11 (c)
1. Control: Produce evidence of the execution of the Test Plan and the results of the security testing/evaluation.

2. Discussion: Modify existing processes to track and report. (BHIS p24)

SR114; SA-11(e)
1. Control: Correct flaws identified during the security testing/evaluation.

2. Discussion: Modify existing processes to track and report. (BHIS p24)

### Baseline/Compliance elements of Security Test Plan

2. Produce evidence of the execution of the Test Plan and the results of the security testing/evaluation

3. Correct flaws identified during the security testing/evaluation.

# Microsoft Teams

*Created: 2024-08-15T15:51:03.778Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-11-04T16:58:37.767Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3384377360/Microsoft+Teams*

---

1. Problem: User unable to connect to Microsoft Teams, MFA failure from Authenticator app.

Solution: Went into Teams and saw College Foundations radio button.  We selected it and when we went into Authenticator we saw a newly named account with CFI in it, then scanned the QR code to take care of connection.
1. Problem: User experiences an error with MS Teams after changing password.

Solution: 
- Steps to resolve

- Step 1

- Go to settings of your computer

- Click on Accounts

- Type in Work

- Click on Access work or school

- Click the down arrow

- Click to disconnect

- Pop up displays

- Click yes

- May take two attempts to disconnect (It did for me)

- Once it displays as no longer present.

- Step 2

- Right click on the Teams App

- Click on Quit Teams

- This will close out the instance on the app.

- Pull up the App again. This may require sign ins to accounts.

- Sign in. 

- Should resolve the issue.



# Jira

*Created: 2024-08-15T17:38:00.902Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2024-08-15T17:45:47.703Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3384377375/Jira*

---

1. How to use &ldquo;Block or Blocked by&rdquo; in an Initiative, Epic, or Story from another.  You may want to say that one issue (ticket) needs to be completed before another issue is started.  The Link Issue field is your friend.

Within the Story - choose "+Add", then choose &ldquo;Link Issue&rdquo;.  
The Linked Issues field is added to the Story under Status Update.
Under Linked issues use the drop down field to choose your correct selection.
Link Issue can be used at the Initiative, Epic, or Story issue type.

# Mandatory Details

*Created: 2024-10-25T13:40:47.470Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-05-19T15:18:54.442Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3557785601/Mandatory+Details*

---

Mandatory Jira Story "elements" for when a story is added to a Sprint.
1. Team

2. Assignee (No longer Mandatory as some teams will assign a story when the Dev/QA is available).

3. Reporter

4. Story Points

5. Sprint

6. Parent

As we become a more mature agile team we will most likely be adding onto this list.  For now please focus on 1-6.

---

Interacting with the Jira Active sprints board
- As of 04/08/2025 - (As of 03/03/2025 Sub-tasks should now follow the same Status flow as the Stories) (Old process was: We are moving back to the original process) Move Sub-tasks from &ldquo;IN PROGRESS&rdquo; column directly to &ldquo;ACCEPTED&rdquo; column, bypassing the &ldquo;TESTING&rdquo; and &ldquo;READY FOR PO&rdquo; columns.  The parent Story will be tested by QA and placed in the ACCEPTED column by the Product Owner(s).

Stories and Sub-tasks should move across the board as needed.  In some cases, stories and Sub-tasks will be moved back to In Progress if the PO or QA member finds the Acceptance Criteria was not met.
 
- Jacqui Kehoe and Kim Yarborough are the Product Owners and are the only individuals that move stories from &ldquo;READY FOR PO&rdquo; to &ldquo;ACCEPTED&rdquo;

- If a member of the team wishes to pull a story into a sprint that has already started, the member must reach out to the Product Owner and receive permission.

# QA Testing Columns & Procedures

*Created: 2024-12-16T18:14:53.697Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-03-13T20:33:16.180Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3685122055/QA+Testing+Columns+Procedures*

---

Sprint Development Jira Boards:
1. &ldquo;Testing&rdquo; Column has been renamed to &ldquo;In Testing&rdquo;.

1. A new Column has been created and named &ldquo;Ready for Testing&rdquo;.

1. Procedure to use =

A team member, typically Development, will move their story from &ldquo;In Progress&rdquo; column to the &ldquo;Ready for Testing&rdquo; column.  The same team member will remove themselves from the assignee field and leave blank.  A member of the K12 QA/Test team will move the story to the &ldquo;In Testing&rdquo; column and assign their name to the story.  Once the testing of the story is complete the K12 QA/Test member will move the story to the &ldquo;Ready For PO&rdquo; column or move it back to the &ldquo;In Progress&rdquo; column.  If moved back to the &ldquo;In Progress" column, the K12 QA/Test member must reassign back to the original assignee.
Manual Testing Process



# K12 Issue Types Hierarchy

*Created: 2024-11-13T21:42:07.963Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-08T11:54:10.121Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3611557902/K12+Issue+Types+Hierarchy*

---

The Jira issue types being used in the K12 Project are:
1. Initiative - a collection of epics that drive toward a common goal.

2. Epic - a large body of work that can be broken down into a number of smaller tasks (called stories).

3. Story (aka &ldquo;user story&rdquo;) - a smaller body of work under the epic, it contains limited requirements or requests written from the perspective of an end user.

4. Sub-task - a smaller task that's part of a larger Story  

5. Spike - a spike helps teams gain knowledge, validate assumptions, and reduce risk by exploring technical feasibility or understanding requirements better. A spike should be created as a story with &ldquo;SPIKE:&rdquo; at the beginning of the title.  Story points for Spike issues should be pointed the same as Stories.  

6. Bug - issue that represent problems or defects that impede the progress or functionality of work.  Functional bugs need to be addressed ASAP.  Bugs that are not impeding functionality can be fixed when a developer has time available.  Bugs are not pointed (as of 07/10/2025).

7. Tasks - The Platform and Architecture Team is the only team that will be using Tasks.  A specific technical, or administrative work item (the "how") required to complete a Story or Epic but doesn't provide direct business value itself. 

Subtasks should have an owner. Sub-tasks were previously pointed but as of April 2025 they no longer are.  The Story gets pointed using Planning Poker during Sprint Planning.
● QA tasks
   ○ QA points will not be added to the story.
   ○ The manual testing QA sub-task will be added, but not pointed.
● The QA-only story for creating the test automation script will be pointed.
● BA stories for creating requirements will not be pointed.#### ● UX stories will not be pointed.
#### ● Bug stories will not be pointed.
The Development team(s) will not be using Tasks.  If you have the need to create a Task, which is not written as a User Story, select &ldquo;Create /Story&rdquo; and use the Story issue in place of a Task issue.  You can also create a Story and add Sub-tasks to the Story.
*Platform and Architecture Team (PAT Team) - 
Story Vs. Task - 
Story describes a feature or requirement from a user's perspective (the "what" and "why"), providing business value. A Task is a specific, technical, or administrative work item (the "how") required to complete a Story or Epic but doesn't provide direct business value itself. Stories are typically user-focused and involve code development, while tasks are for internal team work like setup, documentation, or administrative processes.



# Assign a Flag to a Story

*Created: 2025-01-02T18:18:59.516Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-01-07T18:00:16.007Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3705438219/Assign+a+Flag+to+a+Story*

---

By creating a Flag within a Jira Story the red flag will help to visually identify that this Story is being blocked by another Story.  
In this scenario we would like to set a Flag on a Story and add a comment that this Story is being blocked by another Story.
Flag =
Create a Flag on Story K12-1102 and add comment stating &ldquo;Blocked by K12-11xx&rdquo;.
On the Backlog screen you can Add flag without opening the Story.
1. Click on the three dots to the right of the Story

1. Choose Add flag

1. Insert your comment and select &ldquo;Confirm&rdquo;

You can also use Jira Relationships - Blocked and Blocked by,  to create a similar result as &ldquo;Assign a Flag&rdquo;.
Block or Blocked by...



# K12 Agile Maturity Summary

*Created: 2025-03-03T16:01:59.345Z by Jacqui Kehoe*
*Last Updated: 2025-03-26T11:50:44.038Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3864264710/K12+Agile+Maturity+Summary*

---

Agile Maturity
Overview
CFI K12 Agile TeamsAgile Maturity Overview
The Agile Maturity Matrix: A Roadmap for Growth
The Agile Maturity Matrix is a structured framework that helps organizations
assess where they are on their agile journey and identify key opportunities for
growth. It provides a clear, visual way to evaluate agility across teams and
processes&mdash;highlighting strengths, gaps, and areas for continuous
improvement.
This isn&rsquo;t about rigid compliance but about guiding transformation, enabling
teams to evolve from simply doing agile to truly being agile.Ratings Scale
0= Impeded
1=In Transition
2=Sustainable
3=Agile
4=Ideal
Topics Scale of 0-4
Team Dynamics 1
Team Environment 2
Product 1
Agile Process 2
Engineering Practices 3Team Dynamics (1)
Team Dynamics: Morale, Teamwork, Tuckman Stage, Team Working
Agreement- Tuckman Stage - Storming and Norming. We flip back and forth


between these two stages as we continue to grow our processes and
capacity. Not uncommon to see both of these stages at this point.- Team Working Agreement - This would be a good addition to our


implementation. This is a document that allows the team to determine
how they best want to work together. Gives a source of truth for the
team to be held accountable to.Team Environment (2)
Team Environment: Team size, dedicated, cross functional, self-
organizing, impediments- Self-organizing - Goal: That the team pulls in the most valuable


Ready work from the top of the backlog to meet the velocity target for
the sprint, and the team organizes around the work to get it
completed. All skillsets and members actively work towards the same
goal until the goal is met.- I see that we are making progress towards this. Would love to


have a retro on this topic specifically to take this further.- Impediments - The team does a good job of raising impediments and


blockers. Keep it up!!216 %



# SDLC - Team Level

*Created: 2025-03-03T16:03:29.814Z by Jacqui Kehoe*
*Last Updated: 2025-04-10T19:29:53.393Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3863871496/SDLC+-+Team+Level*

---

K12 Product Development - SDLC and user story subtask process
User Stories: Structure, Story Tasking & Pointing User Stories
● Stories break down the epic, encompassing all work needed to fulfill its requirements
● Written by the product team (PO and BA)
● Small, bite sized functional based stories.
● Each story should include all disciplines in it, not separate stories anymore
Tasking and Pointing
● The dev team can break down any Ready for Development stories into Subtasks, as needed.
● Sub-task breakdown is developer-driven, as they have the insights into tasks needed to complete the story
● Subtasks should have an owner and a story point estimate
● QA tasks
○ QA points will not be added to the story.
○ The manual testing QA sub-task will be added, but not pointed.
● The QA-only story for creating the test automation script will be pointed.
● BA stories for creating requirements will not be pointed.Sprint Development Flow
1. The development team collaborates on a user story to complete fully within a sprint.

Once story development and the required reviews are completed, the story is moved to the Ready for Testing column and the Story is unassigned.
a. Developer Subtasks should be in the Done column at this point (other than the QA subtask)(no PO acceptance at the sub-task level)2. Once the QA engineer picks up the story, they move it to the In Testing column and assign themselves to the story.

3. Once the QA engineer is done, they move the full story to Ready for PO.

4. Once in Ready for PO, the PO tests the story in the Test environment, then when done, they move it to the Done column.

Story Refinement
Preparing for Refinement is an ongoing process for each team&rsquo;s backlog* in Jira.
● Two permanent, placeholder &ldquo;stories&rdquo; are set up in the backlog to identify the work as:
○ Ready for Refinement - the BA deems the story ready for development, but it hasn&rsquo;t been reviewed by the team.
○ Ready for Development - means that the team has discussed the story, all are in alignment and it is ready to be
pulled into a sprint.
● The BA and PO will align the stories in the backlog accordingly so that the team always knows story status, and can
pull in if they complete their work early
● This also gives team members the ability to look ahead and review stories in preparation for Story Refinement.
● Once the stories are in Ready for Development state, the PO can then slot them into the appropriate sprint
During Refinement, the Product team walks through the prioritized stories with the developer team in order to
● Review and elaborate on acceptance criteria
● Identify and understand dependencies
● Discuss outstanding issues or questions
● If the story is &ldquo;Ready for Development&rdquo;: estimate
● If the story needs more info, defer until it is updated- In order for a story to appear in the team&rsquo;s backlog, it must have a team assigned to it

Sprint Planning
Preparing for Sprint Planning is also an ongoing process for each team&rsquo;s backlog
● Product team will pre-plan for 2 sprints in the backlog. Rolling 2 sprints pre-planned
● Current sprint - in dev, committed, etc
● Current +1 - preplanned
● Current +2 - preplanned
During Sprint Planning, the team selects the Ready for Development work it can complete in the sprint.
● Total points should not exceed the velocity target based off of the prioritized backlog.
● Do sub-task breakdown and estimate in story points (confirm accuracy if it&rsquo;s already done)
● &lsquo;Assign&rsquo; a story owner
○ Within one story, there may be more work within the story for one person than another,
○ The story owner will usher the story through the sprint



# Fibonacci Story Points

*Created: 2025-06-11T17:02:39.248Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-06-11T17:12:01.396Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4071456795/Fibonacci+Story+Points*

---

The Fibonacci sequence is a series of numbers where each number is the sum of the two preceding numbers, starting with 0 and 1.
The K12 Teams currently use 1, 2, 3, 5, and 8 story points.  If a 13 or 21 are expressed from a team member the entire team should focus on shrinking the story to an 8 or lower, so the story can be completed within a 2 week sprint.
https://www.productplan.com/glossary/fibonacci-agile-estimation/



# Definition of Done

*Created: 2025-04-30T19:25:01.014Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-06-11T16:59:27.108Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3989700631/Definition+of+Done*

---

Development Definition of Done - (Not UAT)
1. All Acceptance Criteria defined in the story have been met. 

1. Pull Requests (PR) are approved by one or more team members but not the author.  Changes are then merged and deployed by the pipeline.

1. The story has been tested using agreed upon standards in the Test Environment by the QA department. The story has passed testing.

1. All pertinent links are captured in the comments. Documentation includes Figma links when available. 

1. The story is reviewed by UX when necessary, reviewed by BA that wrote the story, who places a comment and tags the Product Owner (PO).

1. All Sub-tasks within the story have been completed.

1. Outstanding bugs/defects have been triaged. All defects are in an acceptable state including acceptable workarounds. 

1. Status of the Story is set to Done by the Product Owner.

 
Anyone notice anything missing that needs to be added?
 
 
We will save the following when we have &ldquo;Security Review&rdquo; and &ldquo;Performance Testing&rdquo; in place:
Sub-tasks and Stories that are subject to a Security Review have successfully passed such review.  We do not have Security Review tools in place currently.  Hold for later? Performance Testing of the application is within agreed limits.  We do not have a formal Performance Testing mechanism, outside of Azure default reporting metrics and K12 Dev/QA members observations, in place currently.  Hold for later?



# Definition of Ready

*Created: 2025-04-30T19:25:51.633Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-06-26T15:57:16.343Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3989700645/Definition+of+Ready*

---

Definition of Ready
When the Story meets the &ldquo;Definition of Ready&rdquo; the status will be changed to
&ldquo;Ready for Development&rdquo;.  Why?  For POs to have a good understanding of their backlog.
 
1. Story has acceptance criteria that is clear to all parties involved.

1. Has well defined actionable items.

1. Story is small enough to be completed within one two-week sprint.

1. Design has been accepted and attached to the story, where applicable.

1. Story has been pointed by the team.  If a story has a zero story point, include it as 0.

1. Each Jira Story should have the following elements filled out: Team, Reporter, Story Points, Sprint, and Parent. 

1. Any dependencies are identified.

1. Acceptance criteria is testable.

 
Team Backlog:
Once a story has been refined and meets the DoR (Definition of Ready) for the team, we will indicate that by updating the status to "Ready for Development".

# Stories should be written as...

*Created: 2025-07-15T13:51:20.309Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-07-15T13:56:48.671Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4146692107/Stories+should+be+written+as...*

---

Make sure stories are in the correct format &ndash; 
- As a  

- I want 

- So that

Many user stories are looking more like requirements than they are behavior driven user stories. (The exception is enabler stories for the architectural runway).
Format: Often adapted to "As a [system component/role], I need to [technical goal], so that [system/business benefit]."
 
Guidance:
 
Focus on the technical work needed to achieve the NFR.
 
Still clearly articulate the why (the benefit).
 
Ensure there are clear, measurable acceptance criteria.
 
Examples:
 
Performance: "As a system architect, I need to implement caching for product catalog data so that the website can handle 10,000 concurrent users without performance degradation."
 
Acceptance Criteria:
 
Load testing shows product catalog pages load in under 1 second with 10,000 concurrent users.
 
Cache hit ratio is at least 90%.



# SQL Server Installation

*Created: 2024-11-18T14:51:44.765Z by Jason Dean (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2024-11-19T15:55:03.570Z by Wesley Song*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3623125014/SQL+Server+Installation*

---

Download and install the latest version of SQLExpress
https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server/sql-server-downloads Download and install the latest version of SSMS
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/ssms/download-sql-server-management-studio-ssms?view=sql-server-ver16for Mac users
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure-data-studio/download-azure-data-studio?view=sql-server-ver16&tabs=macOS-install%2Cwin-user-install%2Credhat-install%2Cwindows-uninstall%2Credhat-uninstall  Open SSMS and connect to SQLExpress instance with Windows Authentication (you might need CFI admin to open SSMS &ldquo;As Administrator&rdquo;).
Right click server and choose Properties > Security.  Change server authentication to &ldquo;SQL Server and Windows Authentication mode.&ldquo;
 Change &lsquo;sa&rsquo; password and verify it is part of the sysadmin role
 Close SSMS
Restart SQL Server Service
 Open SSMS and try to login with new &lsquo;sa&rsquo; username/password.  If successful, create a new database.  If you don&rsquo;t get any errors, you should be good to go.  If you do get errors, work with your CFI tech to resolve.

```

Then if your azure function is named `dev-api-enrollment` your code would be.
```
-- Create a login for the managed identity
CREATE USER [dev-api-enrollment] FROM EXTERNAL PROVIDER;
-- Assign roles (e.g., db_datareader and db_datawriter)
ALTER ROLE db_datareader ADD MEMBER [dev-api-enrollment];
ALTER ROLE db_datawriter ADD MEMBER [dev-api-enrollment];
```

This will grant reader and writer permissions to the principal your Azure func identifies as within your target db.

# CORS

*Created: 2025-01-28T14:58:26.331Z by Jason Dean (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2025-02-24T18:45:56.366Z by John Grant (Unlicensed)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3770089473/CORS*

---

There are two places where CORS will need to be configured. 
- Azure Portal - Select the Function App in the list of Resources, then APIs > CORS

Azure API Management (APIM) - Select the APIM in the list of Resources, then APIs > API-Name > Version. > All Operations. In the right-hand window there should be a box that says &ldquo;Inbound Processing&rdquo;.  Click Add Policy and find the &ldquo;Allow cross-origin resource sharing (CORS)&ldquo; box.  

Once CORS is added, make sure it is first in the list or it will not work.
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/api-management/cors-policy#common-configuration-issues



# Standard API Responses

*Created: 2025-02-17T18:20:28.813Z by Jason Dean (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2025-02-19T18:54:59.374Z by Jason Dean (Unlicensed)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3826155560/Standard+API+Responses*

---

This document is to describe how we are handling API responses with all our endpoints.  This is a guide and is only meant to keep the codebase consistent across all backend teams.  In general, we are using derived classes from the ObjectResult class in the Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc namespace.
POST/Creation endpoints:
return new CreatedResult(url, Entity that was created);

Utilize view model validation attributes for data validation.  If validation errors return back list of validation results:

var validationResults = new List<ValidationResult>()
{
    new ValidationResult(message)
};
return new BadRequestObjectResult(validationResults);
PUT/Update endpoints:
return new NoContentResult();

If you need to pass back the updated object:
return new OkObjectResult(Entity that was updated)

If invalid data on update or a caught error in a try/catch, return:
var validationResults = new List<ValidationResult>()
{
    new ValidationResult(message)
};
return new BadRequestObjectResult(validationResults);
DELETE:
return new NoContentResult();
500 Error (uncaught)
Application returns 500 by default500 Error (caught)
Log to App Insights and return:
return new StatusCodeResult(500);

# K12 Structured Release Process

*Created: 2025-10-24T13:31:34.872Z by Luke Samuels*
*Last Updated: 2025-10-24T20:36:11.463Z by Luke Samuels*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4375904257/K12+Structured+Release+Process*

---

12falsenonelisttrue# Environment Definitions
## Local (a.k.a. &ldquo;developer laptop&rdquo;)
### Owner
Individual Developers
### Purpose
When referring to &ldquo;local&rdquo; environment we will mean the laptops used by developers to generate and do simple testing of K12 application code. This may occur within or without containers and leverage any number of additional tools as needed, but will not &ldquo;live in the cloud&rdquo;. Code will be pulled from the development branch in the respective repo on a regular basis to ensure proper integration with the larger code base
The following are Azure Cloud environments represented as all resources associated with a resource group of the same name. For instance the &ldquo;development&rdquo; environment is a set of Azure resources (APIM, Static Web Apps, SQL databases, etc) that all belong to the &ldquo;development&rdquo; Azure resource group.
## Development 
### Owner
Principal Software Engineers
### Purpose 
This is the &ldquo;continuous integration&rdquo; environment where developers merge their code to ensure that it functions properly both on cloud resources and with other engineers code. Moreover, this pipeline that deploys to this environment runs security, code quality, and other functional tests prior to deploy. Code does not move out of this environment until we have a reasonable expectation that it will &ldquo;work&rdquo; and is ready to be tested, and only with the approval of the environment owners or their delegates (e.g. Technical Leads)
## Testing
### Owner
QA Lead
### Purpose 
A place for longer-running or more complex test scripts as well as manual testing. Code is only merged to this environment at the discretion of the owner of the environment. 
## Staging
### Owner
QA Lead
### Purpose:
A place for User Acceptance Testing, customer demos, and training. This environment should be extremely stable and &ldquo;production-like&rdquo;. Code does not get deployed here until it is ready for review by the customer, then only at the discretion of the system owner. Release notes are required to be delivered alongside deploys to this environment. That process is outlined here. Currently this environment is in CFI&rsquo;s Azure commercial tenant with the other &ldquo;lower&rdquo; environments (development and testing) but is intended to be in the same tenant as Production in its final state. 
## Production
### Owner 
Customer (SEAA)
### Purpose 
This environment will live in Azure GovCloud but will otherwise be arranged in the same way as Staging. This environment is expected to be stable and actively maintained by the operations staff. The cadence for releasing to this environment is at the discretion of the system owner and in alignment with CFI&rsquo;s change control process.
# Promoting Code Between environments
In general, the promotion process from one environment to another is a matter of creating a pull request (PR) from one branch in a given repo to another. Upon creation of the PR a build pipeline will run that checks for defects. If that passes the PR is merged into the target branch at which point another pipeline runs that build and deploys the code to the environment that corresponds to the branch. For clarity&rsquo;s sake each environment has its own branch which is named the same as its target environment. The differences between the environment promotion processes has to do with who creates the PRs, from what branch, who reviews them, and who approves them. The pipelines differ in which tests run and which parameters are used for the build process. 
## From Local to Development
### Requirements and Rationale
The following rules/heuristics should be honored by the process (and enforced by branch protection rules in Azure DevOps):
- Allow self-approval but require at least one further approval (minimum 2 approvers) regardless of who you are.

- Default should be nobody pushes to environment branches directly in any environment.

- If changes are committed after PR is approved, approvals should removed and require approval again.

- Approvers are from one of two groups. FE Leads and BE Leads. Full stack leads should be in both.

### Workflow
1. A developer (henceforth &ldquo;the author&rdquo;) ensures their local repo is up to date with the origin

2. The author creates a feature branch based off of the development branch 

3. When complete and tested locally as much as possible, the author pushes their branch to the remote (Azure DevOps)

4. The author creates a PR against the Development branch

5. The pipeline runs and provides feedback

6. The author addresses any warnings or failures in their local and then pushes it up to the remote branch

7. Two required reviewers will be automatically added to every PR against Development. Those reviewers will be a member of the leads group that is responsible for the repository and any other engineer on the team. This can be the author of the feature branch, but only in emergency situations like vacation or sick time for leads.

8. After reviewing the code the approvers approve the code

9. The author completes the PR

10. The pipeline runs to build and deploy the code to development without any further human gates

## From Development to Testing
https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/x/JYBN3Q 
## From Testing to Staging
Code Release From Testing To Staging 
## From Staging to Production

# Confluence Tips

*Created: 2024-02-15T21:28:59.266Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*Last Updated: 2024-02-16T20:19:45.007Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3023929345/Confluence+Tips*

---

| ## What is Confluence?  
Confluence is part of the Atlassian suite of products. It is a product used to organize, share, and collaborate on content with a group or team.  Its It&rsquo;s a cloud- based product, so you don&rsquo;t need CFI VPN access.  This makes it great tool for collaborating with groups that include folks that are external to CFI. | ## What is a Confluence Space? 
A &ldquo;Space&rdquo; is an organized grouping of content within Confluence, like a single website within the internet or a SharePoint site within a company&rsquo;s instance of SharePoint.  Each Space is set-up with it&rsquo;s own permissions.  The Spaces are comprised of &ldquo;Pages&rdquo; of content.   | ## Navigating in Confluence
Here is a link to a short video (less than 5 minutes) that gives a quick overview of most of the top and left navigation menus:  https://www.google.com/search?sca_esv=557451199&rlz=1C1GCEA_enUS1019US1019&q=navigating+in+confluence&tbm=vid&source=lnms&sa=X&ved=2ahUKEwjRwazmq-GAAxWsElkFHZI5DcoQ0pQJegQIDBAB&biw=1920&bih=931&dpr=1#fpstate=ive&vld=cid:de7f194d,vid:YKHQwn4s6o8 |
|  |  |  |
| ## Creating a Page
Here is a ~3 minute video on creating a page in Confluence: https://www.atlassian.com/blog/confluence/create-engaging-confluence-pages-4-easy-steps | ## Formatting a Page
Here is a 7 minute video that covers a variety of formatting options in Confluence:   https://www.google.com/search?sca_esv=557502889&rlz=1C1GCEA_enUS1019US1019&q=confluence+formatting+cheat+sheet&tbm=vid&source=lnms&sa=X&ved=2ahUKEwjcl-WngOKAAxWGKFkFHY_cCEYQ0pQJegQIVRAB&biw=1920&bih=931&dpr=1#fpstate=ive&vld=cid:2d6fe5f7,vid:LNQUUHFFwug | ## Collaborating/ Commenting on a Page   
Here are the ways to collaborate on a page:Start with selecting the pencil and when you are done select the blue publish button. What you can do:Add text to the page.  Just start typing and hit the publish button (top right) when you are done.  You can select the pencil again if you want to add or change anything.Add a comment about something specific on the page:  highlight verbiage on the page and create a comment (similar to Word). You can use the @ sign followed by the person&rsquo;s name (e.g., @[account-id:60410c6a5290e70069aad89f] ) to pose your comment to someone in particular.  That person will get an email notifying them of your comment. You can also edit, delete or mark your comment as resolved.Reply to a comment on the page. It will be highlighted on the page and you just click on it to see the comment and type a reply.Add a comment about the page in general.  (You don&rsquo;t need the pencil for this).  You can add, edit or delete a comment at the bottom of any page. |
|  |  |  |
| ## Add Yourself as a Watcher to a Page
You can set yourself up to be informed when changes are made on a page using the Watcher feature.### Click on the eye at the top right corner and add yourself as a Watcher to the page.  You&rsquo;ll get email updates when updates are made to the page.
### Note:  The person who created the page is automatically made a Watcher.
### Click Stop watching if you no longer want to be a Watcher on a record. | ## Creating a Table of Contents on A Page
Here is a short video on how to create a table of contents on your page:  https://www.google.com/search?q=confluence+jira+macro&sca_esv=557502889&rlz=1C1GCEA_enUS1019US1019&tbm=vid&ei=KzPdZP_NBdqf5NoPt62K-AM&start=10&sa=N&ved=2ahUKEwi_j5Hwg-KAAxXaD1kFHbeWAj8Q8NMDegQIEBAW&biw=1920&bih=931&dpr=1#fpstate=ive&vld=cid:1703d4c3,vid:uWwo9J4j1hI | ## Creating a Template in Confluence
Here is a 6 minute video that explains how to create a template (i.e., a reusable form) within Confluence. a   https://www.google.com/search?sca_esv=557502889&rlz=1C1GCEA_enUS1019US1019&q=creating+a+template+in+confluence&tbm=vid&source=lnms&sa=X&sqi=2&ved=2ahUKEwiq78O82-GAAxUoE1kFHVJoDuUQ0pQJegQICBAB&biw=1920&bih=931&dpr=1#fpstate=ive&vld=cid:8e47db88,vid:QJFjiM_c_0Q |



# User Story Template

*Created: 2024-03-11T19:40:46.666Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*Last Updated: 2024-03-11T19:43:51.730Z by Martha Haley (Deactivated)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3066822717/User+Story+Template*

---

As a   ________________   Who needs it?
I want   _______________   What action do they need?
So that I can ___________   Why do they need it?
 
Acceptance Criteria (this is where the Product Owner lists any additional requirements that need to be met in order for them to approve the story as done).  This will be used for QA testing.
&middot;         Business Rules:  List or attach any Business Rules that need to be met for this story (e.g., eligibility criteria based on loan status or other loan attributes, or other restrictions that apply).
&middot;         Display Requirements:  Define any requirements for what is expected to be visible on a screen (verbiage, conditional messages, fields, links, etc.). Indicate what is pre-populated and what is for the screen user to populate.
&middot;         Edits:  If applicable, identify any edits for any information the user enters on the screen(s). 
&middot;         New Fields, Codes, or Processes to be created:  identify what new fields, codes or processes are expected to be created for this story.
&middot;         Design Requests:  Although Design is mainly left to the UX Designers, list any specific design requirements such as the order of information presented on a screen, access to the screen from another screen, etc.
&middot;         Sources of information to use:  If applicable list where the Business expects IT to get the information used for completing the action
&middot;         Existing Processes to use:  If applicable list the existing process or processes that the Business expects will be used to complete the requested action.
 
Related Stories: 
&middot;         Epic:  if there is one.
&middot;         Exceptions:  if this is the happy path story
&middot;         Happy Path:  if this story is an exception (unhappy path)
&middot;         Other related stories that are part of an overall process.
Check each user story to verify that it meets the INVEST* set of criteria:
&middot;         Independent &ndash; can be developed in any sequence and changes to one User Story don&rsquo;t affect the others.
&middot;         Negotiable &ndash; it&rsquo;s up for the team to decide how to implement them; there is no rigidly fixed workflow.
&middot;         Valuable &ndash; each User Story delivers a detached unit of value to end users.
&middot;         Estimable &ndash; it&rsquo;s quite easy to guess how much time the development of a User Story will take.
&middot;         Small &ndash; it should go through the whole cycle (designing, coding, testing) during one sprint.
&middot;         Testable &ndash; there should be clear acceptance criteria to check whether a User Story is implemented appropriately.
*Source:  https://xp123.com/articles/invest-in-good-stories-and-smart-tasks/

# RAID Log Entry Criteria

*Created: 2025-02-07T19:44:06.931Z by JIves*
*Last Updated: 2025-02-07T19:45:31.418Z by JIves*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3800006698/RAID+Log+Entry+Criteria*

---

Criteria to determine if a new RAID Log Entry (under the Decision section) is needed.  If you can answer &ldquo;Yes&rdquo; to any of the following questions.
Architecture:
Are there multiple approaches that can be used (i.e. when the decision had to be made for the K12 development teams to use Material or Prime NG for UI)?
Software:
Is a new or additional type of software needed for this project?
Do/Can we build this software in-house, or should it be outsourced?
Do we need to purchase this software or is there a free version we can use?
Does the software used need to be discarded because it did not meet the project&rsquo;s requirements?  
Data Integrity:
Are there any legal requirements around how the data is gathered or stored?
Would a third party/outside entity be needed to validate the data?
Is there a need to convert/transfer data from one database to another?
Criteria to determine if a new RAID Log Entry (under the Risk section) is needed.  If you can answer &ldquo;Yes&rdquo; to any of the following questions.
External:
Could a change in law/compliance stop/change the way we do business or collect data or open new possibilities to do so?
Could an upcoming change/upgrade to currently used software cause a change in how a process/program being developed works?
 
Data Integrity:
Is relevant data being stored outside of the database of the system that is being converted?

# Database Reset Process

*Created: 2025-11-10T19:32:16.474Z by Bill Ewald*
*Last Updated: 2025-11-10T19:33:03.884Z by Bill Ewald*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4446421008/Database+Reset+Process*

---

Development Database Reset
Luke Samuels - This is done. For posterity here was the general process:
1. Rename database via query console with this command:
ALTER DATABASE K12 MODIFY NAME = K12_backup;

Run a terraform plan and verify that it will create a new databse
Run terraform apply to create the database
Manually add the sql_dev_ops user:
1. insert sql statement here

Run the pipeline with the dacpac in it (this is different between environments):
1. https://dev.azure.com/CFI-AzureDevOps/K12/_build?definitionId=32&_a=summary

Add the function app&rsquo;s managed identity as a user and give it the needed permissions (grab its uuid, this is different between environments):
CREATE USER [development-api-enrollment] FROM EXTERNAL PROVIDER
WITH OBJECT_ID = '71587242-04ef-4cce-b2c8-b240e0be01be'
ALTER ROLE db_owner ADD MEMBER [development-api-enrollment]ALTER ROLE db_datareader ADD MEMBER [development-api-enrollment]
ALTER ROLE db_datawriter ADD MEMBER [development-api-enrollment]
GRANT EXECUTE ON SCHEMA::dbo TO [development-api-enrollment]
GO

# K12-2134 & K12-1328 & K12-2353 (My Documents Endpoints) For QA & FE

*Created: 2025-05-01T15:32:26.088Z by Jason Dean (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2025-05-14T15:39:24.567Z by Brandon D'alleva*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/3991371806/K12-2134+K12-1328+K12-2353+My+Documents+Endpoints+For+QA+FE*

---

### URL Structure
/Files/GetFiles/{applicantId}/{container}
Example URL (works in POSTMAN):
https://development-api-enrollment.azure-api.net/Files/GetFiles/101809a2-dd0e-49f5-a99c-5bba893e7e8c/provider# DownloadFile url structure
### /Files/DownloadFile/{Blob Container}/{base64 encoded string}
### DownloadFile sample url

https://development-api-enrollment.azure-api.net/Files/DownloadFile/provider/ZmFjaWxpdHktYWdyZWVtZW50LzEwMTgwOWEyLWRkMGUtNDlmNS1hOTljLTViYmE4OTNlN2U4Yy9Xb3JraW5nLURvY3VtZW50LnR4dD92ZXJzaW9uaWQ9MjAyNS0wNC0yNFQxODo1NTowMy4zOTk3ODI3Wg==Notes:
- Requires SubscriptionID header in GET request.

- May need Content-Disposition header on Angular side for proper file handling.

### Test Scenarios (For Patrick)
File Download Testing:
- Download a blob with metadata.filename and one without metadata.filename.

Metadata Override Testing:
- Use Azure Blob Storage to:
Add metadata.

- Attempt to override status by adding a CustomStatus metadata key.

Versioning Test:
- Add a new version by overwriting a file in blob storage (must use the same name).

Filter by rootPrefix:
/Files/GetFiles/{applicantId}/{container}?rootPrefix=facility-agreement
→ Only pulls files from the facility-agreement folder./Files/GetFiles/{applicantId}/{container}?rootPrefix=individual-agreement
→ Only pulls files from the individual-agreement folder.DownloadFile Validation:
- Confirm it works in POSTMAN (requires subscription key).

- Check if Content-Disposition header is needed if downloads fail.

### Upload file structure
### Files/FileUpload/{Blob Container}/{Applicant Id}/{collection type}
- Requires all of the parameters 
Container will probably be provider

- applicant id as before

- collection type will typically be individual-agreement or facility-agreement

To test in postman, ensure that in the body section of the post request, there is a key named file with a file type, and select the file from your local machine to upload to the storage account and container.
Scenarios:
- Upload a PDF with a base name such as myDocument.pdf
You should see a 200 result, with the attachment id of the response indicating the filename + a datetime stamp (which will be the actual filename in the blob)

- When selecting those file properties in metadata, you should see the original filename, as well as the version and version id, all which use a different combination of datetime stamps and random strings. As an example:

# Local APIM Testing and Setup

*Created: 2025-05-06T14:06:09.341Z by Brad Larsen (Unlicensed)*
*Last Updated: 2025-05-19T21:02:51.710Z by Brad Larsen (Unlicensed)*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4002185226/Local+APIM+Testing+and+Setup*

---

25falsenonelisttrue## Setting up a local APIM instance 
In order to reduce the overhead in testing Azure functions and APIs behind APIM, we can stand up a locally hosted instance of APIM that will sync down changes from Azure.  
Your local instance of APIM is effectively managed in Azure as if you were interacting with a cloud managed instance, and all changes and configuration are managed in the Azure portal.  Once you have have configured a local instance, it stays in regular contact with Azure&rsquo;s APIM instance to ensure that all changes are mirrored to your local instance.  With this, we do not need to fully deploy an application stack to Azure in order to confirm APIM and API behaviors prior to testing.
### Establish an API clone in APIM for local development
In APIM, click the 3-dots next to the API we&rsquo;re aiming to clone, and choose clone.
Modify the settings for your new clone (which should be something like dev-api-enrollment-dashboard (clone) initially).  For the sake of convention, rename (Display Name) to use &ldquo;local&rdquo; instead of &ldquo;dev&rdquo; as the leading portion of the name, and some indicator that this is for a specific developer (&ldquo;blarsen&rdquo;) at the end.  I&rsquo;ve removed the word &ldquo;dashboard&rdquo; as well, since we were using it to differentiate between the other API endpoint only.
The Name property here is actually the &ldquo;Api Id&rdquo; when using the Azure CLI, and we&rsquo;ll use this later when uploading Swagger to APIM using the CLI.
The Web Service URL is your locally running copy of your Azure functions.  Here I&rsquo;ve entered the URL that Visual Studio is using to host the application.  I&rsquo;ve also allowed for both HTTP and HTTPS in this API, as there was some conjecture around problematic certificate validation outside of the Azure environment.
The API URL Suffix needs to be used here.  The original copy has no suffix, but you might notice that they still share the same Base URL.  Adding the suffix ensures that we&rsquo;re speaking to our own unique endpoint, but this won&rsquo;t be directly referenced by any applications.  I believe this is primarily used in the local APIM instance to synchronize changes in this instance.
Ensure that Products is set to Unlimited as the original copy of this endpoint is configured to use.
When we configure our local gateway, it will automatically add to the Gateways section when selecting the default API.  You may return here to confirm.
### Configure a new local gateway and backend instance
While still in APIM, navigate to Deployment + Infrastructure > Self-hosted Gateways on the lefthand menu.  Click &ldquo;+ Add&rdquo; to begin adding a new gateway for your local APIM instance.
Under the Add dialog, you&rsquo;ll configure the name, location, description, and the API you want to associate this gateway with.
After clicking &ldquo;Add&rdquo; at the bottom of this screen, you will return to the list of self-hosted gateways.  Click on your new gateway to view the details.  This will take you to the Overviews section.  On this page, you&rsquo;ll have some basic graphs that will show you traffic details and other metrics once you&rsquo;re interacting locally.  You&rsquo;ll also have a history of communication with your local instance to confirm connectivity.  
From here, we need the deployment details from the Deployments section in order to configure Docker locally.  You can also confirm that this is associated with your API under the APIs section.
Lift the details from the Environment section at the bottom and save to an env.conf file somewhere that you can execute commands from and revisit easily.
Next take the Docker command that they&rsquo;ve given you and execute it from the same directory that your env.conf file resides (or provide the path to it).
If all goes well, you should be able to tail the log using something like:
```
docker logs -f blarsen-local-apim
```
 (Note that my container name matched my gateway name as well here)
If all goes well, you should see that your gateway has established a connection with Azure by confirming back in the overview section of your gateway.  Clicking that status link will show you heartbeat activity as well.
At this point, you should be ready to talk to APIM locally.
### Configure Postman
Earlier, I explicitly added a new API URL Suffix of &ldquo;blarsen&rdquo; in the example from above.  We also defined the backend service URL that we will forward requests to.  In my case, I used the default Visual Studio defined URL and port number but opted to use my IP address rather than &ldquo;localhost&rdquo;, as sometimes there&rsquo;s some nuance in docker networking with resolving localhost.  Per convention, I&rsquo;m opting to add a new environment for my local APIM instance with minimal changes needed to use it.
Assuming that your visual studio instance is running, you should be able to use your local APIM instance through postman in order to forward through to your local Azure functions.
If you&rsquo;re still tailing your logs in Docker (docker logs -f blarsen-local-apim), you should also see the request being logged.  You will also see activity on the self-hosted gateway dashboard in the overviews section (after some delay).
noteBonus points:  (in PR at the moment) In the `\k12-api-enrollment\Automation\Docker` path in your project, we have a Docker Compose file that runs SQL Server and APIM locally.  To make this work, you will need the compose file and env.conf that was created above in the same directory.  From there, you can run:
```
docker-compose up -d
```
Doing so will set up the above stack, which will warrant needing to deploy schema and some initial seeding to your local copy of SQL Server.

Bonus points:  (in PR at the moment) In the `\k12-api-enrollment\Automation\Docker` path in your project, we have a Docker Compose file that runs SQL Server and APIM locally.  To make this work, you will need the compose file and env.conf that was created above in the same directory.  From there, you can run:

docker-compose up -d
Doing so will set up the above stack, which will warrant needing to deploy schema and some initial seeding to your local copy of SQL Server.

## Managing APIM Endpoints
The process for posting Swagger json to APIM (OpenAPI) is the same process you would use to update the Azure managed APIM instance.  The advantage of maintaining a local APIM instance is that you may update your local endpoint and APIM definitions without impacting the managed development instance, and you do not need to wait for a PR and full deployment to confirm behaviors. 
### Post Swagger to new APIM API
Currently, we are handling this process manually but would like to automatically post these updates as part of the deployment.  In the interim, we&rsquo;ve been working on scripts we can run at the command line to try and automate this process.  Our goal for future state would be bolting this functionality onto the deployment pipeline to avoid manual updates to cloud infrastructure.
#### a. Manually update the APIM API spec
1. First, ensure that your Azure functions project is running and navigate to your swagger json endpoint.  For example: http://localhost:7071/api/swagger.json

1. Save this JSON to a local file for editing.  You will need to update the title to match your new local APIM API display name.  Additionally the host and basePath should match what you had provided as the Web Service URL (and how your APIM instance communicates with your locally running Azure Functions project).

2. Remove any paths that aren&rsquo;t going to be managed by your API by editing the paths section in this document.  In the case of our team, we&rsquo;re removing anything prefixed with &ldquo;/enrollment&rdquo; and &ldquo;/history&rdquo; to limit the scope to what we&rsquo;re maintaining.  It was explained that we should be able to begin with the first entry in the &ldquo;paths&rdquo; tree, and highlight all of the way to the first instance of a path that begins with &ldquo;/comms&rdquo;.  Remove everything leading up to &ldquo;/comms&rdquo; (these instructions are specific to Team Cobra Kai, and your team may differ in practices and endpoint requirements).

3. Next we will import the edited JSON document into APIM by using the import feature.

Select OpenAPI as the specification, and browse to your JSON document using the &ldquo;Select a file&rdquo; button on the right.  From here, you may click the &ldquo;Import button&rdquo;.
This should conclude the process of importing updated Swagger content into APIM manually.
#### b. Automated update to the APIM API spec
As a prerequisite, you will need the Azure CLI installed locally in Powershell.
This will install the CLI.  See Microsoft Documentation for more information.
Install the Azure CLI.
```
winget install --exact --id Microsoft.AzureCLI
```
Login using the appropriate tenant Id (as seen below).
```
az login --tenant 3f80a41f-c452-4071-a415-4df4176730e2
```
This will in turn launch a window asking for your Azure credentials (Work or School account typically).  Once you&rsquo;ve entered your credentials, the command prompt will advance to prompt you for the subscription ID, which should just be the number &ldquo;1&rdquo; in the list.
From here, you should be authenticated with Azure 
At the time of this writing, we have a Powershell and Bash script version of this same solution (due to unknown preference, and to support mac/windows/linux).  Both share the same configuration file in order to isolate actual implementation details from the script and maintain configuration centrally.
This project is not currently under our source control, but the hope is to hand this off to a DevOps or deployment team to wire it in as a post deployment step for configuration.
######  APIM - Top level folder for docker environment config and script projects.
######       OAPISwag - Swagger scripts and resources
######   logs - Logging output from script execution
######   OutputJson - The JSON that is saved and edited by the script execution, and will post to APIM
 local-swagger.json
######  config.json - The configuration for either of the scripts below
######  SwagToOpenAPI.ps1 - Powershell version of the script.
######  swagToOpenAPI.sh - Bash version of the script
######  env.conf - The configuration file for APIM in docker as seen in the section at the top here.
######  README.md - Additional write-up to explain process and the configuration
##### Configuration (config.json)
The config.json file provides configuration settings for the swagToOpenAPI.sh (Bash) and SwagToOpenAPI.ps1 (PowerShell) scripts, which process Swagger JSON and import APIs to Azure APIM. It contains general settings and environment-specific configurations. Users must update paths and other settings to match their system and requirements.
json###### File Structure
The JSON file is divided into two main sections: General and Environments.
###### General Settings
The General section contains settings shared across all environments and scripts.
| Property | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OutputPathUnix | String | Path to the output directory for Swagger JSON files on Unix-like systems (e.g., Linux, WSL). Example: /home/user/repo/APIM/OAPISwag/OutputJson |
| LogPathUnix | String | Path to the log file on Unix-like systems. Example: /home/user/repo/APIM/OAPISwag/logs/apim_script.log |
| OutputPathWindows | String | Path to the output directory for Swagger JSON files on Windows. Example: C:\Users\user\repo\APIM\OAPISwag\OutputJson |
| LogPathWindows | String | Path to the log file on Windows. Example: C:\Users\user\repo\APIM\OAPISwag\logs\apim_script.log |
| PathFilters | Array | List of API path prefixes to exclude from the Swagger JSON (e.g., ["/enrollment/", "/history/"]). Paths starting with these prefixes are filtered out. |
Notes:
- Paths: Users must replace placeholder paths (e.g., /home/user/... or C:\Users\user\...) with their actual directory paths. Ensure directories are writable.

- Unix vs. Windows: The Bash script uses OutputPathUnix and LogPathUnix. The PowerShell script uses OutputPathWindows on Windows and OutputPathUnix on Unix-like systems (e.g., WSL, Linux).

- PathFilters: Add or modify prefixes to control which API paths are included or excluded.

###### Environments
The Environments section is a dictionary of environment configurations (e.g., dev, test, poc). Each environment can have unique values for the following properties:
| Property | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SwaggerUrl | String | URL to the Swagger JSON file for the environment. Example: https://development-api-enrollment.azurewebsites.net/api/swagger.json |
| OutputJsonFile | String | Filename for the processed Swagger JSON output. Example: dev-swagger.json |
| ResourceGroup | String | Azure resource group name. Example: development |
| ApimName | String | Azure APIM service name. Example: development-api-enrollment |
| ApiId | String | Unique identifier for the API in Azure APIM. Example: dashboard |
| ApiPath | String | Base path for the API in Azure APIM. Example: / or poc |
| ApiDisplay | String | Display name for the API in Azure APIM. Example: dev-api-enrollment-dashboard |
| ServiceUrl | String | Backend service URL for the API. Example: https://development-api-enrollment.azurewebsites.net/api |
Notes:
- Unique Values: Each environment (e.g., dev, test, poc) can define its own values for these properties. Add new environments by creating additional entries (e.g., "prod": { ... }).

- Required: All properties are required for each environment to ensure the scripts function correctly.

- Customization: Update URLs, IDs, and names to match your Azure setup and API specifications.

##### Usage
Update Paths:
- Set OutputPathUnix and LogPathUnix to your Unix paths (e.g., /home/johndoe/repo/APIM/OAPISwag/...).

- Set OutputPathWindows and LogPathWindows to your Windows paths (e.g., C:\Users\janedoe\repo\APIM\OAPISwag\...).

- Ensure directories exist and are writable.

Configure Environments:
- Modify existing environments (dev, test, poc) or add new ones.

- Ensure each environment has valid URLs, Azure settings, and unique identifiers.

Run Scripts:
Bash:
PowerShell:
Additionally, you may run the script and specify which configuration you want to run in your config.json by answering the prompt.  The remaining details will be defaults.



# Examples/Use cases of custom versioning

*Created: 2025-05-07T19:56:59.428Z by Brandon D'alleva*
*Last Updated: 2025-05-07T20:43:51.974Z by Brandon D'alleva*
*URL: https://cfi-nc.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/KR/pages/4005068856/Examples+Use+cases+of+custom+versioning*

---

# Data Versioning Notes
## Source #1: Lakefs
Lakefs Blog on Data Versioning
### Versioning Approaches Covered:
Full Duplication
- Saves a full copy of data in a new location each time you want it.

- Pros:
Good for small data sets.

- Easy to understand.

Cons:
- Space inefficient.

- Correct date values may have to be manually set.

1. 
Valid_from/to Metadata
- Adding/maintaining two metadata fields in a table: valid_from and valid_to.

- Never overwrite an existing record. Instead, append new ones and update the valid_to field to the current timestamp.

- Works similarly to temporal tables in SQL Server.

- Pros:
Useful for "time traveling" through a collection of data.

Cons:
- Only one method of interacting with versions: adding filters to queries on metadata fields.

- Limited to getting table/file data at specific points in time.

2. 
## Source #2: Blob Snapshots
Azure Blob Snapshots Overview
### Key Points:
- Snapshots are a read-only version of a blob created at a specific point in time.

- Identical to the base blob, except the URI has a DateTime value appended to it:
Example: <http://storagesample.core.blob.windows.net/mydrives/myvhd?snapshot=2011-03-09T01:42:34.9360000Z>

Unlimited snapshots can be created for a blob.
Base blob's metadata is also copied to the snapshot.
Cons:
- Can result in extra storage usage.

- Supported for HNS accounts (currently in preview; requires enrollment).

### How It Could Work:
- Create a snapshot of the blob/file before making changes. If you need to revert, copy the snapshot to the current file.

### Custom Versioning with Naming and Metadata:
- Append a version number or timestamp to the file name:
Example: myfile_v1.txt

Store version identifiers in the metadata of the blob.
Works well with HNS because files can be maintained within organized "directories" (though Azure does not actually use directories).
Potential Use Case:
- Native blob versioning is not supported with HNS, so custom versioning could be a good alternative.

## Source #3: Snyk
Snyk Documentation on Custom Versioning
### Key Points:
- Uses semantic versioning with regular expressions for container images that don't follow standard semantic versioning.
Example: snyk/example:1.2_V3

### For Potential Use with HNS and Blob Storage:
- Naming conventions that embed version information in a pattern:
Examples:
my_document_v1.0.txt

- my_document_v1.1.txt

- reports/annual_report_2024_revA.pdf

- data/sensor_log_20250507_001.csv

Add versioning information (e.g., v1, v2, v3).
Use hierarchical namespaces to organize versioned files into logical directories and subdirectories:
- Example: A folder for each document, with all versions of that document stored within the folder.

Store additional information in blob metadata.
### Example Use Case:
- A file named reports/monthly_sales_report.xlsx could have the following versions:
reports/monthly_sales_report_v1.0.xlsx

- reports/monthly_sales_report_v1.1.xlsx

- reports/monthly_sales_report_v2.0_20250507.xlsx

## Source #4: NASA MISR File Naming and Versioning Conventions
NASA MISR File Naming and Versioning
### Key Points:
- NASA's Multi-angle Imaging SpectroRadiometer (MISR) project embeds version information directly within the filename of their HDF files.

### Before Software Version V2.1.3:
- Version string consisted of two numerals at the end of the filename.

- Example:
MISR_AM1_GRP_TERRAIN_GM_P178_O003830_AN_03.hdf (newer version)

- MISR_AM1_GRP_TERRAIN_GM_P178_O003830_AN_02.hdf (older version)

### Since Software Version V2.1.3:
- Enhanced version string format: Fnn_nnnn (or optionally Tnnn_Fnn_nnnn).
nnnn: Old version number (four numerals).

- Fnn: Format version of the product (incremented when a software delivery results in a product format change).

- Tnnn: Optional field indicating a time range of validity.

### Example:
- MISR_AM1_GRP_TERRAIN_GM_P178_O005900_AN_F02_0007.hdf (newer software)

- MISR_AM1_GRP_TERRAIN_GM_P178_O005900_AN_F02_0006.hdf (older software)

## Other Sources:
1. How Blob Versioning Works (Includes Snapshots)

2. Azure Blob Versioning Q&A

## Working Session Norms:
Confluence page (this page) is our source of truth. At minimum, update Confluence before each working session.
Keep the working group size manageable. Bring in additional people as needed.
## Scope:
Develop and present a Proof of Concept (POC) and Vision for a data virtualization solution for Shannon by 2025/10/7 and SEAA demo 2025/10/21 . Target of 2025/9/23 to complete initial run and gather feedback. This project aims to demonstrate the value of data virtualization by creating specific cross-functional reports, evaluating the approach against alternatives like a data lake, and providing an estimated timeline and cost.
Broader demonstration with/for other CFI stakeholders will take place after October 21.
## Conditions of Satisfaction:

## Vision Statement:
Data virtualization may be a viable option for applying advanced analytics needed for SEAA, more so than current available methods. A proof of concept (POC) looks to demonstrate the ease of creating a virtualization method and using it to perform advanced analytics with a relatively low barrier to entry.
